Citizen’s Fall
Book 1 of the Citizenship Series
by Richard Saar

CHAPTER 1

PROLOGUE

“So, this is it... this is how it all ends,” he thought as he slammed into the dirt, rocks and branches gouging him from every angle. 
His head spun, a nauseating whirl from the fall, he was sprawled legs and arms out wide, eyes squeezed shut against the chaos. He could hear the van’s engine fading into the distance, tires crunching over the gravel road. With trembling hands, he scrambled to rip the hood from his head, gulping air as he blinked into the dim light. 
He seized a final glimpse of the van speeding away, dust settling over the road’s edge where it cut through the jagged spine of the Northwestern Range—or what remained after the Reclamation.
How did it end up like this? All he’d tried to do was the right thing—play by the rules and get ahead just like anyone else—but they’d hounded him until he had nothing left and had no option but to push back.
He had no idea where he was; they’d been driving for hours, or so it felt. Time was a funny thing when you had a hood over your head and your arms and legs are shackled to a van floor, with only the rumble of the tires on the road for company.
He’d been expecting something like this to happen; he just wasn’t sure what it would be. Jail wasn’t going to be an option—he was too well-known now. Infamous would be more accurate, and it would certainly get out that he was in custody. Which would more than likely provide a location for the protest movement to picket against, just like they did at the courthouse during his so-called trial. He hadn’t been at the courthouse personally, but the crowd had caused more than enough disruption and embarrassment for the government to avoid giving his supporters a new focus.
No, they simply couldn’t risk him becoming more famous. They had to take the steam out of the protest movement that had been threatening to build into something that couldn’t be stopped easily.
Was it going to be a bullet in a back room? Was it going to be torture and public humiliation, or something even worse? Not knowing his fate past the next few hours of survival had made the last few months a living hell. If he stopped running and got caught, the consequences were as dire as they could be.
Today, that day had come.
Somehow they’d found him. He wasn’t sure how—was it one of the cameras on every street corner? Was it a dutiful citizen noticing him and reporting it, earning themselves some social cohesion points? It didn’t matter anymore; they’d caught up with him. When it came to it, there was no heroic standoff or fight against the odds. No, it was quiet, efficient, and a total surprise.
He’d been skulking through the rusted sprawl of the Mid-Continental Belt, where factories choked the plains from old Kansas to the Texan fringe, hiding out among the workers for a month.
He’d managed to steal a high-vis uniform from one of the big companies in the area and could walk around without anyone really noticing—he was just another worker. He’d worn a hard hat and safety glasses to make himself less recognisable; not that many people remebered what he looked like anyway.
He’d even been able to blend into the throng of uniformed workers going into the factories with the morning shift. From there, it was easy to get more uniform pieces, have a shower, and even visit the company canteen twice a day. It was, he thought, the perfect deception—become just another body in a nameless crowd.
He was feeling almost comfortable for the first time in months when the van screeched to a halt next to him as he walked to his bolt hole in an abandoned office building for the night. The door to the van slid open quickly and violently. Two sets of hands snatched him off his feet and bundled him backward inside, while another set thrust a hood over his head. There was no struggle—the arms attached to those hands were far too strong and practiced. It took no more than five seconds, and he was off the street as the van sped away.
Almost immediately, a female voice began addressing him coldly. He tried to calm himself down so he could hear the words she was saying instead of the blood pounding in his ears.
“Mr. Dalton, you have been convicted, in absentia, of crimes against the state that include, but are not limited to, seditious libel and seditious conspiracy. For these and other crimes, you have had your citizenship revoked. You no longer have any rights. This decision cannot be appealed and is permanent—not even an act of government can reverse it.”
The voice softened slightly. “Mr. Dalton, this means you can no longer exist in our society. You are not welcome anywhere under the jurisdiction of the Bi-Continental Government of the Americas. To be found inside the limits of our borders will result in immediate and summary execution.”
With finality, the voice said, “Mr. Dalton, by the power invested in me as an authorised person, I hereby consider this judgment to have been delivered. Driver, take him away.”
With that the van jerked to a halt and a door slid open. Someone got out—he guessed it was the owner of the female voice.
#
The hours since being snatched off the street had raced by in a blur of monotony punctuated by pain, maybe six or seven by his best guess.
He had tried counting the time in seconds and minutes, but when the van left the road and started on what felt like a goat track, he lost focus. It was hard to keep a regular cadence when every bump smashed like a punch to the kidneys as his spine compressed against the steel bench he was restrained to. His shoulders were stretched tight, his hands forced painfully behind him in cuffs.
The guards—or whatever they were—hadn’t uttered a word since grabbing him off the street and bundling him into the van.
Every time he asked even a simple question, like who they were or where they were taking him, the answer had been a sharp poke in the ribs with one of their batons—or, like the last time, a swift crack across the head that left him seeing stars.
Admittedly, asking them if they were okay and whether they needed to stop for a break might have been provocative.
Eventually, they stopped, but he didn’t have more than a few seconds to enjoy the blissful lack of movement. No sooner had they halted than he was unlocked from the floor and dragged out of the van.
The shackles came off his ankles and legs; the hood stayed on.
The two guards took an arm each, half-lifting, half-marching him to what felt like the side of a road—then unceremoniously shoved him.
That was when he realized he wasn’t on flat ground. Where he expected to hit solid earth, there wasn’t any. 
He struck a steep slope and cartwheeled—one, two, three, four times—arms and legs flailing. Crashing into the foliage, gravity won.
The spinning in his head slowly wound down and In the quiet aftermath of his violent exit from the van, he gauged his situation
He’d been shoved off the side of the road down a steep embankment and had ended up at the edge of a forest, buried in some bushes.
He gauged his condition: What hurt? Did he have any serious injuries?
No, he didn’t think so. Apart from the pain of bouncing down the slope and being winded somewhere after the second or third barrel roll, he was relatively unscathed.
However, he had nothing on him. He was dressed in the same stolen work clothes he’d been wearing when they dragged him off the street—at least it was relatively new gear, stolen from the laundry a few days ago—but they had taken his boots. 
That was about the extent of his inventory: no phone to call family (not that anyone would answer anyway), no wallet, money, or ID, no food, water, not even a jacket.
He gingerly stood up, making sure his initial assessment of his lack of injuries was accurate. Yes, all good so far—though he was pretty sure he’d hurt more later.
The forest behind him was black and impenetrable, swallowing the fading light of the day. Snow gleamed on the peaks hemming the valley—somewhere deep in the Cascades or Rockies, far from the Belt’s smog. They’d hauled him north, alright, into the wild, lawless territory that no country claimed or controlled.
The van was long gone, the dust already settling from its speedy exit. The noise of it driving away was being replaced by an oppressive silence. Having lived in the city all his life, he didn’t like the silence too much, and the silence here was far too loud.
It didn’t seem like it had snowed down here recently. Thankfully, he was a few hundred meters below the snow line, but the sun would be going down soon, and the cold was already biting.









CHAPTER 2

UNRESTRICTED CITIZEN (JIM 1)

Citizenship Act: Revised 2045 
Section 4, Subsection 3 – Citizenship Status
2.1 Citizenship Status Level 7 (Unrestricted Citizen - Permanent) – A citizen that has accrued more than 1500, but less than 1999, citizenship points will be awarded this status.
2.2 Level 7 status allows full and unrestricted access to all Governmental positions. 
2.3 A level 7 citizen will gain priority queueing for all government programs over all citizens level 6 and below.
2.4 A Level 7 citizen’s yearly allowance will be 75,000 fully unrestricted credits; this allowance is paid over and above any private income earned. 
2.5 Credits will expire after 12 months if not used.
2.6 Citizenship points will accrue at 1.25x the normal rate whilst a citizen holds this status. 
#
The sleek, dark grey, late-model EV rolls nearly silent through the city streets. Jim sits relaxed at the wheel, not even glancing where the car takes him.
The car is seamlessly cutting through the traffic, the streets are buzzing with activity, inside the cocoon of the real leather lined car the noise and activity outside doesn’t penetrate enough to disturb Jim from his thoughts, the street nosies pleasantly muffled by the active noise cancelling system.
The car glides past perfectly clean, sleek marble and glass buildings cut through by immaculately manicured gardens of millimetre perfect hedges and impossibly bright flora displays. the dusk sun glinting bronze gold off the mirrored facades of the high rise buildings lined the billiard smooth street. 
Certainly none of the Outer’s drab grey oppressive utilitarian suburbs and far from the Belt’s rust-choked plains, this was the glittering core. Jim has worked hard for years to have left that world behind.
The car alerts and takes a fast and violent swerve, jolting Jim and his wife out of their collective musing, Jim’s wife, sitting next to him, squeals loudly as she loses hold of the tablet she’d been using to search for holiday destinations.
Jim, takes hold of the wheel reflexively, not that it helps, the car has better reactions times then he could ever have. He sees the problem, a work truck lurched off the curb without looking and caused all the around to evade it.
“Damn manual drivers, probability some probationer” Jim mutters using the derogatory term for those who are stuck in menial work. “ Why can’t they just go auto drive like everyone else”
The truck pulls away down a side street, it was a sanitary systems truck.
“At least we can’t smell it” says Jim as he watches is disappear into another building.
Helen picks up the tablet checking it’s ok.
“Jim, be careful, language!” she snaps sharply. “You don’t want anymore speech warnings, not with all the work we’ve done to get to here” Helen gestures around the opulent car interior not looking up from the tablet.
Jim shrugs, this is old territory.
Noticing the streetscape, Sarah says, “This really is the big end of town—just look at all the money here” her excitement building for the evening ahead “I’ve been dying to seeing Shane and Helen’s new place for months, I’m still dissapointed we didn’t get an invite to the house warming”
“Yep, you can smell the status here can’t you, this is where you’d want to be” Jim says just a bit too lightly ignoring the dig that he couldn’t secure the invite.
They both know this is exactly where they want to be, meeting up with Shane at his new place is a big deal for thier plans to climb the ladder, a BBQ with someone like Shane can make things move in the right direction and fast.
“Are you and Helen still talking about the Europe trip?” Jim asks.
“Yes, Helen’s keen on Italy, I’d like France, but it all depends on what kind status we can get with Shane being a Level 9 now… of course Helen didn’t say if he was there or not, she’s keeping that one quiet, but everyone’s got to know right? I mean she was talking about first class ticket for us all, a level 8 can’t afford do that for four people.
“We can barely afford economy right now” Jims says, his mood shifts slightly as his reality as a Level 7 citizen creeps in. 
Shaking the mood off Jim, gets excited again ”a free First class holiday to Europe with a Level 9, guess what, we go wherever Helen wants to go!”
The car softly chimes they have arrived with thier destination, the screen on the console reads: Level 8/9 restricted building, temporary level 7 guest access granted.
Jim raises his eyebrow at Helen as they read the message at the same time “Here we go”
Helen chuckles excitedly, this is going to be an important night for both of them.
#
Shane and his wife Helen greeted them on the 47th floor in the small lift 47th floor lift foyer. The elevator ride was so smooth and quiet you wouldn’t have known it had even moved, it only had one button “penthouse”.
They were both in their mid thirties and exuding that kind healthy, fit and tanned confidence that people from the successful upper managerial classes can pull off with ease. Their clothes fit just about perfectly, their teeth white and straight, their hair expensively groomed, their figures slim and athletic.
Shane was a good two inches taller than Jim’s already impressive six feet and Helen was much shorter. Jim always wondered if they had enhanced thier heights at some point, but you normally ended up with oddly long legs, which wasn’t the case here as with everything else Shane and Helen were in perfect proportion.
Shane embraced Jim in a genuinely warm hug, like long-lost brothers.
“Jimmy! So good to see you, it’s been too long brother, so glad you could finally make it!
Helen similarly embraced Sarah “Sarah! have you been brushing up on your Italian? Benvenuto, entra e rilassati!” 
Helen waved her through the antique wood and iron double entrance doors to the apartment.
Shane followed the with his arm over Jim’s shoulder “I’ve got a meat feast for you tonight my friend!”
Jim could smell it, hints of real smoke, charred meats and tangy sauces, had his tastebuds working overtime already.
The apartment was spectacular, double height floor to ceiling windows on three sides overlooking one of the best views of the city skyline that Jim had ever seen. 
The city positively glowed with the last rays of the setting sun bouncing around between the glass buildings creating a kaleidoscope of reflections.
Shane strode through the apartment to the balcony, Jim trailing in his wake—an oasis 47 floors up. Potted palms swayed in the evening breeze, a strip of impossibly green grass stretching from the balustrade to the BBQ. There, real flames danced over the meat: sausages hissed, ribs blackened, and a rare beef slab oozed juices.
“You weren’t lying about the meat” says Jim incredulously
“Only the best for you Jimmy, I’ve been out here slaving all day to get this ready, come on lets help the girls get the table set-up”
#
Jim and Shane are lounging in the living room, basking in the aftermath of the BBQ, watching the football game on Shane’s brand new 16k 200 inch TV.
The view from the lounge is no less impressive as the neon lights of the city below them waxes and wanes with huge full colour screens advertising everything from the next new phone or car, to the healthiest soft drink ever made to the latest new housing development. 
Building lights twinkle all around them as others go on about their lives, but here up in the Shane’s penthouse, it all seems so calm with the rest of the world so far beneath them.
The doors to the garden are open and the soft rumble of city sounds drift in with the cooling breeze, the occasion blaring horn punctuation the relaxing hum.
Shane ask Jim “Did you want that last sausage? I think that one is a real beef one” and points to the plate on the low marble table in front of them.
Jim pats him stomach “Nah… you go for it, I’m stuffed, I haven’t had that much real meat for ages, I’ll be digesting this for weeks!”
Shane chuckles “Tell me about it, I blew through a couple of months worth of meat points for this” he picks up the lone sausage and bites off a big chunk, with a full mouth “but it was so worth it”
Jim smiles as we watches Shane eat that last sausage, satisfied that even though he gave up some real meat, there was no way he was fitting anything more in. 
Shane casts his mind back to his childhood and clearly remembers his parents doing the Sunday BBQ with real meat when he was a young kid, that’s got to be thirty years ago now.
The limits to meat consumption were small at first, just a restriction of how much you could buy at one time to stop so-called shortages and allow everyone the equal chance to buy meat after rolling global supply issues took most meats off the shelves one too many times. 
Over decades the system evolved to an all inclusive app that accrues what people now call “meat points” or more accurately called the “Government Animal Based Protein Allowance”. 
The system is very highly regulated, each citizen has a certain number of points added to thier allowance each month, the amount of points you get is based on your citizenship level. Jim can afford real meat once or twice every month on his Level 7 allowance.
Points can be used at any government regulated butcher to purchase real meat. If you run out, you just have to wait for the month to click over to reset your points. For most people it’s not an issue as plant based proteins are essentially limitless and free from the government and mostly approximate the taste of real animal based meat. 
In fact more people just eat the plant based without ever dipping into thier meat points.
“You can’t bank meat points anymore, I don’t want to know how you were able to buy so much all at once, my butcher is strictly by the book and won’t go one gram over the monthly allowance”
Shane is licking his fingers for that last taste “You’ve got to shop around, no point just sticking to the one place, there’s always someone will help you out if you help them out.”
“I don’t know man, what if you get caught?”
“Lets put it this way, we’re not sitting here on the 47th floor overlooking downtown if I didn’t ask some questions and bend a few rules” 
Jim looks at Shane, his smile is surprisingly relaxed for someone who just admitted to his cheating the system, a criminal act. 
“I don’t want to know” Jim unconvincingly says as he looks out the window “but I will tell you it is nice view”
Just as Jim is contemplating how you could even bend the rules, his phone gently pings that he has a message from his health app.
[Calorie Alert – you have consumed well above the recommended daily intake of protein and fats. You will need to adjust your diet and exercise regime for the rest of the week. A new menu and work-out program has been uploaded for you. Remember your health is your responsibility]
“Damn it, now I’ll be on oatmeal for the rest of the week” says Jim exasperated.
He notices, Shane’s phone doesn’t ping.
“Hey, how did you avoid a calorie alert?
Shane leans in conspiratorially with a wry smile “Can’t exceed your limit if the system doesn’t know what you’ve eaten”
Jim is genuinely shocked, over allowance meat purchase and now no calorie alert and now telling him for the second time that he’s breaking the system.
“Shane that’s dangerous territory, even for you”
Shane glances over to Helen and Sarah, who not being interested in either the football or eating the last of the meat, are in the dining room pouring over some travel websites on their tablets planning the trip to Italy.
“Don’t worry Jim, we’re safe in here, like I said, if they don’t know what you’re doing they can’t ping you.”
Shane turns back to Jim and points at the 200inch 16k wall panel, the game is playing out on with the sound turned mostly down. It was so good that Jim felt like he was quite literally standing on the sidelines himself.
“I didn’t get the meat, this beast of a TV, or even the apartment by following the rules like everyone else Jim, real life isn’t about waiting in the queue. I keep telling you follow me and the sky’s the limit my friend”
The TV was just one example, of many, how the higher your status was, the more you got. It was one of the next generation 16k TV’s that will eventually be given free to anyone who has enough citizen status points to qualify for the program. 
Much like the previous generations of TVs, this next generation unit was a partnership with the TV manufacturers and the Government to ostensibly get old and energy inefficient TVs out of peoples homes, reducing load on the grid and at the same time ensuring the old units are recycled properly.
No one thought too much about it these days, these tech-government trade-in partnerships started decades ago and are now available on almost anything.
Most people were walking around with the latest free phones, watching TV on their free TV, banking on free tablets pre-loaded with unhackable banking apps, cooling their food in free fridges, driving in free cars… the level of quality and how early you got them after release all dependent on where you sat on the citizenship ladder.
The alliance between tech and government was widely credited with raising the standard of living for the average person who no longer needed to save up for tech or appliances, just sign up to the government program and you get most of the tech at no cost.
That Shane has a this huge top of the line model months before the program will be officially rolled-out says a lot about just how high Shane’s status must be now. 
Not that you could come out and ask, that would be a step too far, most people kept thier points and status quiet, it was after all a competitive market, but you could work out roughly where someone was pretty quickly.
Shane can see Jim’s mind turning over “I can see those wheels turning Jimmy, go on ask what you want to ask, it’s ok”
“Aren’t you worried about losing all of this? “ Jim waves his hand around at the apartment. “I can’t take any risks, Sarah is on a mission to get us up a level and I can’t afford to step out of line anywhere.”
Citizenship status is possibly the most important measure of success in society these days, ever since all the various credit ratings, drivers licence points, health status and a dozen other metrics, including what your phone, TV, fridge and any connected appliance was observing, were merged into the one overarching Citizenship Status.
Jim had made it his mission to climb the ladder as high as he could go. Being a Level 7 sitting on the lounge in Shanes 47th floor two level penthouse couldn’t be further from his upbringings in the Outer. 
The Outer was the slang term for the oppressive grey suburban sprawl where the lowers levels, mostly 3’s and 4’s lived out thier lives working, never ever getting ahead. Whether is was lack of drive or skill only a few escaped, you could do it and Jim had succeeded by working harder than everyone else, every day.
Cultivating friends like Shane was key to his ambitions to make it all the way to the top. Not that Shane wasn’t genuinely a friend, but it didn’t escape him that hanging out with someone of his status could be benefit to his own status. Sarah seemed to think so and was very pleased for Jim to foster this friendship.
Shane now leans forward a bit more until his face is getting almost too close to Jim, his smile and relaxed demeanour hardens slightly and there’s a steely glint in his eye. This was what Shane had been waiting for.
“That wasn’t what you want to ask was it? Go on ask, I dont mind”
Jim feeling the pressure of Shane’s charisma asks “Are you level 9? is this how you get all this stuff, the apartment, the meat, all of it?
Shane looks around again to their wives to see if the are looking and says to Jim “Sure I’m a level 9, I have been for ages, but I didn’t get there and all the extra perks by following the rules, watch this”
He goes to the screen menu on the TV and it shows a bunch of channels that Jim hasn’t seen before, some he knows about, but they are restricted so he’s confused as to why they are now in the menu.
Shane see’s Jims confusion and says quietly, so thier wives can’t overhear “I know a guy can de-restrict the TV, now I can see anything on here and the best part is it’s hidden from the network, they can’t ping me for watching things I’m not supposed to see” Shane gives a derisive snort “Assholes” he growls.
Jim is shocked at how aggressive Shane is, and a little bit excited, “For real, like fully restricted… I call BS on that, they hard-wire the restrictions into the new units nowadays.
Shane responds “I know, you can get some software cheat codes, I’ve tried them before, but they don’t do anything and the TV just sends a signal back to the network that the software is broken and the next morning the software is reverted back to normal”
Jim ask “Doesn’t that ping your score?
“No, the code just looks like a loading error or some random memory issue so they can’t actually prove anything, like I said it’s doesn’t do anything anyway”
He continues “This is way better, my guy installed a hardware bypass chip on the the circuit board that tricks the TV into thinking nothing in the software has changed, but allows it to recieve any signal or feed, kind of like the old school VPNs used to do before they shut them down, only way someone would find out is they open the TV up, I’ve just got to remember to take it out if I ever upcycle to a new unit again”
Jim is incredulous “Fuck man that’s dicey as hell, if you get caught they’ll clip you hundreds of points”
“Technically it would be 500 points and a year’s probation, but don’t worry about it, no one will ever know, the chip is cutting edge”
“Does Helen know?
“You kidding me, no fucking way man, she’ll divorce me for sure!” Shane says not entirely joking. “You know how she’s all about that top tier credit”
“Don’t I know it… Sarah is the same” 
“So lets not tell them then… do you want me to hook you up on the chip? I can have my guy hit up your place anytime and Sarah will never notice”
I can’t risk it, Sarah wants that first class experience you can only get from being a Level 8”
 “Level 8? Really?” Shane seems impressed by Jim’s ambition “That’s a lot of work to get there, are you close?” he asks.
“Not far actually, why do you think I get dragged to all those social courses… bane of my life, I’d rather be anywhere else”
“Love the ambition Jim, lets just say I can help you on your journey, but I’ve got to know you and me are tight. Let my guy come over and fix your TV as my gift to you, on the quiet of course. Don’t hurt my feelings and refuse me now”
Jim’s mind is spinning, Shane will help him
The chose was easy, for Jim “Sure Shane, lets do it”
Shane lets out a breath dramatically “I knew you where ready for the next step Jim!
He calls over to Helen “Hey Hon, any more bubbly left, it feels like the time for another toast!”

#
Jim and Sarah are back in the leather filled cocoon of their later model EV, sliding through the sparse late traffic almost without a sound, outside the city is sleeping and the car is doing it’s best not to wake it.
Inside the car it’s the polar opposite, the excitement is palpable, both Sarah and Jim have exciting news and are bursting to get it out.
Sarah is talking fast “Jim, we were looking at the most amazing villas in Italy,pools, wine cellars, personal chefs, Helen was telling me we should go all out and take three or even four weeks, she was pretty sure Shane could swing it, do you think we can, Jim like really get four weeks?
Jim answers, half-distracted, ‘Four weeks? That’s a stretch…”
Sarah Cuts him off “If they are willing to spend four week with us, just imagine what else they’ll do with us” 
Sarah goes silent pondering the future possibilities.
Jim not noticing says quietly, almost to himself “Shane said he’s take me under his wing I think, he said he help me climb the ladder if I stuck with him”
No reaction from Sarah,
“Did you hear what I said, Shane’s going to help me get to the next level”
Sarah snaps out of her daydream “What? did you just say Shane was going to get you bumped up?”
“Yes, that’s exactly what I said, let me quote “I can help you on your journey, but I’ve got to know you and me are tight”… now that sounds a lot like we’re on the way!”
“Oh My God!” Sarah beams.







CHAPTER 3

FULL CITIZEN (JIM 2)

Citizenship Act (revised 2045) 
Section 4, Subsection 3 – Citizenship Status
3.1 Citizenship Status Level 8 (Full Citizen) – A citizen that has accrued more than 2500, but less than 5000, citizenship points will be awarded level 8 citizenship status. 
3.2 Such status will allow full and unrestricted access to all Governmental positions. 
3.3 A level 8 citizen will gain priority queueing for all government programs over all citizens level 7 and below. 
3.4 Furthermore, citizenship points will accrue at 1.25x the normal rate whilst a citizen hold this status. 
3.5 A level 8 citizen yearly credit allowance will be 150,000 fully unrestricted credits, this amount will be over and above any private income earned. Credits will expire after 12 months if not used.

#
It’s a grey and dreary Monday morning as Jim starts his forty-five-minute commute. It’s early, just after six, as he exits his comfortable but nondescript apartment building on the edge of what would be considered the affluent area of the city, heading toward the glittering lights of the city centre. His car is in self-driving mode and slipping through the wet peak-hour traffic with ease, so he can relax, sip his coffee, and watch the morning news on his console. It’s fast become part of his routine since he earned the credits to upgrade to full self-driving.
This morning, though, he’s ignoring the news. Instead, Jim is sitting back, his eyes closed, savouring the smell of the coffee and musing about the turn his life has taken in the last few weeks. It’s been a roller-coaster ride since his BBQ with Shane and Helen—everything he’s been working toward for years is finally coming to pass. Shane’s guy set him up with the unrestricted TV chip while Helen and Sarah were at work, just days later. Jim is already hooked on the stream of information he’s never seen before—as Shane put it, he’s seeing political views and news that lower-level citizens just aren’t allowed to see, information that will help get Jim ahead.
He looks around the inside of the car. He’s been pretty proud of it since he got it a few years ago—its sleek, dark-grey exterior reflects perfectly that he’s a man on the rise, someone to be noticed. Today, though, it feels old and small. He can’t wait to get rid of it.
His quiet reflection is interrupted by a muffled ping from his phone. “No,” Jim groans to himself. “I’m just not ready for work yet—give me ten more minutes.” He ignores the ping, and as phones do, thirty seconds later it pings again to remind him he has a message. Reluctantly, Jim grabs his phone from his jacket pocket, cautiously opens his eyes, and looks at the message. It’s not from work—it’s from the Citizenship Department, probably just another rule change like all the others they send out. He opens the message:
“Dear Jim Roberts, your status points have been updated. Revisions your the Citizenship Points account have recalculated the value of a number of lead-in points generators. You now have 2512 citizenship points. You have passed the threshold of 2500 citizenship points to be elevated to: Level 8 Full Citizen. Congratulations, your central account and all benefits will be upgraded to reflect your new status. To view your new benefits, log in to your portal.”
Jim sits in stunned silence as his car continues sliding through the wet morning road and navigates the route to his office. He’s numb—this is what he’s been working toward for years. “Level 8!” he says out loud to the empty car. It doesn’t respond. He had faith he’d get there, but he never thought it’d be so soon. His phone starts ringing—it’s Sarah, and she’s excited.
“Jim, did you get a message from the Department? I just did—they revised my points upward. I’m now on 2371 points. I logged into my portal, and it says I’m getting a points bump for being married to a Full Citizen.” Sarah is rapid-fire now. “Did you get a message? Did your points get updated?”
Jim interrupts quickly. “Yes… my points got updated too.” He pauses dramatically. “And you’re now talking to a Full Citizen! Can you believe that?”
“Oh my God, this is amazing! Did Shane do this?”
“Maybe. He said his lawyer would look over my accounts and see if I was missing out. I’m still in the car—I’ll log in to my portal when I get to work and see what’s changed.”
“You know what that means, don’t you?” Sarah says, her voice brimming with excitement about the prospect. “It means our trip to Europe just got upgraded from business to first class—we might even be able to afford to add an extra week! Do you know when the new credits will hit our account?”
“I’m not sure, but let’s not spend the credits until we’ve got them. Let me check on all of that when I hit the office—I’m fifteen minutes away now.”
“Okay, okay, just call me back and let me know… Oh, I’ve got to call Helen and let her know our plans have changed!”
Jim chuckles. “Tell Helen she’s lucky to be going on holidays with us.”
“Ha ha, I will do no such thing! I’ve got to go and call her.”
“Okay, bye,” Jim says, but the call has already ended.
Immediately, Jim calls Shane.
“Hi, Jimmy boy,” Shane answers without the phone even ringing. “It’s a bit early for a social call. What’s going on?”
“Did your lawyer look at my accounts?”
Shane chuckles. “You know, I think he might have mentioned it the other day—something about finding a bunch of points that got missed somewhere. Why?” He knows exactly what’s going on.
“I just got bumped to Level 8—that’s what! Did you do this?”
“I can’t take credit, Jimmy. Maybe my lawyer gave you a little push, but it’s all yours now. Congratulations and welcome to a new life! Let’s do dinner soon, okay? Got to go now—chat later.” The connection cuts, leaving Jim to sit in shock, not knowing what to do next.
#
A few minutes later his car pulls into his spot in the underground carpark at his office. Jim was lucky to get the spot, but that was when he started, he’ll get a better spot now, maybe a level up or closer to the lift.
I’d better not be too full of myself Jim thinks, no one will know yet anyway, your Citizenship Points are not public knowledge, but your citizenship status does have a habit of getting out pretty quickly. 
Most companies HR department have to know what your status is so they can allocate salaries and benefits accordingly, and just like most offices, its get noticed when you get extra benefits and gossip is alway faster than official communications.
Jim jumps into the lift at the carpark, it’s empty at this time of the morning, just how he likes it. Normally he likes to just get into his office and set up his day before too many people start distracting him, today it was entirely different, he needed the early morning quiet time to process what being a Full Citizen meant for him now.
He pressed the button for the his floor, the 57th floor, one of fifteen floors occupied by the Landis Corporation, the commercial risk and consulting firm he works for as a senior mid-level management consultant. 
He been there almost three years now, the company had poached from his old job just after he achieved level 7 status. His old company, a much smaller firm couldn’t really handle the salary he could command once he’d been upgraded, so it was always going to happen, in fact it was all part of Jims plan to climb the ladder.
That how it was in the upper levels of the corporate world, what kind of status you could bring to the company was more important than your skills or education. Which was actually fine by most people as you could climb your way up the pretty far up the ladder without the backing of family money or connections. 
It was universally considered to be a very fair system, you got as far as your actual worth as a citizen, there were no rich freeloaders, or not that many at least.
Steeping out on the 57th floor Jim made his way over to his office past the empty reception, empty boardrooms and past the staff cafeteria, where the cafe staff were just firing up the coffee machine and Jim could see them putting out fresh pastries ready for the early rush, he’d have to come back soon to make sure he got the pick of the plates.
His office was the top end of what his position could get, his work was always exemplary and he’d earned on on the largest offices of the 57th floor. It was tastefully decorated with the typical corporate style that seems to promise luxury and class but never really delivers it. It was big enough for a good sized dark wood veneered desk and matching bookshelf that was packed with neatly arranged and labelled client folders. Across from the desk he had a two seater couch upholstered in uninspiring blue-grey fabric, it wasn’t particularly comfortable, but had been good enough for the occasional late night nap.
It wasn’t until around 7:30 when he was actually at his desk setting up his day enjoying the solitude. He wasn’t expecting an email this early he was surprised by a new meeting request from the People and Culture department at 9am, Jim always had trouble adjusting to the name, it was always HR in his head.
Knowing he had his big client pitch meeting at 9:30am he declined and suggested a new time pushing HR back to 11:30am after the pitch meeting was over.
Almost immediately he received an email from Rosalind the head of the P&C department, it was short and to the point.
“Hi Jim,
I understand you declined the meeting as you had the Industrial Composites pitch meeting this morning. 
Don’t worry about that, John will take over the pitch for today.
See you at 9am!
Regards,
Roslind
Director - People and Culture”
Had his big news hit the office already he wondered, he thought it might take a few days at least. Last time he upgraded his level it had taken a week for his old company to tell him they couldn’t keep him on thier books, it had been nice working for with him and they’d let him stay until he accepted a better offer at a bigger firm.
The Landis Coporation was the top of the game, so it wasn’t going to be that, he was actually getting quite excited about where this was going.
#
It was only a few hours later that he got a text from an unknown number.
The message was perfectly timed to hit his phone just as he was walking out of the meeting with HR. 
He was right to be excited about his future, his newly minted Level 8 status had hit the office already and Landis had offered him a promotion on the spot, moving up to the 50th floor with a nice big pay bump almost doubling his salary. Jim knew how to play the game, if this was anything like jumping to Level 7, he knew he’d have other options, so he told them he’d consider it.
He read the message with growing anticipation.
Hi Jim, Rob here from Nova Corporation, Shane gave me your number. I heard you have some good news, lets meet up for lunch, say 12:30 at Apex? My shout, see you there!
Apex! Only one of the most exclusive restaurants in the district, strictly top tier status. He’d often daydreamed about saving up enough credits and taking Sarah to dinner there one day, but Level 7’s struggled to get bookings. 
He had to stop thinking like that now, he was in the top tiers now.
Jim couldn’t recall hearing of Nova Copoaration, and he’d made it a point to know all of the most prestigious companies, but if Shane had set it up it must be important.
Apex was in a reclaimed old brick warehouse down by the old industrial centre of the city, an architectural voice from the past when this was the industrial heartland of the new government after the Reclamation.
The building exuded a mix of old school bricks and mortar charm, interspersed with flashes of reflective glass and steel that showcased the money and effort it took to restore it and not just knock it down like the rest.
Jim had arrived precisely on-time in the cab Rob had ordered him and was immediately ushered inside by the wait staff, they knew who he was and who he was meeting. For someone who grew up in the Outer, this about as far as one could get from his old life there.
Rob was already sitting at the table, a dark burgundy leather lined booth, one of a number of booths along the old exposed brick walls. He was sipping a red wine whilst reading through some papers he had laid on table in front of him.
He saw Jim and stood to his full six foot three height and offered a very firm two handed handshake “Jim, thanks for coming!”
Jim extricated his hand from Rob’s, it hurt from the pressure of the handshake, sat opposite Rob “Nice to meet you Rob, thanks for the invite… you know I’ve never been here before”
Rob looked around “Oh this old place, it’s nice but there many I think are nicer, it was just close to you”
Jim’s head spun, he thought Apex was the top of the heap.
“So, Nova Corp, I must admit I haven’t heard of your company” Jim inquires.
“No, not many would have, we tend to keep a low profile, but let’s no get too deep into business, let’s eat” Rob points at Jim’s glass that has just been filled with the Red wine “Taste the wine, I’m sure you’ll enjoy it!”
The meal is all that Jim would have expected, not a flavour out of place, perfectly balanced and immaculately presented, and paired with three different types of wine, his head was light and giddy from both the meal and his newly minted status.
Rob, pushing back his empty plate with a satisfied smile on his face, looked across at Jim who had been eyeing his watch for the last twenty minutes. “Jim, do you need to be somewhere” he asks mildly amused.
Jim cautiously answers “Rob, the lunch has been great but we’ve been here for well over an hour now, at some point I do need to get back to work” Jim hopes this pushes the conversation along, which up until this point had consisted entirely about the meal and the wine.
Rob smiles and shakes his head “Jim, you’re not needed back at work, I’m not sure you understand that now you’re a Level 8, the 6’s and 7’s do all the work for you. You and I take as long as we want… welcome to your new life!”
“Ok…” Jim paused and the realisation of what Rob had just said hit home for the first time in visceral sense. 
He was not working for the bosses, he was one of the bosses and the rule had changed for him.
Rob could see his words hit home and pushed “Jim, you’ll get used to it soon enough, I still remember when I got the nod, years ago now, it took a while to adjust, for now sit back and enjoy it”
With that Rob raised his glass to Jim and they silently toasted as Jim decided to do just that, sit back and enjoy his success.
Rob looked around for a waiter to get another bottle of wine “Where are these wait staff, you know customer service has gone backwards so much, I mean these damn level 5 and 6’s sitting on thier universal income and housing doing absolutely nothing to better themselves, can’t even get motivated to being good wait staff” 
Rob on a roll spat our “Just between us, you know what we call them? Troffs, just like little piggies, snouts in the government trough doing nothing.”
Jim was shocked to hear the word said out l;oud, of course he‘d heard it but you’d never say it, but whether it was the wine or the company, he laughed and went along “Troffs, I like it”
Rob nodded “Level 8’s don’t mince words Jim… “ he paused as a waiter filled up his glass and left the bottle for them 
“Now, lets get to business.” Rob said switching to serious mode, gone was the good humour of just seconds ago.
“Nova is offering you the role of VP of Private Client Business, it’s what you’re doing now, just for a select few private clients of ours. Don’t worry, you’ll have a team of 6 and 7’s to do the grunt work, VP’s are there to deal directly with the clients. We’re talking 9 and 10’s Jim, top of the top.
Jim was stuck immovable in his seat, this was more than he’d ever dreamed of, just imagine the connections he could make mixing with the top level elites.
Rob shuffled through the papers he’d put away whilst they were eating, finding the page the wanted.
“We know what you’re on at Landis, and a little birdie told me about the offer they just made. It’s good Jim, don’t misunderstand me, but that’s no place for go-getters like you and me”
Jim still riveted to his seat found his voice “Rob, Landis has been great to me and if you really know what the offer is, you’ll know it’s going to be hard to say no, loyalty is important to me”
Rob smiled wryly “Shane thought you might say that, so we’re prepared to offer you three times the Level 8 allowance in fully private credits, you do what you want with them, no restrictions on what you can use them for… how does that sound?
Jim was stunned, he’d never had private credits before, did he say triple the Level 8 allowance? He really need to keep it cool here, this was the big end of town.
Jim, after pause said a little bit too coolly “Rob, that is certainty some offer you just made, and I really appreciate it. I’m going to have to weight it up with the other offers that might come in and also I’d like to get to know a bit more about Nova Corp first”
Rob, shook his head and leaned in towards Jim “I understand all that, I really do, but Nova is not the same game and Landis or anyone else who will come calling, this is a one time offer that expires right now. If you want in you need to make the choice right here right now” He pushed a contract over to the table to Jim along with a pen and waited expectantly.
Jim was flustered now “Rob, I don’t think I can sign anything now…”
Rob put up his hand interrupting Jim, his tone now hard “Jim, Shane told me you were a risk taker, this offer expires by the time the waiter has cleared this table. In or not?” 
Jim looked around and saw their waiter making her way over to thier table to do just that.
Rob pressed “You’ll never see an offer like this again Jim, and you’ll never ever regret choosing Nova”
Jim felt he was starting to sweat, Rob’s right the offer was simply unreal, maybe this is the way they do business at this level, he’d expected it to be cut throat, but this was something else.
“What the hell”Jim exclaimed as he pick up the pen and signed.
Rob smiled broadly and clapped his hands, “Great choice Jim! Sorry to put you on the spot like that, but we only want people that can handle pressure and make good decisions on the spot”
Jim relaxed, it was done, his future was set now, the sky might not even be the limit.
The waitress had arrived and was clearing the table as Rob and Jim sat in silence, she asked Jim “Sir are you finished with your glass” Jim just waved her away his mind elsewhere.
She cleared the plates, but fumbled his wine glass. With a crash it dropped onto the table and shattered, red wine splashing across the white table cloth and Jim’s brand new contract with Nova.
“Jim without even thinking shouted “Fuck! Watch what you’re doing… do you know what you just did?”
She yelped at Jim tone “I’m so sorry sir, I didn’t mean too, I’ll clean this up right away”
Jim was still angry and barked back at her “Fucking idiot, you’ve just spilled a thousand dollar glass of wine over a contract worth more money than you could even imagine, are you sorry about that” He picked up the now soggy contract stained red with wine and shook it in her face. she recoiled “See that, it’s a real job, nothing like you’ll ever get you fucking Troff”
Rob reached out and put his hand over Jim’s “Alright there Jim, calm it down, we’re still in public” 
Turning to the waitress Rob, smiles and nods towards Jim conspiratorially “Don’t worry about him, he’s just had a big day, it’s just paper we can reprint it anytime… you go head and grab ne the bill sand I’ll get this one one his way home”
The waitress smiles relieved by Rob’s kind touch and takes the empty plates away.
Rob, still holding Jim’s hand with an iron grip, slowly lowers the contract still grasped in Jims hand to the table  “Jim, don’t worry about it, the signature was just symbolic, we got the digital approval when you said yes. Anyway lets get you home my friend, you just cost me a $500 tip, you owe me lunch on your first day!”
Jim relieved now it’s all smoother over, chuckles, “You’re on”


#
The autocab hummed, the quiet rumble of the tyres on the road numbed Jim, just what he needed. 
The three bottles of red wine they consumed over the two-hour lunch weighed heavily on him, his thoughts as blurred as the scenery slipping past on his way home.
Jim sifted the mess of what had just happened. Had he signed a contract without reading it?
He had, but it was more money than he’d ever thought he’d make.
Level 8 was clearly a shark’s game and he’d seen it’s teeth today. Now he had time to breathe, he saw Rob had plied him with drinks and food before hitting him with the deal. Jim resolved to be more careful.
His phone alerted, the noise stabbing at the back of eyes.
A message flared on the screen:
“Dear Jim Dalton, you have been issued a warning: Anti-social Language in a public area. This warning carries a penalty of 50 citizenship points. You have 30 days to appeal this penalty. To view more details, log in to your portal.”
Shit, he was only 12 points over the line for Level 8, barely clinging there by his fingertips. Jim’s head throbbed as the warning clawed deep.
Almost immediately, his phone blared with a call —Shane. The embarrassment from the lunch’s end flooded back in a huge wave; his pulse jumped.
Without opening his eyes, he answered, trying hard to sound light, “Hi Shane, I didn’t expect your call.”
“I hear you’ve got a new job! Congrats!” Shane crowed.
That snapped Jim’s funk—Shane was right; he’d signed on for the biggest job of his life. “Yes, thank you so much for setting up the lunch. I can’t thank you enough.”
Shane was magnanimous. “Jimmy, I might’ve nudged you, but it’s your hard work.”
His tone shifted, serious now. “Rob says you had a bit much wine, want to tell me about it?”
Jim squirmed. “I’m okay, just got excited, that’s all.”
Shane prodded Jim now, “You’ve signed with Nova Corp, where I work. No slip-ups, Jimmy.”
Jim mumbled, “I know, I know.”
Shane pressed, “Anything else to tell me?”
Did he know?
Jim admitted sheepishly, “Yeah, I just got a language warning—50 point, but I can appeal it.”
Shane replied, “Jim, you can’t afford warnings. The job’s Level 8 only. My lawyer will handle the appeal—I’m sure he can sort it—but you’d better stay sharp from now on.”
Jim was flooded with relief Shane understood. “Absolutely, Shane, you won’t need to worry about me.”
The line was already dead. 
Jim sagged back, all his remaining energy bled from him as the cab sliced through late afternoon traffic. He didn’t want to contemplate how close he’d come to ruining it all.






CHAPTER 4

PROBATIONARY CITIZEN (NEIL 1)

Citizenship Act (revised 2045) 
Section 4, Subsection 3 – Citizenship Status
8.1 Citizenship Status Level 3 (Probationary Citizen) – A citizen that been released from full time incarceration will we deemed to be a Probationary Citizen.
8.2 Such a Citizen will start the probationary period of no less than 12 months without any points, but may accrue points throughout the probationary period.
8.3 If a level of 250 points is not achieved in 12 months, the probationary period will be extended until such a level is achieved.
8.4 Such status will allow access to all government sponsored work programs and approved private sector work programs.
8.5 Furthermore, citizenship points will accrue at 0.25x the normal rate whilst a citizen holds this status. 
8.6 A level 3 citizen yearly credit allowance will be 25,000 fully restricted credits, to be used only for food and lodging, this amount will be over and above any private income earned. Credits will expire after 12 months if not used.
#
He is standing in the line waiting to be processed with about twenty or so other prisoners in a large windowless room. It’s lit with the kind of artificial light that makes everyone look like they’ve got jaundice. He thinks it’s odd not to see armed prison guards here, they never let inmates out of sight normally, but as always there are the cameras, silently watching every inch of the room. 
Maybe not having the guards here is one final test, he’s now self conscious.
He’s standing still on a yellow line painted on the polished concrete floor, there are other lines like to his left and right leading his fellow inmates up to the row of counters at the other end of the room, much like an airport passport control, except no one here has been anywhere for a long time.
It’s quiet, there is no talking allowed, the signs on the walls of the room stating this rule were not necessary, everyone in the queue knows the rules and no one is willing to do anything that could stop them from leaving today.
Five years stuck in here, five years of so-called re-education classes, working twelve hours a day, six days a week for a few credits you could blow on chocolate or noodles.
Five years since he’d seen his family, when he came in here he’d left his wife and three kids and they’d long since left him. He left a career as a commercial builder, hell he owned the company, but that’s gone too, seized and sold off by the government who took the proceeds to pay his fines and back taxes, supposedly. He never saw a credit of the business sale, once he was convicted he was disallowed from having any credits, it was like the last decade of his life working, earning and building a nest-egg for the future never existed.
He’s pretty sure his accountant owns the business now, well that’s what he heard anyway. You wouldn’t know once you’re in Jail they allowed no contact with the outside world. Makes sense really, not many people would voluntarily want to talk to an Incarcerated Citizen anyway, it would scrub off too many social association points.
It was a good business too, he’d built up a good team who’d worked for him, staff that has stayed with him for years and were, he’d thought at the time, loyal and perhaps even friends. He had good clients too, the kind that came back and did work with him again and again. He made really good money, more than he probably should have as it turns out, he did offset his profit with his philanthropy and social donations, he’d done free work for underprivileged families that he was genuinely proud off.
He was on track to becoming a Full Citizen around the time he was going to turn forty-five or so. That was the point he thought he could take the foot off the gas and semi-retire, maybe have one of his children start to take over the business as he and his wife did some serious adventure travelling before they got too old to enjoy it. He’d always wanted to hike the Inca Trail to Machu Picchu before he was too old to really enjoy it.
Five years ago it was his plan and his life was good.
Until it wasn’t.
#
The first thing he noticed back on the day changed his life forever just over five years ago was that his bookkeeper hadn’t turned up to work yet, neither had his office manager, which was odd as it was the end of month they had lots on that day, it was even more odd as they normally beat him into the office most days.
Must be bad traffic he had thought at the time and made a note to ask his AI to check the traffic reports when he got to his desk. 
He set about turning the office lights on, his office wasn’t so fancy that the lights were activated when he’d unlocked the front door, he was a builder and didn’t need to waste money on fancy office automations. 
He walked through the small reception area by the empty reception desk, which was more of an office prop than functional, he’d never had receptionist, but he thought the dark wood desk and his company logo in embossed glass on the wall behind it gave just the right impression to visitors. That he made the desk himself gave him a little proud moment each morning, he was always sure to give it a friendly pat when he went past it.
He’d walked past the five small offices, flipping on the lights as he went through to the open area at the back of the office where he could set up the big boardroom desk for important meetings. The small kitchen off to the side was also his handiwork, no point paying someone else when you could do a better job for free, after all what kind of builder would he be if he couldn’t showcase his own work.
He remembers checking that the coffee machine was topped up with water and a fresh roast, and then checking if the fridge had ordered enough milk, bread and snacks for the rest of the of the week. The screen on the fridge was showing the order had been sent and would be delivered in a few hours.
This was such a well trodden path that he’d didn’t even need to think about it, what he actually thinking of was making a mental checklist of all the things he needed to do that day. It was an old fashioned habit the his team ribbed him about all the time calling him the human computer, actually more often an abacus if he wanted to admit it. His team all just relied on thier personal AI’s to set thier days up for them and couldn’t understand why he didn’t follow suit.
The reality was he had never taken to having his personal AI do anything important for him, it just never felt right, sure he used it for menial tasks, but never for anything he could easily do himself, secretly he thought relying on an AI for everything was dumbing everyone down.
With the coffee machine still needing to brew for a few more minutes, he walked the ten or so step to his office, it was the closet office to the kitchen, and did was he always did a the start of every day since he taken the plunge and went out on his own, sat down to check his bank account.
The computer had already turned on and ready for him to start work. His watch had notified the desktop terminal he was at the office as soon as he’d unlocked the door. His desktop terminal knew his routine and had opened up his messages, emails, news and  banking apps before he’d even sat down in his chair.
He reflexively clicked through to the banking app.
[Please Log-in again] was flashing on the screen.
Damn, what’s his password he thought, cursing his decision not to let his AI store it. He thought for a moment and it popped right into his mind.
He entered the details.
[No account know under this name]
Damn, he must have typed it in wrong, it was too early for this crap he thinks. Slowly and deliberately he types it again.
[No account know under this name]
Same message, what the hell?
A new email notification pops up from on the bottom of his screen.
[Message from United Banking Services regarding your account]
Ok good, he thought, they are already sorting the problem.
He opened the email and as he started reading he started to fell a chill descend onto him.
“Dear Customer,
We are writing to inform you that United Banking services is no longer able to provide you with any banking services at this time.
As a result your account has been closed and all funds will be held in trust by the bank until such time as the Court orders appropriate distribution of the funds.
This decision is final and no further correspondence will be entered into.
Regards,
United Banking Fraud Department”
Fraud Department, Funds held in trust, what the fuck is going on here? 
He sat still, not moving, barely breathing and he started to processing it all.
He didn’t get to sit for more than a minute or two,  it felt like mere seconds, before he heard loud banging on the office door as someone was yelling through it. The door wasn’t locked. 
Then all of a sudden, a huge crash as the glass door shattered which snapped him out of the frozen state he hadn’t realised he was in.
He got up to go and see what was going on. He didn’t even get around his desk when black clad heavily armed Police rushed into his office, guns drawn and pointing directly at him
 They were screaming at to get face down on the floor, he hesitated for a split second, this was enough for two of them to crash tackle him to the ground. As soon as he went down his face was showed with prejudice into the carpeted floor and his arms were wrenched behind him and handcuffed.
Despite the handcuffs, the pressure on his face hadn’t let up.
An unknown voice spoke from behind as tactical mask. “Neil Lucas, you are under arrest for bribery, fraud and violations of the citizen code of conduct.”
“Under the revised criminal act of the Bi-Continental Government of the Americas you do not have the right to silence, not cooperating with Police will be held against you in a court of law. You will now be transferred into detention where you will remain until the investigation is concluded and you case is heard before a judge. A lawyer will be appointed to you. Do you understand these rights?”
He could actually respond as the weight from at least three or four Police was pressing him into the floor.
“I’ll take your lack of response as a Yes”
It was only when he was being dragged out of the office that the shock had started wearing off slightly that was able to croak out the words
“You’ve got the wrong person, I haven’t done anything…”
One of the Police who is dragging him to the wagon starts laughing at him.
“We never get the wrong person,

#
He was to learn later at his trial that this was not mere coincidence, both of them had been working with the Police Serious Fraud Division for months passing on information about his business in exchange for immunity.
When they said he was under arrest for multiple bribery and fraud charges, his whole world had turned upside down.
He still wasn’t sure what he had done wrong, he understood what he was eventually convicted of well enough, but that he was at fault and deserved five years in prison is something he had a long tine to think about.
What he actually was guilty of was the well known crime of getting duped by a get rich quick scheme. He reflected long and hard about how he’d been duped into the supposed inside track on a new tendering scheme for government contracts, it had just seemed too real and too easy at the time. 
The scheme had worked really well, he’d won some amazing projects and earned some really good money, the profits in his bank account were definitely not fake. At the time he was surprised to win so many of the projects, it seemed he couldn’t lose. He put in his best offers and he had a good reputation in the industry so he took the wins. What he didn’t know was the whole scheme was fake, they were real contracts all right, but built into the project costings were various bribes to unions, lawyers and crooked businesses. The tenders were deliberately set about ten percent above the actual value, the bribes were already built in and just looked like legitimate payments. 
His company was the lead project manager, so when the money was moved around it was done with his bank and his personal authorisation. Technically that meant he was paying the bribes himself.
Now five years on and the only two things he’d worked out was he was too hungry for success and got blinded by the possibility of his plans coming to pass sooner than he expected, he honestly had pictured himself hiking the world before he was forty , not fifty. The other thing he’d worked out was the people that duped him were much smarter than he was, they’d manipulated him from thier very first meeting and then every step of the way they fleeced the projects until he got arrested.
He’d find them eventually.
Standing in the line to be released from prison he was still white hot angry that being duped and set-up had cost him five years and pretty much everything else in his life. No one else was caught, no one else was even charged, the shell companies just disappeared and the money was moved around so much no one knew where it went, not that they tried too hard. 
The judge didn’t care, it was his name on the payments, he was guilty. The trial took less than a day to play out.
It was his turn to be processed now, how long had he been standing there day dreaming?
He stepped forward to the counter. It was staffed by a women in her mid thirties who was dressed immaculately like one of those high powered lawyers you saw on TV, she smiled a million dollar smile and introduced herself.
“Hello, today’s the big day for you, congratulations! My name is Jocelyn Bates and I’ve been appointed as your case worker for the next twelve months whilst you work through your Probationary status, of course if you don’t earn the required points and your probation is renewed, unfortunately I would not be allowed to continue to work with you, but lets not get too far ahead of ourselves. Can you confirm your name please”
“Neil Lucas”
“Lucas… she looks over her files and picks his out of the pile on the counter in front of her 
“Here we go” She’s reading the summary sheet and her eyebrow raises just a fraction.
“Lucas Construction, 12 counts of bribery, 8 counts of wire fraud and an extra six months added for  not pleading guilty, seems harsh”
“I thought so too” Neil quips.
“Please don’t interrupt Mr Lucas and we’ll get through this quicker”
Neil holds his tongue.
Jocelyn is flipping pages on his files making some quick notes, she doesn’t say a word.
She looks up “Well Mr Lucas, all of this seems in order, you’ve served your full term, there was no early release requested, did you not have a lawyer to do that?”
“No, my court appointed lawyer ignored me after I was convicted and no one would take my calls, I’m surprised I even got a case worker”
“All probationary citizens get a case worker Mr Lucas, who you get is luck of the draw, it just so happens today is my day for social volunteering case work. Don’t worry, we’ll get you sorted”
Neil is getting the impression he’s just a box to tick for his new case worker.
She softens a little “Now, I’m informed that your wife has declined to be the post incarceration contact and you have no other family to stay with, is that correct?”
“Yes”
“Ok we’ll get you set up in some temporary accommodation, you’ll receive your full credit allowance into you account as soon as this day ticks over. Just log-in to your personal portal via this new phone” She picks up a box , one of a dozen or so similar boxes that are on the counter and hands it to him.
“That phone is to be with you at all times, no exceptions. Failure could result in you being arrested again. My number is saved on it and you can contact me anytime if you have any issues, now I might not answer straight away, but make sure you leave details when you leave a message. Other than that we’ll meet up one a month at the nominated offices, the address is on the information sheet” 
Jocelyn hands over a folder, the title on the front reads “Probationary Citizen Information Pack”.
“Good luck Mr Lucas, keep your nose clean and we’ll get you through this so you can start your life again”
She ushers him past the counter to the exit with a small wave.
“Thanks” he say says, but she’s already occupied with a new file as he walks past
#
Neil looks out the window of the grimy autocab that is taking him to his new home for at least the next twelve months. 
He doesn’t recognise the neighbourhood, but he knows what kind of place it is from seeing neighbourhoods just like it on the news, or so he remembers, not being able to watch the news for the last five years.
Its an area of town you avoided if you wanted to keep your status clean, a place where no one over Level  wants to be in by choice, back in the day he would have derisively called it the Outer and refused to take on work out here.
It’s no slum, far from it, the government would never let real dirt and filth stay around long, but it’s not the shiny modern city with lush manicured greenery that you might expect these days. The buildings are much more utilitarian, all raw concrete with the very little to soften the architecture. 
This suburb and many other like it is what society says is the minimum you can live with, enough to keep you safe, sheltered and fed, but with little else. What colour you can see is spontaneous street art, not legal, but it’s not worth it to keep covering it up, so it stays.
Neil is taking it all in, the grey drab scenery affecting his mood. He was already planning how to get out of here, and back to his old world, it would be a grind but he always backed himself.
The Cab stops in front of a small four story apartment building, jolting Neil back into the real world.
The halfway house is a nondescript grey box on a nondescript street, but at least he can see a supermarket and a few shops close by, something to explore once he’s settled in.
He’s greeted at the door by a messy man, tall and quite round, with five day growth about as long as his buzz cut. He’s cleat not soft as his handshake almost crushes Neil’s hand as he introduces himself.
“Hi, Neil? I’m Grant the House Manager, we’ve been expecting you”
He gestures Neil inside “Welcome to your new home, lets go in”
Once inside Grant ushers Neil past the small foyer and reception area into his office. It’s surprisingly clean and well organised, the terminal is pretty late model and the furnishing is new enough to still give off a faint factory smell.
In fact the whole place smells a bit antiseptic.
“Take a seat Neil, congratulations on your first day of freedom!”
Neil laughs “Thanks, was nice to see the back of the prison”
“I’m sure, I’m sure… now lets get you booked in and sorted out, I’m going to run through as few things here for you, standard orientation stuff, but I’m legally obliged to record this and your responses, are you ready?
“Yep, shoot”
Grant takes a beat with Neil’s turn of phrase “For the purposed of the recording Yes is all I need to hear ok?
“Yes”
“Good” Grant presses record on his terminal and starts reading off the screen. 
“Neil, based on the severity of your crimes and the length of you incarceration, you have been assigned to this transitional accommodation for a period of no less than twelve months.
“At the end of the twelve months we hope you have done enough work to be able to vacate this facility and reintegrate fully into society as a productive and lawful citizen.”
“If you have not earned the right to be reintegrated, you will be moved to a more permanent situation supplied by the government. Do you understand?”
“Yes” Neil answers.
“This facility is not free, as part of your path to becoming a productive citizen, it is important that you pay your own way, to that end the costs of living here will accrue for the next twelve months and be converted into a government loan. After twelve months interest, which has been set at the nominal federal rate, will be applied until the loan is paid off. Do you understand?”
Neil was shocked, no one had mentioned this “How much does this place cost?
A raised eyebrow from Grant “Yes only”
“Yes”
Grant continues “You will be assigned employment based on your skill profile, you are expected to remain employed in that role for the full duration of twelve months. do you understand?
“Yes”
Grant clicks a button on the terminal “Ok, that’s done, official part is over… sorry about that it always surprises me how little they tell you about life after prison. If it’s any consolation I didn’t know either”
“You were in jail?”
Grant waves his hand “it was a while ago but nothing changed it seems. Lets get you settled in to your room”
“So how much will I owe at the end of my time here? How am I supposed to build back up?” Neil asks as they leave the office and head to the lift.
“I don’t get to see that info, it’s different for everyone, all depends on what you did and who you were before. It’s all on that new phone they gave you, you just have to look it up”
They stop on the third floor, Grant walks Neil to one of the eight apartments on the floor, 307 and opens up the door. “It’s coded to you, and also to me if I need to go in for any reason, although I never do. The bus will be out the front at 6:30 am sharp to take you to work, don’t be late it doesn’t wait for anyone. There’s some food in kitchen, my welcome gift to you, and some work clothes that should fit you, you’ll have your monthly credit in your account now so from now on anything you want is all up to you.
Neil steps in to the apartment and asks “Work, where am I working?”
“Like I said, I don’t get to know that, just get on the bus tomorrow at 7am sharp… oh and the curfew here is 8pm, which was about twenty minutes ago, so you’re in for the night now, but the rec room is open all night, it’s just behind the reception downstairs.” 
Damn, Neil was looking forward to taking a walk later, and he had so many more questions for Grant, but he was already back down to corridor getting in the lift.






CHAPTER 5

THE OUTER (NEIL 2)

Neil inspected the apartment, a small and uninspiring one-bedroom unit. He walked the well-worn track in the dull grey office-grade carpet from the front door to the small galley-style kitchen perched on one side of the dark open-plan space. 
The air hung heavy with the faint smell of a thousand previous meals, no one odour winning the battle; the echoes of those meals reminded Neil he was hungry. He opened the kitchen cabinets on the wall, the doors having long since lost their perfect white luster. The waft of disinfectant assaulted his nostrils—at least it had been cleaned, he thought. The gift of food from Grant turned out to be a box of five packets of instant noodles. 
He opened the dented fridge door—nothing. He dropped into one of the chairs at the small round dining table, the plastic veneer of its surface scratched and cold under his touch. 
Neil sat silently, taking in his new home. A faint, almost imperceptible buzz emanated from the old TV, even when it was off. The thin walls did little to block the muffled sounds of neighbouring apartments, a constant low rumble of other people’s lives. 
That’s enough feeling sorry for myself—from here on, I only move forward, Neil scolded himself. He set about preparing the noodles—he was well-practiced; this was prison food. 
He slumped into the couch, instant noodles cooling in a cracked bowl on the small coffee table in front of him. The aroma from the noodles, a chemical sweetness, was the only thing that could overpower the other smells in the room. The relative quiet and dark was a welcome change from the constant noise and light of the prison life he’d left behind. 
Neil pulled out the phone they’d given him. It wasn’t new and was almost without any apps—he’d already tried to install news and opinion ones on his trip here, simple things he’d used back in the day, but it was all blocked. Sighing with sudden weariness, he checked his Personal Citizenship Points Portal, or PCP, as it was most commonly shortened to. 
His monthly point allowance had been deposited—just over 2,000 credits, barely enough to live on and a far cry from the 6,000 baseline credits he’d received as a Level 7. Neil wondered how much he’d get paid over and above the baseline in the new job and was plotting what he’d do once he started climbing back up the ladder. Sure, he’d need patience, but he’d built his business from scratch before, and he was confident he could do it again. 
Then he noticed an extra line on his points statement: Government housing loan accrual: 1,000 credits. That was half his monthly allowance! How the hell could he get ahead? 
Neil sat there, stunned; he’d owe almost half his year’s baseline credits at the end of the twelve months, and then they’d add interest on it. He sat on the worn couch, the street light outside bleeding through the too-thin curtains, and silently raged against the system. 
They wouldn’t break him.
#
Neil had no chance of missing the bus in the morning; he simply hadn’t slept. He’d lain in bed replaying the events of the last five years in his head, snippets of thoughts flashing in and out of his mind’s eye in the half-awake, half-sleeping hours before the sun rose. 
It was old territory, nothing he hadn’t done countless times in jail. He’d thought he might leave it behind when he finally got free—he was wrong; it followed him. 
The bus was almost full by the time it got to his building. He didn’t feel like talking to anyone, so he’d waited in his apartment until it had arrived. Neil was in no mood for socialising this morning. 
He sat in an empty row a few rows behind the driver, hoping he was the last onto the bus. 
He wasn’t. Just as the bus doors closed, a tall, wiry man burst through the front doors of the apartment building yelling, “Wait, wait…” and sprinted after the bus that was already gaining speed to its next destination. 
The bus driver swore and slammed on the brakes, jolting everyone on board forward violently. The passengers universally responded with a chorus of profane complaining. 
In the row behind him, a large man, easily outweighing Neil by fifty pounds of muscle, yelled, “Hey Mike, where’d you learn to drive? You owe me a coffee!” 
There was a round of agreement. 
The man caught up to the bus and jumped through the doors, thanking the bus driver profusely. “Hey Mike, thanks, man—you know I’m never late. I owe you one!” 
Mike said, “Be on time—there won’t be a next time.” 
The man looked around and saw the empty seat next to Neil. 
Shit. 
The passenger behind Neil was still grumbling along with some others about the sudden stop. He said to the man as he got to Neil’s seat, “Hey, man, you’d better not be late again—now you owe me a coffee!” 
The man calmly stared him down and coldly said, “Tully, I don’t owe you anything. Last I remembered, you owed me something.” 
Tully pushed back. “I ain’t got nothing for you and didn’t owe you shit.” 
The man, still standing in the aisle as the bus pulled away from the curb again, sighed. “Okay, Tully, some people will be disappointed, but hey, it’s your choice.” 
Tully paled slightly at the mention of the other people. The bus was deadly quiet, all the passengers fixated on this confrontation. Neil worried he’d end up in the middle of a brawl on his first full day out of prison. 
Tully’s seatmate nudged him. “Tully, don’t—it’s not a fight you want to pick.” 
Tully looked down, suddenly engrossed in his phone. 
The man sat next to Neil and, with a smile, introduced himself. “Hi, friend, name’s Rook—not my real name, but everyone calls me that.” 
Neil reluctantly engaged Rook. He nodded his head toward Tully sitting right behind them and said quietly, “Is this going to be a problem?” 
Rook laughed. “Nope, Tully’s all talk. We just had some business that he didn’t get around to finishing yet—don’t worry, no brawl on the bus today.” 
Neil relaxed very slightly, still ready for something to kick off. 
Rook continued their conversation with far too much energy. “So, you’re new—when did you get out?” 
Neil again reluctantly responded—this was exactly what he’d been hoping to avoid. “Yesterday.” 
Rook blew out his cheeks. “Wow, total green! Don’t worry, it’s a blur at first, but you get used to it. Though some days I can’t tell the difference between this and jail.” 
That did nothing to help Neil’s mood; he didn’t respond.
