Neil stopped suddenly, the back office wasn’t empty. Inside the small room the Manager was doing some sort of accounting on her terminal, Neil could see the screen and was familiar enough to recognise the kind of program she was using. It brought back a wave of nostalgia, he used to stay late to tidying up the accounts years ago when he owned his construction business.
The door was wide open and he need to cross it to get the the staff exit, he held his breath and watch from the shadows. She was absorbed in her work, so he just took the plunge and darted past the open door as quickly and quietly as he could.
He didn’t stop and went straight to the staff exit, opening the door and getting outside as fast as he could. He pressed his hand against the door to slow its close to a faint click. 
So far so good, Neil thought, no alarms had gone off and no one had seen him. He stood for a moment in the doorway his heart pounding loudly in his chest. 
Keep moving he told himself as he stepped away from the door and into the laneway behind the building. The laneway connected all the shops and offices rear entrances and was dark and quiet at this time of night. Neil dodged the industrial waste bins in the laneway and came out onto the street around the corner from his half-way house where no one could see him.
He knew there was public bus stop just a few blocks over and as he set out for it he checked his watch again, 1 hour and 45 minutes before curfew, he’d be cutting it fine.
#
Neil turned off his phone before he got to the bus stop, he wasn’t sure if it would help or not, but he wasn’t taking any chances. 
He desperately wanted to see his family again, even despite being told they had decide to sever all contact with him more than five years ago. If he was able to just see then from a distance to know they were ok, to see how much his daughter had grown, to have any kind of connection, he could endure his time in the Rust Belt working his way back up to be in thier life again.
There was no direct bus from the Outer to the Green, he have to change busses at the terminal hub which was on the loose border between the two zones. There were no check point or gates, but over the space of a dozen miles or so, the drab dirty grey of the Outer slowly turned over to the verdant green of the affluent suburbs where Neil used to live a lifetime ago.
He’d worked out that he had about ten minutes on the ground at his house, not much time, but enough. He couldn’t afford to miss the busses back.
Neil sat on the bus to the Hub going over in his mind what his wife and daughter would look like now? 
Neil accepted he’d probably only get a glimpse of them inside the house from the outside, but he was seriously toying with just knocking on the door and to hell with the consequences, it would be worth it.
The bus stopped at the hub and Neil stepped off with a resolve to speak to his family one way or the other. 
He waited a few minutes for the next bus to his old suburb, standing against the wall away from the cameras trying to be as unnoticeable as possible.
It only took another fifteen minutes to get to his old street, checking his watch again he had less than ten minutes before the bus swung by the stop on the way back to the hub.
He only had about five houses to walk, each step felt heavy and his house seems miles away. After what felt like an eternity he was standing on the footpath outside his old house. 
All his plans just disappeared, staring silently at the house he built,the home where his family grew from just his wife and himself to include his daughter. All the memories they’d made together in this house came flooding back, and then realisation of all of the memories he’d missed over the last five years made him equally sad and angry.
Who were these people who had taken his life and torn it all apart? 
He would find out.
The garage door of the house sprung to life, the panels starting to tilt up into the ceiling at the same time as the lights in the garage came on.
It was at this point Neil realised he’d just been standing on the street and staring at his house. 
Shit, how long had he been standing there? He panicked and sprinted away to hide behind a tree just down the street. A car was pulling into the driveway
Neils’ heart was in his mouth, was that his wife?
No, it was man. Before Neil even had time to think a woman stepped out of the passenger side and a young boy, not more than seven or eight burst out of the rear door and sprinted into the garage to disappear inside. The man and woman almost in unison admonished the boy, but he was long gone.
They followed the boy inside through the garage door and it smoothly closed behind them, eventually the lights in the garage went out and the front of the house fell silent.
Neil hadn’t considered that his family might not be there, they’d owned the house outright before he went into prison. His dreams of a reunion were dashed and he felt the weight being alone heavy on his shoulders as he turned around and deliberately walked away from his old life. 
He had to run the catch the bus, the trip back to his now home was a blur of bitter disappointment.
#
For everyone else it was just another day, for Neil it was the first day he really felt there was nothing to work towards. His family had disappeared from the house they’d built together and he was stuck out here the Outer in a half-way house working six days a week racking up debt that he had no hope of paying off. 
He’d made it back the night before with about a minute to spare before the curfew, at least that had gone to plan. Neil spent most of the night tossing and turning thinking over just what he was going to do now, he had no idea what time he’d eventually fallen asleep. It was a violent shock to be woken with banging on his apartment door.
Almost falling out of his bed he stumbled top the door and open it to see Grant standing there. Groggily Neils asked “What time is it, did I miss the bus?” a wave of dread spreading over his body, missing the bus was bad.
Grant, ignoring Neil’s question “My office in ten minutes” he turned and walked away from Neil’s door leaving Neil standing in his open door barely awake and very confused.
Nine minutes later Neil was downstairs sitting in Grant’s office still, now that he was awake he was sure this was about his excursion last night, he didn’t think anyone had recognised him.
Dispensing with any small talk Grant spoke “Neil, do you want to tell me about last night?
Yep, he was right. Neil had leant in prison not to admit to anything, just in case those accusing didn’t know all the facts. 
He answered calmly “I don’t know, I went for a walk, I got back before curfew so not sure why there’s a problem?”
Grant, moving from annoyed to visibly frustrated said “Don’t bullshit me Neil, tell me why I got a notice this morning not only from the local metro Police, but also from the fucking Citizenship Enforcement Bureau?”
Shit! “I’m sure you’re going to tell me” Neil said defiantly.
“Damn right I will, not only did you leave the local jurisdiction area, you’re curfew area, but you also went all the way to the Green to stand outside your old house!” Grant was on a roll and Neil wasn’t in any place to interrupt him.
“ You’re just lucky these were only location warnings, if your family had still been there and you’d made contact, they would have hauled you back into jail on the spot. What part of you’re a Probationary Citizen and not actually free to do what you want don’t you understand?”
Neil was getting angry himself “Fuck you and fuck the whole system, it’s like I never left prison, who the fuck is anyone to tell me I can’t go and see my wife and child”
Grant looked like his head was going to burst “Neil, check your attitude right fucking now! You’re just lucky your family had left years ago and you didn’t exceed the penalty threshold or you’d be sitting in prison right now. As it you’re one minor penalty away from going back anyway”
The room fell silent as Grant’s words resonated with Neil, he was not going back prison.
Grant softened his tone “Look you’ve only been out for a couple of weeks, this is not the kind attention you need. Your family is completely off limits until you get back up to Level 6, and you need accept that.
Neil was silent.
Grant continued “Neil, I’m trying to advise you here, but I can’t if you’re pushing back, fighting the system isn’t going to get you anywhere except stuck in forever as permanent restricted citizen.”
Neil was calmly taking Grant’s words in, beneath the facade he was seething. He could see it know, they were trying to hold him back as a virtual slave forever, how many countless others had sat in chairs like this he wondered.
Grant, seeing that Neil seemed to be listening ended their meeting “Ok, good, message delivered, I don’t want to be here again Neil, you better make a move to catch your bus”
Neil got up and left the office without a word.

#
The next few weeks were different for Neil, he simply did as he was told, never offering another word of defiance. Outwardly he played the role of the chastened man, acknowledging his error and working to be the prefect citizen again. 
Work, sleep, work, sleep, his routine was like clockwork, never straying from the bounds of the office or his apartment building and the convenience store.
Grant still kept a close eye on him, but Neil gave him no cause for alarm. 
All the while Neil was waiting.
This morning he got on the bus with plenty of time to spare as he had every morning since his warning. He scanned the occupied seats looking to see if he recognised anyone on the bus. 
There! About halfway up the bus with an empty seat next to him he found who he was looking for. Purposely he made his was into the bus and sat down in the empty seat next to the person he’d spotted
“Good morning Neil, I haven’t seen you in a while” the man said.
Neil responded in cautious tone “Morning Rook, it’s been a while since you’ve been on my bus”
 Rook nodded “I get around, as it happens I’ve got some work to do in your direction”
With that short exchange they both settled in for the trip the Rust Belt whilst the rest of the passengers took their seats and said thier Hello’s to friends and colleagues.
After a while Rook addressed Neil “ So Neil, how’s life on the outside treating you?”
Neil, nervous knew this was important “No as well as I would have hoped, in fact if I’m being honest pretty shitty actually”
Rook gave a short laugh “No.. it never lives up to the hype, more of the same out here as you have back inside”
Neil was pretty sure he was saying the right things, but he didn’t want to blow it said “Some days I don’t see the point, I can’t really see a way out”
Rook studies Neil for a few moments “It can feel like that, but you’ve only been out for a short time so let some time pass, mind your own business and you’ll find a way.”
He’d blown it, after weeks of waiting to find Rook, he laid it on too thick.
Deflated Neil mumbled “yep.. I expect you’re right, I’ve got all the time in the world now”
Rook nodded, apparently done with the conversation, looked out the window at the grey city sliding past. Neil just sat there staring at the back of the seat in front of him.
After a few minutes, without turning back to look at Neil, Rook said “Of course, you might just be feeling down because you don’t have any friends, did you consider that?”
Startled out of his funk, Neil looked at Rook, this was it “You’re right, I don’t have any friends out here, I think I’d like to start making some”
Rook turned to look at Neil, his eyes bore into Neil’s, but Neil didn’t shy away “Smartest thing you’ve said since I’ve met you Neil, perhaps there’s some hope for you yet” Rook smiled and broke his gaze with Neil.
The pressure of that stare was enough to unnerve Neil, but he needed to push forward. “So.. got any suggestions where I’d start?”
Rook shook his head and passed him a folded note “For when you get off the bus, make sure you shred it properly”
Not another word was spoken for the rest of the trip. Neil pondered the note, it was burning a hole in his pocket, he was desperate to know what was on it.
Finally, the bus arrived at his stop and Neil and most of the passengers got off. Rook didn’t move, just nodded his farewell.
Neil jumped off the bus and once he’d cleared the throng of workers at the bus stop he opened the note and read it: 
352 Langston Street 7pm, leave the phone in the apt.
It was hand written, Rook clearly had it ready for Jim, did he find Rook or did Rook find him Neil wondered.
He didn’t know the street, but he’d be able to look it up on one of the work terminals, he wasn’t going to miss this meeting, it might be his only change to get out of here.
#
Neil struggled to concentrate on his work during the day, thankfully it was mindless work, just organising, collating and filing plans and project schedules for various construction projects. 
All he could think about was the meeting Rook had set up, luckily he was able to do map and property searches from his work terminal and dig deeper into the location he’d been given.
It was only a few blocks away from his halfway house, maybe a five minute walk. It looked like an unassuming small office building, 10 or so small office, very similar to the one he rented for his own company back in the day. According to planning record is was registered to a nameless shell company that Neil couldn’t get any information on. 
What he really wanted to know was how to get out if things went bad in the meeting, so he tapped into the public works database and found the building plans. A simple generic small office building design, built about forty or so years ago when they tried, but ultimately failed, to gentrify the area. Not tall enough for lifts at three stories, there was a main set of stairs at the front, but more importantly a rear set of emergency stairs accessed through the back of each for the office. Neil filed that fact away for use if needed and checked out of the records portal quickly so his footprint was not noticed.
 A few hours later Neil had just arrived back at his apartment, he tried to act as nonchalant as possible on the bus ride back from work, like nothing was going on, just a regular boring day. Though he was sure he was giving himself away every time he moved, everything felt too forced and unnatural.
Neil had a bit of time to think about what he was going to do before he stepped into the unknown. He was sitting quietly in his small and sparsely furnished one bedroom apartment. Neil looked around and he couldn’t see any evidence that he actually lived there, no pictures or keepsakes, nothing that would let someone know Neil Lucas, formerly a business owner and a Level 7 citizen with a wife and daughter even existed. 
After his run in with Grant, he’d viewed the world differently, more cynically, it was like a shroud had been lifted. Everywhere he looked he could see the apparatus of the system holding him, and countless others, back. He’d climbed the ladder and built his life around the system, agreeing with the rules and working them to his benefit as much as he could, he never once never questioned it. He bought the story that the Citizenship points system was the best way to rebuild a fair and just society after the Great Reclamation. 
He’d never taken much notice of the lower levels, blindly assuming their life in the lower levels was thier own fault from lack of ambition and laziness and poor decision making. They could rise up just like Neil did anytime they wanted.
Stuck here in this halfway house, that he was paying half of his monthly allowance for, cut-off from his family seemingly forever, his life being controlled with curfews and location tracking Neil decided he wasn’t going to just sit and accept it. If the Greys offered him away to fight back he was going to take it.
Checking his watch he saw it was time to go. With a clarity he hadn;t had since he left prison Neil got up form the couch, took his phone out and deliberately set it down on the small dining room table. Even thought he’d memorised the walk to the meeting location, the idea of leaving his phone behind made hi feel uncomfortable. 
He strode to his front door, at the last moment he remembered he was still wearing his watch, which as all watches were, was linked to his phone. Disgusted at the thought of being tracked again, Neil took the watch off and threw it back into the room, not caring where it landed and walked out with a steely determination to change the course of his life.








CHAPTER 10

THE GREYS (NEIL 4)


It was 6:45pm, the sun had gone down, but the soft glow of dusk was keeping the street light enough for Neil to see the building across the road.
He’d taken a round-about route and tried as best he could to sneak up on the building. It looked different in real life, but he could pick out features he saw on the plans. The old brown brick facade gave up its age, although some of those bricks might have been over a hundred years old, the building itself was built only about ten or twenty years ago. Neil had been involved with some of these nouveau brown stone buildings using the reclaimed old city bricks. He’d never really understood the point of them, but was told by architects and town planners they were trying to bring create a warmer suburban streetscape than the ubiquitous glass and steel of the city. It was very costly and the buildings just looked dirty after a while, plus it never brought in the desired demographics, so the style was mostly abandoned for some new stylistic fad.
This particular building, the one Rook had told him to come to at 7pm, was dark. No lights were showing in any of the four floors of windows facing the street, apart from the lone light over the front door. 
Neil had placed himself across the road in unlight alley with a good view of the front of the building, in the ten minutes he’d been there he hadn’t seen anyone go in or out. Most of the offices would have closed hours ago, so it wasn’t surprising.
A bus passed, it was the 533 to the hub by the panel on the side of it. Neil was waiting for it, without a watch of any kind he’d figured he’d need to have some way to tell the time. He looked up the timetable before he left his apartment and knew the 533 would arrive at the stop a just a block up the road at 6:55pm. He knew had five minutes before he had to go inside, or not.
He swallowed at the thought of it, he just didn’t know enough about these so-called Greys, only snippets of rumours overheard from workers and a clear admonishment from Knox about not getting involved with Rook and his friends.
“Now wasn’t to the time to lose your nerve” he thought and he stood straight readying himself to dash across the road in a effort not to be seen by too many people, thankfully the traffic was light.
Suddenly, a firm hand grabbed his shoulder from behind and a voice whispered into his ear just inches away from him “See anything over there?” the voice mocked as the hand slowly released the pressure.
Neil turned around warily to see Rook standing in the alleyway behind him, his face somehow a mixture of amusement and threat at the same time.
“Not going to see anyone going into that building, smart move scouting it out” Rook said, a hint of approval in the words.
“Follow me” Rook said as he turned and walked into to the darkness of the alley.
Neil did as he was told, how long had they been watching him standing here thinking he was so smart that he’d get the jump on them somehow?
Moments later a door in the side of the building opened for Rook, a faint light spilled out onto the alley blocked mostly by another man in the doorway.
He gruffly barked “Hurry up”
Neil followed Rook inside, the door was closed quietly behind him, bolts were locked, Neil wasn’t getting out of this.
#
Neil was standing in a corridor, the led panel in the ceiling was painted black suffocation most of it’s light. The door to the alley had been locked shut by a mechanical lock, the likes of which Neil hadn’t seen in real life. A wheel in the centre of the door pushed bars into bolts into the door frame on all four sides. No digital access codes here, strictly steel and human muscle to lock it.
Rook nodded at the burly man who’d locked it shut, he turned away and walked down the corridor towards the front of the building and disappeared around a corner.
Neil stood there not knowing what to do, did he follow the man or not? 
Rook spoke in normal voice “Glad you could make it, sorry about the subterfuge, you never can be too careful, not everyone can keep their secrets” Rook’s eyes bore into Neil, was he waiting for him to say something? 
Neil didn’t flinch and shrugged “You do what you need to do I guess”
Rook simply nodded, the silence lingered. Neil avoided the need to fill it with hos own words, a trick he’d learnt in prison: silence was often a trap, baiting you into saying more than you wanted to. So he waited.
After few minutes the man came back, despite outweighing Neil and Rook by at least fifty pounds, he was light and quiet on his feet.
He spoke to Rook “I can’t see anyone out there, scanners show no signs either.”
Rook said “Good, keep an eye on it, they might be waiting to turn thier devices on later to catch us out”
Neil was confused “Catch who out?” he asked.
Rook, glancing back at Neil said coldly “Friends of yours maybe, you’d better hope there aren’t any”
Speaking again to the man “If any thing pop’s up you come running to us, understood?”
“Understood” said the man, and strode back down the corridor and out of sight.
Rook looked at Neil appraisingly “So, first test passed, but that was just the first course, the real test comes next” he gestured the down the corridor the opposite direction to the where man had gone and started walking.
Neil having been standing in the same spot for the last few minutes was caught flatfooted and he scrambled to catch up as Rook reached the end of the corridor, opened the door and waved Neil into the room.
Neil brushed past Rook and entered the room, it was hard to make out anything in the room, the LED ceiling panels were painted back just like in the corridor. He stepped inside the room a bit further instinctively to allow Rook to come in, but he didn’t. Rook simply smiled and closed the door behind Neil.
That was the second time the doors have been shut behind him tonight thought Neil as he stood in the dimly light room.
The room was silent and from what he could see empty, what the hell was this?
With a loud click, shocking bright lights flashed on directly in Neil’s face blinding him, instinctively he slammed his eyes shut as he put his hands up to shield his face from the painful brightness. He couldn’t see a thing, but he heard all to clearly a door open on the other side of the room and footstep clicking against the tiled floor.
“Hello Neil” a female voice said.
Who was this? Neil’s mind was racing, his eyes squeezed shut in pain making it hard to think.
“Take your time, relax you’ll get you eyesight back eventually” the female voice said.
He could hear furniture being dragged towards him, then the footsteps backed away.
“There’s a chair on your right, take seat, you’ll be more comfortable”
Neil felt around and his hand hit the back of the chair, he slumped into the seat getting his bearing back “Can you turn off the fucking lights!”
“Sorry, can’t its part of the security protocol, it’s best for both of if you can’t identify me, they stay on.” she said in a matter of fact way.
Neil was rubbing his eyes, the bright spots were slowly fading and he risked cracking his eyelids open, the lights were still there, but had been turned down.
Squinting into the room he could make out a dark figure behind the lights, but that was all.
“Ok, I’m here and I’m blind, what did you want?” Neil asked getting annoyed now.
A chuckle “I like the fire in you’re belly, we could use that. I guess an introduction is in order, you can call me Vex” the voice was smooth and commanding.
Neil didn’t think Vex was someone he wanted to cross just form the way she spoke, he reminded himself he was in dangerous territory and to watch his words carefully.
Vex spoke again “My friend Rook tells me you’ve been having a bit of a tough time since you left prison?”
Neil answered “I won’t lie to you, it’s been shit, I may as well have stayed in prison for all the difference it makes”
“I believe that Neil, most of us know that we’ll never be amongst the elites, but Neil you’ve been there, a Level 7 no less, so you have a unique perspective” Vex said.
She continued “I also understand that you’ve had a warning for trying to reach your wife and Daughter?”
How did they know that? “Fuck their warning, who are they to tell me if I can or can;t see my own flash and blood” the subject still got Neil angry.
Vex calmly said “Neil, whilst I love the passion, a word of advice, keep it under wraps if you want to be my friend, I need people who can control themselves”
Chastened Neil responded “I can control myself just fine, but we’d better be talking about away to get the people who put me here”
“Oh Neil, we can do better than that. What if I told you your family never requested no contact, that they’ve been kept from you as much as you’ve been kept from them?” Vex’s voice was soothing.
Hope swelled up inside Neil, could this be true? were they just playing him?
Cautiously he said “I’d say that’s a big claim, you’d need to it prove to me”
“We can do that, lets make a deal, a quid pro quo if you will, I’ll get you in front of your family without any one knowing or you being sent back to prison, if you’ll do something for me… how does that sound?” Vex asked.
Neil was floored, could they really do it? “What do I have to do?”
Vex, as smooth as ever said “We’ll let you know in due course, all I need to know now is a yes or no”
Neil wasn’t sure what saying no would result in, but he didn’t have anything to loose anyway and said “You’ve got deal, but you’d better deliver”
“Back at you Neil, Rook will fill you in”
The light went out and the room was plunged into darkness, Neils eye’s had now adjusted to the brightness and he was blind again. The door at the other end of the room opened and shut as Vex left, Neil was alone again.
What had he agreed to do he wondered?
#
Neil was waiting for the morning bus to his job in the Rust Belt. The weather was starting to get cold now as Fall set in, here in the outer the cold seemed to get amplified by the drab mostly grey streetscape. Neil shuddered a little thinking what winter had in store here.
He was just going through the motions of his new life now, yet again he found himself waiting for something to happen. It had been hard to pretend things were normal for him, he found himself second guessing routine conversations, was the person he was talking a Grey, secretly testing him, was the new face on the bus a Grey keeping an eye on him. He was on edge.
Neil hadn’t heard anything more from his meeting over a week ago with Vex. It had ended with a whimper. Rook must have got some sort of signal that Vex had left and the meeting was over had opened the door and quickly ushered him out of the building into the darkness of the alleyway. The only further information he’d got was Rook uttering a single cryptic sentence. “We’ll be in touch, just keep your nose clean”
With that the door had been shut and he had to move fast to get back to the half-way house to beat the curfew. Luckily Neil had made it back easily and Grant was nowhere to be seen when he passed through the lobby, he wasn’t sure he’d had been able to deal with questions about where he’d been without his phone.
The bus pulled up and Neil shuffled on with the rest of the workers waiting and sat in his favourite window seat. He found looking out the windows in the morning seemed to calm him. 
The bus was almost full, but no one had sat next to him, in fact since the meeting he’d mostly sat by himself. It was as if he had some invisible mark on him now that told everyone he was someone to be avoided, it was off putting, but as far as he was aware nobody knew about his meeting. Neil didn’t mind he wasn’t in much of a mood to have company anyway.
At the next stop a man finally took up the seat next to Neil, they nodded at each other and Neil turned back to the window as the bus pulled away on it’s journey to the Rust Belt factories.
The man put his bead back and went to sleep, Neil dosed off looking out the window.
Neil was jolted away by the man sitting next to him elbowing him in the ribs, he had realising he’d fallen asleep. He glanced out of the window and saw they were only minutes from the bus stop at his office. The elbowing had stopped now the man saw Neil was awake. Before he could form any words to ask just what the hell he was doing, the man leaned in to Neil and said “Rook says hello”
Neil was instantly alert now, the mention of Rooks name dumped a jolt of adrenalin through his body dispelling any lingering grogginess. 
He asked the man “Are you a friend of Rooks?” 
The man nodded “You can call me Brask, Rook’s got a job for you, but he’s asked me to work with you on it”
Neil was a bit confused “I’ve got a job, and the deal I had with Vex was a two way street”
Brask looked around to make sure no one was listening in and gestured sharply to Neil to be quiet “Shhh… don’t say that name in public, not ever if you value your life. You’re lucky someone so important has taken an interest in you. From now on you deal only with me ok?” 
It was statement not a question, just like Rook did, Brask came looked fairly unremarkable, but there was a menace to the man that made Neil’s skin crawl.
Brask continued “I know about your deal, you do this one small job first and I’ll show you how you can see your family again” 
Neil wanting this conversation over “Ok, what is it”
Brask handed over a small flash card about the size of a thumbnail, with a handwritten note tightly wrapped around it and said “There’s a job Stratum Projects has worked on and we need to get all the plans for it, especially electrical, data and security. Dump it all on to the flash card, wear your red jumper to work when it’s done and someone will get the card from you. Names on the note, memorise it, destroy the note” Brask said completely flatly in a tone that said he wasn’t going to be repeating himself
Neil took the card and note “I’m not sure if I can access projects I’m not working on.” 
Brask bluntly said “Find a way, or no family reunion, you have three days”
The bus had just pulled up to the stop and Brask sprung to his feet and walked off, thier conversation was over. 
Neil pocketed the note wrapped flash card and took time to calm himself down, he was the last off the bus.
This was the point where he had to produce a result, Neil had made the decision a week ago to do whatever he needed to do see his family. but today it got real. He’d been expecting it, but he hadn’t anticipated the mixture of nerves and excitement at the task ahead.
#
Stratum Projects looked and acted like very much like a normal large commercial builder doing run of the mill large government and corporate projects. Neil hadn’t seen anything in the time he’d been working at there to think it was anything other than what it looked like. Now he was wondering if he’d missed something.
The note Rook gave him only had a project number, no name or any other identifying details. That wasn’t in itself uncommon as most projects were filled under project numbers here, however he didn’t recognise the number format.
With the flash card stashed and the note torn up and thrown away into two different rubbish bins, Neil had made his way up the third floor where his desk was located. It was a large open plan office that took up most of floor, there were windows on one side with views of the vast sprawl of factories and warehouses of the Rust Belt. Not that Neil ever got the chance to look out of them, his supervisors made sure he wasn’t wasting any time, and stopping by window to gaze at the view would be picked up on immediately.
Neil was working in the open plan area with level 4’s and 5’s most of who had been there for years, as fas as he knew he was the only Level 3 on the floor. A rare privilege.
Sitting at his terminal Neil logged-in a looked up his list of tasks for the day, a list he was given every day and was required to complete or he’d be deducted points on his monthly work assessments. A Level 3 Probationary citizen needed to have exemplary assessment scores. 
Today was just like every other day, it was mundane work sorting and collating digital files and scans of manifests and site plans from works sites with as built alterations, he could do it the work without even thinking about it, he’d have plenty of time to search for Rooks data.
Neil worked fast and efficiently for the next few hours breaking the back of his task list, stopping only for a quick break mid morning. Satisfied he’d done enough real work so far Neil waited until lunch was called, it was just a quick chime on his terminal that started a timer, he had thirty minutes to get to the canteen and back for lunch. Punctuality was very important.
No one would question if he stayed a few minutes longer at his terminal, so Neil punched in Rook’s project number into Stratums database search bar.
Nothing came up.
Neil paused and thought hard to make sure he’d remebered the number correctly and typed it in again. Nothing, not even a “no file found” answer, it was just blank as if he never searched.
Undeterred Neil widened his search out the the public building database that he also had access to. This time he got a hit”
“Project 243/402/143 – Nova Dynamics Corporation – Datacentre”.
Nova Corp, Neil remebered that name from his own company five years ago, one of the construction jobs he’d been accused corruption and bribery, and ultimately jailed for, had been for Nova Corp. 
Memories of those dodgy contacts were itching around in the back of Neil’s brain now after being dormant for years. Neil didn’t want to reawaken that part of his brain, he’d spent years working to finally accept his fate, even going so far as to tell himself that he did bear some blame for his own downfall. Seeing the Name Nova Corp there on the screen was painful.
Diving in, Neil clicked through to see the documents but immediately got a warning “Unable to access data – incorrect access level”.
“Damn” thought Neil, he wasn’t sure of how to proceed, he’d assumed it would be easy to just copy the data and be done with it.
At that moment one of his co-workers who’d also stayed back a few minutes came sauntering over to his desk, “hey Neil, lets go eat, I need to break!”
Neil quickly shut down the searches and logged off his terminal, said” Yep, me too let’s go”


#
Neil was much a social person at the best of times, this lunch even more so as he was caught up in his own world thinking how he could get access to the Nova Corp files. At the same time he just couldn’t help shake the idea that seeing the Nova Corp name again wasn’t a coincidence.
He didn’t see the two security guards coming towards him until they were standing right next to him.
“Neil Lucas” one of them said gruffly.
Startled, Neil looked up to see them standing there staring at him expectantly.
“Yes” Neil stammered.
“Please come with us” They didn’t move just kept standing and staring.
Neil looking around could see all the other staff in the canteen also starting at him, he was sure his face was bright red, it certainly felt red hot.
“Now” prompted one of the guards.
Neil stood up and one guard motioned to Neil to follow the other guard and then fell into step behind Neil as he moved away from his half eaten lunch.
“Where are we going Neil ventured”
The guard behind him spoke “You been called to see the Director of security”
“Why?” Neil asked, he knew why.
“Apparently Mr Lucas you’ve been trying to access date outside your status level” said the guard walking behind Neil.
The guard in front clicked his tongue in disgust and said over his shoulder “You shouldn’t have told him that, that’s the Directors job, just keep you’re mouth shit ok”
“Relax, like I’ve said before, it’s not confidential if they’ve told us, beside what’s this guy going to do, run away now?”
“Just keep it shut from now on” the guard in front said as they entered a waiting  lift. The first guard turned away from them to press the floor button as Neil and the second guard followed inside, as they both turned the second guard discretely placed a finger on his grey uniform and tapped for just a moment.
It took Neil a second, but it quickly dawned on him that the guard had just given him a few precious minutes to come up with an excuse as to why he was running searched for Nova Corp. 
Minutes later Neil was marched into the 8th level office of the Security Director, as the name plate on the door told him. The Director was a small man with posture of someone who’d spent too much time in front of a terminal. He didn’t get up but instead addressed Neil immediately from behind his terminal screen.
“Neil, can I call you Neil” he asked.
“Sure” Neil answered, his heart still beating fast.
“Neil, I’ll get to the point, at 12:03pm you started searching for some data that was beyond your access level. According to the logs, you searched both on the company server and then the public database” he referred to his terminal screen “for a Nova Dynamics Corporation, is that correct?”
The few minutes he’d been given had allowed Neil to formulate a strategy here, tell the truth, or at least mostly the truth.
Neil answered “Yes, I did, I didn’t think searching for them was out of my access level until the system warned me”
The Director, satisfied with Neil’s answer said “Good, no point denying it, care to tell me why you were looking up this company?”
Neil opened his hands as a sign of compliance “I’ll be honest, Nova was one of the companies involved in my trial year back, I guess I was just curious to see if they were still around”
“It sounds like you were digging into your old case” the director said “Neil, that is old news, you were convicted and served a full sentence, there is nothing to gain from poking around in the past”
Neil didn’t say anything, the narrative he’d made up was working, it was better to keep quiet now and let it take hold.
Nodding as if Neil’s silence confirmed his theory, the Director continued “Ok Neil, you didn’t access any restricted data, but you did try, so we’ll be keeping an extra eye on you from now on. I’ll be putting a note on your file that we’ve had this discussion, but if I don’t need to talk to you again I’ll delete it before your next review. If I do have to talk to you again it’s not going to be good for you, understand?”
“Yes” Neil said.
“My advice to you is to let your old case and any thoughts of digging around this Nova Corp die, you’ve got a chance a new life now, don’t waste it”
With that he waved at the two guards who had remained in the room for the whole conversation, as if Neil might have attacked the Director “Take him back please”
Neil walked away relieved, but he’d have to be much more careful now, that was too close.










CHAPTER 11

THE DEAL (NEIL 5)

Neil was thankful he’d finally finished work for the day, the hours after his lunchtime run-in with the security director had dragged long. He’d wanted to leave early, crawl back to his sparse apartment in the halfway house and shut out the world, but of course he couldn’t. No only would he be sanctioned for not completing the work day, but there was no way to get back to to the city except on his designated bus.
Not a single co-worker spoke him after lunch, not that he cared much, but yet again he felt that invisible mark, telling others he was not to be associated with, was amplified after being marched out of the canteen.
Neil trudged onto the bus with all the other regular passengers, some he knew by name, some were just nodding acquaintances. He saw Knox sitting in his regular seat by the window. Knox saw him, smiled, waved and gestured for him to sit next to him.
“Sorry, I took your seat, we can swap over if you want?” Knox said as Neil sat down.
Neil didn’t care “No, it’s ok, I haven’t seem you around for a bit” he said.
“They moved me onto a different shift, I’m only on this bus once a week now” Knox responded.
Neil shrugged, Knox had been very helpful to him when he’d first started working at the office right out of jail, but as Neil had found his way he needed Knox’s advice less and less. Neil thought well of Knox and didn’t want to be rude, but this evening he was too preoccupied with other matters to engage in polite conversation.
After a moment Knox spoke again “So, what was that all about at lunch?”
Neil expected the question “Just a misunderstanding, all sorted now”
“From what I heard you were marched out of the canteen by security and taken to the Security Director’s office” Knox pressed “Seems a bit more than a misunderstanding”
News travelled fast, he was the subject of the office gossip now thought Neil “Seems you know more about it than I do” he said.
Knox frowned “Neil, I’m just trying to help here, it’s only been a month or so since you started and you’re getting hauled off by security guards”
Neil sighed, he didn’t want to be mean to Knox, but he also didn’t want to be in this conversation, he said “Sorry, I’m just working through some stuff, nothing you need to worry about”
Knox nodded “I know, it can be tough adjusting to this life, but you have too, otherwise you’ll end up back in jail and then there’s no chance of climbing back”
Neil really didn’t have the energy for this, his frustration was building “Ever since I got out, it’s been: don’t do this or that or you go back to jail, basically be a good little slave or go back to jail, I’ll tell you from where I’m sitting, I’m still in Jail.”
Knox shushed Neil “Careful, if anyone’s phone picks up what you just said you’ll get a language warning”
Neil shook his head “See that’s exactly my point, don’t worry my phone is buried in my pocket here” he tapped his jacket.
Knox, was concerned now, he said “Neil, what’s going on? When we spoke last you were ok. I know you had the warning from trying to meet your family, but something’s changed”
Neil was getting even more frustrated now “Knox, firstly I’ll never stop trying to get my family back, I don’t give shit what some people are telling me, I know my wife and there’s no way she’d abandon me… besides I’m working on a way to see them now”
Know raised his eyebrows “Working on a way? You’ve been warned and you have no idea where they are, what possible way could you have?”
Neil looked directly at Knox now and deliberately said “Knox, I’m making friends now with people who can help me”
Knox paled and shook his head “Neil, don’t tell me it’s Rook”
How did he guess so quickly? “Knox, Rook is misunderstood, but he’s a man who thinks like me, someone who takes action, instead of cowering and complying” Neil said pointedly.
“Neil, you don’t understand, Rook is not your friend and his friends are not your friends, I’ve seen too many people get hurt going down that path” Knox said.
Neil shrugged, he had no response.
Knox pushed the point “I’m deadly serious, those friends will not hesitate to burn you and put you back in jail, or worse, you don’t want to owe them.”
“Thanks for the advice Knox, I’m not playing anyone’s games anymore, I’ll do as I please” Neil said flatly.
“Did you do a deal?” Knox asked simply.
“No deal, they help me and I help them” Neil replied, now really wanting to end this conversation.
“That sounds like a deal, what do you have to do?” Knox asked.
“I’ll handle it Knox. If you don’t like it I’m not forcing you to be my friend, you can keep on accepting this shit, I’ll make my own way” with that Neil stood and moved to an empty seat few rows further back up the bus, He didn’t care if Knox had anything else to say. 
The man in the other seat was asleep, Neil put his head back and closed his eyes, but he didn’t go to sleep. Knox had made him question the deal he’d done with Vex and the Greys. He didn’t see a way to back out now, even if he wanted to, they were expecting results from him and Neil didn’t feel like Vex was someone you wanted to let down.
#
Neil was nervous this morning, he had two more days to deliver the plans to Brask. He had Knox’s words ringing in his ears, these were not people you disappointed without consequences.
His first attempt yesterday at getting the files had been poorly thought out, clumsy and had very nearly got him sent back to jail. Neil resolved to be better, if he was going down this path with Rook and Vex and the whole shadowy Grey organisation, he’d better smarten up quickly. 
Neil had been up most of the night thinking about how he was going to get past the system, an inkling of a plan had formed, but it would require equal parts; nerve, timing and luck. 
Mostly though, he needed some help. The problem was he had no way of contacting Brask or Rook, they just sort of turned up when they needed to talk to him. This morning he was going to take a big risk, he wore his faded red jumper to work, knowing full well that he had nothing for them.
Neil was up and out of his apartment building early so he could stand outside at the bus stop in full view in his red jumper for at least fifteen minutes before the bus arrived.
His message must have been seen. Just as the bus was about to pull away, Brask jumped on. It looked like he had been running, his cheeks were flushed and he was puffing with the effort of getting to the bus stop. Neil grimaced that Brask had been made to run to him for nothing.
Brask sat next to Neil in the now customary empty seat and let out a few big breaths trying to slow his breathing down. Whilst Brask was recovering from his exertion, Neil considered just how quickly Brask had got the message and onto the bus, was Brask, or someone else watching Neil now? That thought worried him.
Brask now mostly recovered, spoke first “So, you’ve got something for me I see? I’m impressed” his held out waiting to receive the flash card.
Neil swallowed, there was no easy way to say it “No, I don’t”
Brask slowly withdrew his hand, his face went from light to dark and his eyes bore into Neil’s with an intensity that scared Neil “What do you mean?” he asked.
Neil was about to answer and Brask waved him to silence. He reached into his pocket and pulled out a small dark grey box, about half the size of a phone, and clicked a button on the side, before putting it back in his pocket.
Brask said pointedly “You were saying?”
What was the box for? thought Neil “Are you recording me? If you are you can tell Vex and whoever else wants to know, I can’t do what they are asking” his anger rising as he said it.
Brask grabbed Neil’s arm and held it menacingly tight, very deliberately and slowly responded “Neil, you’d better start acting smart, right now, you made a deal and I’m here to make sure you deliver, it’s not just your head on the block if you don’t, do you understand me?” The grip tightened.
Neil tried to pull his arm away, but Brask was too strong and they were sitting shoulder to shoulder, there was nowhere to move. 
Brask shook Neil’s arm when he didn’t answer, his face a grimace of anger now “Do you understand?”
Neil felt trapped, even though the bus was full with witnesses. “I understand, I get it, let go of me”
Brask’s grip didn’t loosen “I’m on the hook just as much as you are, if you don’t deliver, then I don’t deliver and I’m not looking to get sanctioned” with that statement Brask let go of Neil’s arm.
The intense stare remained “Now tell me how you’re going to get the files, I don’t want to hear any fucking excuses.”
It was all or nothing now and he didn’t like the sound of being sanctioned either, Neil said “I tried once and almost got caught, I can’t get the files you want from my terminal, they’re well above me grade as a Level 3. Just typing in the project number got me pinged”
Brask had calmed down slightly and asked “You didn’t waste my time this morning to tell me how you can’t do it, you must have an idea have how you can do it?”
Neil answered “Yes, that’s why I needed to talk to you, I know how to get the files, but I need help.” He paused “Is it safe to talk here?”
Brask, patted the pocket where the little grey device was and said “We’re good to talk, nothing will overhear us”
Ok, so not recording, but blocking thought Neil, he’d have to trust Brask on that, he outlaid his plan “There’s a Level 6 on the floor who manages the whole office, not me personally, but he manages the level 5’s who manage the ex-cons. His terminal will have access, but I can’t get to his office without being seen by the security system”
 Neil ploughed on “I thought I saw a sign from one of the security guards who saved my arse yesterday, maybe he is sympathetic to us? Maybe he’s someone who could help with the camera’s in the office?” 
That was it, Neil’s plan as much it it was, if he couldn’t solve the security camera issues he didn’t have a back up.
Brask looked at Neil, not saying anything just pondering what Neil has just said.
Eventually he spoke “I don’t know anything about any security guards. What I do know is your plan is pretty damn flimsy” 
Brask was silent again, so was Neil, his plan looked like it had failed before it had even started.
Brask nodded and finally said “Ok, I’ll see what I can do. Don’t do anything today, I’ll be in touch”
Brask made a show of reaching into his pocket and switching off the blocker, then putting his head back and closing his eyes. Cleary the conversation was over, but Neil felt a small glimmer of hope now, at least the first part of his plan had worked. Now he had to work out how to hack into his boss’s terminal.
#
Neil had spent the day after his brief and unfriendly conversation with Brask doing his best to look small and cowed, like he was suitably embarrassed from the previous day’s ignominy of being led out of the canteen by security. 
He didn’t take his lunch, but instead worked through the break to get a better sense of the ebb and flow his colleagues into and out of the office during the day. It was at that point Neil realised he’d been unconsciously taking mental notes of his colleagues movements for weeks now. It was an old habit from his time in construction, he’d had to keep an eye on his workers coming and going on various job sites so he wasn’t getting ripped off. He was glad it was something he hadn’t forgotten in the years since.
This morning he was expecting Brask to show up on his bus and give him some news, but to Neil’s disappointment no one showed up, neither Brask or Rook sat next to him, the seat remained noticeably empty the whole trip.
Had they decided to cut him loose? What did that mean for his reunion with his family? Telling himself to relax, Neil reasoned that he’d made a pretty unexpected demand, they, whoever they were, would likely take some time to get back him. He guessed it would show just how well connected the Greys were.
Still, there was a part of mind that was telling him he’d blown it, and he couldn’t shake it off. 
Neil got off the bus and paused at the bus stop, perhaps someone would reach out to him here. Standing there as everyone else walked off to thier various factories and offices, looking around, whilst trying not to look like he was looking around, made him feel stupid and far too conspicuous, he reluctantly walked towards his office. The part of his mind that was telling him he’d blown it grew louder.
About half way along the path from the bus stop to his office a group of factory workers rushed past Neil, by the looks of thier uniforms they were from the factory next door and must have been late. Suddenly a hand grabbed the back of Neil’s arm and a voice whispered fast and close in Neil’s ear said “Camera’s will be off at lunch time”.
The grip was released and a man sprinted from behind Neil, he was wearing the same uniform as the factory workers who’d just rushed past. Neil tried to see his face or commit to memory anything about the man, but he never turned back and he quickly reached the group in front and seamlessly merged into the little crowd so the Neil could no longer pick which one he was.
Neil was shocked by the suddenness of the contact, he’d been half expecting another bus trip with Brask or Rook, the fact that this was yet another person who was somehow connected made Neil think the Greys were bigger than he thought.
The message was clear, the cameras in office would be off at lunch time, giving Neil the chance to get into his Manager’s office. Now he just needed to work out how to access the terminal.
#
The hours before lunch dragged on, Neil sat at his desk terminal working in a heightened sense of alertness. He scrutinised every conversation, every look, every movement of the other staff on the floor for signs they were watching him, that they might know what he was about to do.
Neil felt like anything he did or said was going to give him away, which was silly really. He was simply going to walk into the executive office, pull up the files on the terminal and copy them. It was not a complicated task and it would be done in minutes, he might even get some time for lunch afterwards.
After an eternity his terminal chimed for the lunch break, the staff on the floor made thier way out of the office to the canteen. No one gave Neil a second looked when he didn’t get and go with the crowd, no asked him to join them, seems they were all happy for him to stay in the office, Neil felt invisible.
Neil had seen manager leave his office at least fifteen minutes before the lunch break, being a Level 6 out here in the Rust Belt meant being the top of the heap, so there were no timed lunches for him, he could come and go as he pleased.
That was it, the last group had left the office, Neil waited until the lift doors closed on them. “Go Now!” the told himself as started to hesitate.
He stood abruptly up from his desk and walked briskly across the large open plan office, past the now empty desks and terminals, past the little bank of  waist height bookshelves and cupboards that created a corridor between and the main worked and and the glass offices. 
Neil looked the the camera on the ceiling as he walked into the manager’s office, there was no indication if it was working or now, he was taking a leap of faith that no one was watching on the other end.
Neil skirted around the desk and sat at the terminal, it was locked. He knew it would be, most terminals were opened automatically by the authorised users smartphone or watched, so no one used password anymore. Neil, being a Level 3, didn’t get the luxury of smart wearables and had to log in to his terminal manually. The systems tech who showed him how to log-in on his first day, told him it was little hack they’d built just for him as he was the only Level 3 they’d ever had in the office.
Neil just hoped the same hack worked on this terminal.
He keyed in the sequence to open up the manual log-in. Yes! the script worked and the manual log-in prompt appeared. 
Neil typed in the manager’s name, Brentan Hall, in the same format as he typed in his own. 
He typed in the system password he remebered the tech use to activate his terminal back on that first day, he hadn’t meant to look, but the tech took no care to hide it, Neil just hoped it was a universal password.
[User name and password don’t match]
Damn it, did he remember it correctly? Neil very deliberately typed the password again making sure he didn’t make any mistakes.
[User name and password don’t match]
Neil wasn’t sure how many attempts he had, or if he could actually get in at all now. He was starting to sweat with the prospect of failure.
The office was quiet apart from the low hum of the air-conditioning, otherwise Neil wouldn’t have heard the lift quietly chime as it’s doors opened. Brentan Hall stepped out. Shit! 
He quickly exited the log-in prompt and looked up, Brentan was already half way to the office and moving fast. Neil didn’t have time to leave without being seen and had no choice but to duck into the small ensuite attached to the office, he slide the door close just as Brentan stepped in.
He was talking on his phone “Yep, I’m in my office now, give me two minutes, the boss is riding me to get this report sent” He sat at the desk and clicked through his emails looking for something.
“Don’t you leave without me you arseholes! Remember who’s paying for the first round!” He said all to loudly in the empty office, Neil only meters away in the ensuite held his breath.
“Here it is, these fucking criminals I have to babysit finally did something right… and it’s sent, no need to come back here today! I’ll be back down there in sixty seconds”
Neil could hear Brentan exit the office just as loudly and quickly as he entered, after a few second he snuck the ensuite door open and saw Brentan entering the lift, still on his phone.
Neil looked back into the office, Brentan hadn’t logged off his terminal, he’s just walked out assuming, as most people would, the terminal would automatically log-him off in a minute or two.
What a stroke of luck, Neil rushed to the terminal to stop it from shutting down. His heart was racing at the near miss, but the terminal was open with full access, he quickly opened up the file database and entered in the project number.
This time the search came back with the full project listing and more importantly, no warnings, even though Neil was still half expecting an alarm to go off somewhere.
[Project 243/402/143 – Nova Dynamics Corporation – Datacentre]
[Open project folder? Y/N]
Neil paused, then deliberately pressed Yes, he looked around the office, it was still empty.
The project folder opened, inside were multiple subsidiary folders, he scrolled through to find the floor plans and the electrical and data schematics. Jackpot! They were all there.
He placed the flash card on the screen and dragged all of the files over to it to copy them across. Neil checked the clock on the terminal, it had been more than twenty minutes since lunch was called, he needed to get out of this office.
It took only a few seconds, data transfers were normally blindingly fast these days and all the files had successfully copied. Neil pocketed the card, logged out of the database and bolted out of the office. 
He didn’t go back to his desk, but headed over to the bathroom, he needed to wash the sheen of sweat off his face that he hadn’t noticed had built up whilst he was hiding out in the ensuite. He also needed to a private place to take some big deep breaths away from the office. Neil, stood in the empty bathroom, looking at himself in the mirror, he’d splashed some cold water onto his face try and calm himself down, he’d go back to the office when his hands stopped shaking.
Ten minutes later Neil was sitting comfortably back at his desk when the first of his colleagues started trickling back from lunch. Just as they had when they left, they ignored Neil who looked like he just been siting there working the whole time. 
He spent the rest of the day expecting security to come in and haul him off again, steeling himself for the inevitable every time the lift doors chimed open, but it never happened. The Grey’s were as good as thier word, a fact which was both exciting and terrifying.

#
Neil didn’t think it was possible to sleep at all that night, the buzz of his successful little piece of industrial espionage, and the prospect of getting to meet his family once he delivered the data to the Greys had Neil amped up with excitement by the time he got back to his simple apartment.
His body however had other ideas and the adrenaline dump from almost being caught in the office drained him of his energy and he quickly passed out and slept a deep sleep.
The next morning Neil allowed himself to be excited for the first time since he was released from jail, there might be a path out of this. Through he told himself not to get to far ahead as he’d been disappointed enough already. Regardless, Neil stepped out to the bus stop in front of his halfway house in the same faded red jumper he wore yesterday with a little bit of a spring in his step. Today he had something to deliver.
Brask was already standing at the stop, in fact he was the only one there as Neil was again about fifteen minutes early for the bus. How long had he been out there wondered Neil. 
He nodded to Neil and put his hand our as if to shake Neil’s as he approached him, Neil understood what what going on immediately and slipped the flash card into his hand and slapped it into Brask’s palm as they shook hands. 
Brask seamlessly took the card from the handshake and then just kept walking down the street, the only words exchanged between the pair was Brask whispering “We’ll be in touch” as he passed Neil.
Neil empty-handed now turned and watched Brask swiftly moving down the street and before Neil could think about chasing after him, Brask just as swiftly disappeared around the corner. 
Once again Neil was shocked, this was not how he expected this to go, he wanted to know how they were going to hold up thier end of the bargain, but he resolved to be patient this time.
The other workers were filing to the bus stop now, Neil tried attempted to blend back into the crowd of half awake bleary eyed workers despite being very much wide awake.






CHAPTER 12

REUNION (NEIL 6)

It had been three agonising days of waiting for the Greys to contact him with a way to see his family again. Neil was just about at the end of this newly found patience, frankly he was sick of this one way communications deal in general. He’d made a deal with Vex and he’d put himself at great risk to get the data they wanted, these Greys had better hold up thier end of the bargain too. 
 Neil wasn’t actually sure what how they were going to contact him, it seems like they took pleasure in reaching out in different ways each time they wanted to talk to him. He‘d been on edge waiting for contact every time he left his meagre apartment, was it going to be at the bus stop, on the bus, at the office, he didn’t know how or when it was going to happen, he just had to be prepared for anything.
He wasn’t prepared for the soft knock on his door. He looked at the clock on the wall, it read “6:01am” he wasn’t late and no one had ever knocked on his door in the time he’d lived here. It was kind of a unwritten rule, you didn’t disturb people here.
He tentatively walked to the door and asked “Who is it, I’ll be out on time”
There was another knock, more insistent but still quiet.
“Grant is that you? just come on it if it is” Neil said, he was getting a little nervous now.
The voice on the other side simply said “Fucking let me in Neil.”
He knew that voice and opened the door to see Brask standing outside. He didn’t wait to get invited in and rushed past Neil “Close the door” he said.
Neil closed the door and just stood there starting at Brask, it was very disconcerting to see Brask in his standing in his apartment. The man had a menace to him that Neil had never got comfortable with, having him standing there inside the place he lived chilled Neil.
Brask sat down at the small dining table and gestured for Neil to sit too, which he did reluctantly. Brask reached into his pocket and pulled out the same small dark grey box he’d used on the bus a few days ago. He left it on the table in between them.
Brask pointed to it and said “This stops any phone or mic within ten meters from listening into conversation, this is yours He pushed it over to Neil. 
“From now on you keep this on you and make sure it’s activated anytime you’re saying something you shouldn’t be saying. If we’re talking assume I’m using mine. Don’t show it to anyone, ever” Brask paused and looked directly at Neil.
Neil realised Brask was waiting for an answer and he said “ Yes… ok…”
Brask nodded and continued “Make sure of it, if you’re caught with it, going back to jail is the least of your problems… Oh, and if you’re thinking why don’t I just keep it on all the time? Don’t do that, when it’s on keep the conversations short, a small short local outage of connected devices will be ignored as a glitch, but any longer than ten or so minutes will alert the system” 
Brask paused to let that sink in with Neil and them asked “Any questions?”
Neil answered “Why do I get this now?”
“You get this now, because you delivered big time. We checked the data you got us, it was a fucking gold mine and means we can do things we never though of doing before. Some important people are very happy, and against my advice want to read you in on another project.” Brask said.
Neil was actually excited by that prospect, they were so many questions he wanted to ask, but it came down to just one thing he needed to know. “Ok, so what about my family?” he asked.
Brask smiled wryly “We always honour our deals, the other reason you’re getting that is you’ll need it today” Brask said.
Neil was confused and blurted out “Listen I’m not doing another job until…”
Brask cut him off “Relax Neil, it’s no job, you’re meeting your family today”
Neil managed to stammer “What… today… how?”
Brask was enjoyed this too much “Today you’re sick and can’t make it into the office, the office is aware. We’ll wait here until everyone has left the building then we’ll head straight to your little family reunion.”
“What about Grant?” Neil asked
“You’ll find he has an appointment out of the building all day today. As far as he’s concerned you’ve been up here in bed sick all day.”
The enormity of what Brask had said was just hitting Neil. He couldn’t believe after all these years he was finally going to see his wife and daughter again. He didn’t think it would be so immediate, the speed of what was happening had Neil’s head spinning, he had to put his hands on the table to stop from falling out of his chair.
Brask on the other hand was sitting across from Neil calmly watching the emotions rolled through Neil. After a little while as he let Neil sit with his thoughts he stood up and walked the few steps over to the small kitchenette and asked “You got any Coffee in this shithole?”
#
For the next hour or so Neil felt like a caged animal as he and Brask sat in the lounge of his small apartment waiting for the building to empty out.
Neil tried to start a conversation with Brask after he’d made a second coffee from Neil’s supplies.
“How long do we need to wait?” Neil asked.
Brask looked at his watch “Soon enough, I’l tell you when it’s time, no need to ask” 
Neil had so many questions ”Where are we going?”
Brask, deliberately put his cup down on the table “Stop asking questions, you’ll leave when we’re ready, and you’ll go where we take you” he picked up the coffee and took another sip and grimaced “This really is shit, do me a favour and next time buy something better”
“I didn’t expect company this morning” Neil said “I can’t just keep sitting here, should we wait outside?”
Brask closed his eyes and sipped the coffee again, he leaned back in the chair and said “Listen, I didn’t come here to have deep and meaningful conversation with you, so shut the fuck up and stop asking me questions”.
Neil heard the menace creep into Brask’s answer and decided not to push it, so he sat there quietly. He slowly lost himself in thought, playing in his mind over and over again what he’d say when he saw his family again.
Half an hour later Neil was daydreaming and Brask was dosing, the chime from Brask’s phone broke the silence. He was alert immediately and checked the message.
“Ok, time for you to go, there’s car waiting for you around the back” Brask said.
Finally! Neil got up to leave, but Brask stayed where he was “You coming?” Neil asked.
Brask shook his head “Do I look like a fucking driver? This next bit is only for you, don’t screw it up, whatever instructions you get, you follow them to the absolute letter.”
Neil hesitated, the thought of Brask staying in his apartment, as much as it was, whilst he wasn’t here did not fill Neil with a great deal of joy. 
“Are you staying here?” Neil asked
“It’s got to look like someone’s here all day doesn’t it? Now piss off, the car’s waiting” Brask pointed to the door.
Neil hesitated for only a second and then left Brask sitting at his table.
#
Neil rushed downstairs, Brask was right, the building was empty, but that wouldn’t last long, the night shift workers would be coming back soon. He was glad to see Grant’s office was empty, this was a huge step outside the rules and he didn’t think Grant was the kind of person who was going to looked past it.
Neil went out through the rec room and the laundry to the back exit where the rubbish was kept. 
There was a nondescript white EV waiting, it was nothing fancy and certainly didn’t look out of place out here in the Outer. Neil approached the car and the passenger side door opened, he tried to see who was in the car before he got in, but the tinting had been turn up to full.
Neil hesitantly got into the car, he was greeted by a dark haired woman sitting behind the wheel.
“Hi Neil, big day for you” she said.
Neil recognised that voice, but not the face, last time they spoke all he could see was as black silhouette as bright lights had blinded him.
“Vex” Neil ventured.
“At your service, now please close the door so we can get going” Vex said as Neil realised he’d been sitting there with the door open, perhaps subconsciously leaving an escape exit if he needed it.
He closed the door, a bit shocked Vex was the driver today, he’d expected another menacing Grey to do the job. 
Vex drove off and didn’t say anything. Neil saw the car’s console was black, no graphics, no controls, just completely blank with one flashing word in the top right hand corner: [operating]. 
Vex saw what he was looking at and gestured to the screen with one had, the other remained on the steering wheel and said “We’re pretty proud of that, it took quite some time and effort to develop that, we almost had to rebuild the car to get it to work though and of course it’s manual drive only now”
Neil realised that Vex had actually been driving the whole time, for someone who was used to auto driving for most of this life, he wasn’t all that comfortable at the thought of a person manually driving.
Vex could see Neil’s discomfort and laughed “Don’t worry, I’m actually a pretty good driver, I thought I could do with the practice today.”
Neil was once again in a situation he didn’t expect, but he was starting to get the idea that was their aim. Trying to be as nonchalant as he could be about Vex driving the car, he asked “Where are we going?”
“Not far actually, your daughter has an appointment with her doctor…”
Neil immediately interrupted “What? What’s wrong?”
“Don’t worry nothing’s wrong, it’s just a routine check up, she’s healthy and your wife’s healthy” said Vex not looking at Neil, but concentrating on the road.
Good, thought Neil, but this was still very odd and he couldn’t quite make sense of it “So I’m seeing them after the appointment?”
“No, you’ll be meeting them in the Doctor’s office. Neil officially they don’t want to see you, so we had to find a way to get you together without any eyeballs or cameras spotting you, after all right now you’re in bed sick” Vex said.
“They will want to see me, I know my family better than anyone” Neil said.
Vex simply said “We don’t know if that’s true or not, we couldn’t find any evidence they’ve tried to reach you in all these years, even after you got released, either way you’ll know the answer soon enough”
Neil was always so confident that his wife wouldn’t have abandoned him, he was always so sure there was good reason he hadn’t heard from her in all this time. He’d stuck to the though that perhaps she;’d tired but had been prevented from doing so. Vex’s snippet of information cut him deeply, what if it was true, that she really wanted nothing to do with him? The thought was too hard to bear.
“Do they even know I’m coming?” asked Neil.
Vex paused before she answered “No, they don’t know, best we could do is find this appointment in her schedule and change the doctor to a more sympathetic friend of ours.”
Neil didn’t actually care about the details og how they’d done it, he just wanted to get there and prove that he was right, he sat silently in the car trying not to let the doubts take over.
After a few minutes of silence, Vex spoke “So, that data you got for us, nice job. It’s going help us a hell of lot. I think you might have a future with us actually”
Neil wasn’t particularly interested in that conversation right now. “Ok, sure, lets just get me to my family and we can talk later”
They had driven from his halfway house right in the middle of the Outer through to the loose transition into the Green. Vex pulled into the underground carpark of a large public medical centre and parked in a spot well away from the lift. The lights in this section weren’t working creating a dark oasis within the brightly light carpark.
Vex turned to Neil, no more of the chatty demeanour, she was deadly serious, her eyes cold and dark “You have fifteen minutes, not a minute more. If you’re one second late I’m leaving, get your own way home, understood?”
Neil nodded, he didn’t have his phone, his cards, or any way to get back to his apartment, so being late was going to be an option.
Vex pointed the to the opposite side of the carpark from the public lift “Over there, see the service life, you take that one, there’s a nurse waiting for you and she’ll take you where you need to go. Time starts now.”
#
The nurse didn’t say a word to Neil, as soon as he entered the lift she pressed a finger to her lips for Neil to stay silent and she pushed the button for the 5th floor. Thankfully the lift was fast here, unlike Neil’s apartment building back in the Outer which almost seemed like you needed an invitation to use. His keen eye for construction, honed over years of contracting out lift installs, immediately recognised the make and model of the lift. It was a middle of the road product he thought to himself, he never would have installed this model, there were lots of better options out there. He wondered what this so-called Doctor was like if he was leasing in a budget building like this one, and why his wife was coming here of all places.
He didn’t have to wait long to find out, moments later the doors opened and the nurse beckoned for Neil to follow. He walked silently behind her as she entered into the back of the Doctor’s office suite from the service corridor. They walked past a small kitchenette, a storage a room and some admin offices, they weren’t empty, but no one looked up to see Neil and the nurse pass. 
The nurse stopped and pointed to a door which had a plaque that said “Consultation Room 3” and gestured for Neil to go inside.
This was it, Neil thought, he paused for a moment to take a deep breath and collect himself, he had to make this count. He opened the door and stepped into the room.
It was a Doctor’s consulting room that was not in doubt, but it was empty.
“What the fuck” thought Neil, did they bring me all this way to just screw with me.
Neil closed the door and sat in the Doctor’s chair, he resolved to be patient for at least a minute or two. He reached into his pocket and clicked on the blocker.
The door opened and his wife carrying his now six year old daughter walked in, they didn’t see him at first, his wife was holding a conversation about the stuffed Tiger she was holding.
“… yes I’m sure she knows the tigers at the zoo, they are all friends…”
Neil watched from the chair as his wife turned her gaze into the room and stopped on him. They both froze, his daughter was oblivious and still concerned with the stuffed Tiger.
All of a sudden she said “Neil? is that you? what are you doing here? how is this even possible?” words started tumbling out of her mouth. She put down thier daughter and slowly moved towards Neil as if he was a ghost that would disappear if she moved too fast.
Neil stood up nervously and said “Jen, it’s me, I’m here” 
Jen rushed to Neil and and jumped onto him embracing him with everything she had, tears flowed freely form both of them.
Jen still squeezing as tight as she could sobbed “I never thought I’d see you again!”
Neil through his own tears said “I didn’t think I would either”
Jen suddenly released the embrace and gave Neil a shove “Wait… they told me you didn’t want to see us”
Neil was shocked and responded “What are you talking about?”
Jen wiped away tears and said “They told us there was no visitation the whole time you were in jail, every time I asked they told me not to ask”
Neil could feel his anger rising now “That’s absolute bullshit...”
Jen punched him in the chest and looked over to thier daughter “Watch it” she warned. 
Thier daughter was standing there just staring at them, when Jen looked at her she ran to her and hid behind her peering at Neil around Jen’s legs.
Neil felt the tears flow again as he took in his daughter face for the first time in over five years, she’d grown so much.
Neil knelt down tentatively to get closer to her, but it was clear she didn’t recognise him. Neil was heartbroken at the realisation, the anger flared, these people had robbed him of so much.
Jen saw it too, she brought thier daughter around to her side and knelt down also and spoke to her “Clara honey, this is your…”
Neil reached out and touched Jen on the shoulder, she looked up and he shook his head.
Jen spoke again “Clara this is a very good friend of mine, Neil, be polite and say hello”
Clara shook here head and buried into Jens side, hiding away from Neil.
Jen said again “be polite Clara, Neil’s a good friend, he’s known you since you were born”
Clara stayed buried into Jen’s side.
Neil stood up and simply said “Let’s not push it”
Jen nodded and said the Clara, “Honey can you go over to the couch and play with Tiger, Neil and I want to chat”
Clara nodded and scooted over to the couch happy to be away from the emotional adults. Neil watched her with a wistful smile.
Jen spoke “How are you even here? I don’t understand?”
Neil answered “It’s a long story, and I only have a few minutes so I need to get this out ok” 
Jen nodded.
Taking a deep breath Neil spoke quickly “I’m in a halfway house in the Outer, they’ve got me there for the next year and I can’t leave until I build up enough points to get up out of Level 3. I’ve got a job in the Rust Belt, it’s an office job in construction, but it’s barely more than slave wages. I’m on a curfew seven days a week and my movements are monitored. They told me you severed contact with me as soon as I went to Jail, I didn’t believe them. I even went to our old house to find you when I got out but someone else was living there” Neil had run out of words to say and fell silent.
Jen took all this in for a few moments and finally responded taking his hand in hers “Oh Neil, I never stopped trying to reach you, they told ne the same thing, you wanted nothing to do with us, I didn’t believe it either”
Neil could feel his nerves subsiding, he was right, they had both been lied to, he asked “What about the house?”
“We had to sell up as soon as you got sent to jail, I got sent down to Level 5 and served a year as a restricted Citizen. No one wanted to talk to us, so we left it all behind and started new out here.” Jen said with sadness.
Neil made sure Jen couldn;t see the seething anger that was rising in him, it was bad enough he got sent to jail, but the slap Jen with a Restricted Citizen was somehow even worse. 
Neil had an idea and said “Write down your new address for me” he grabbed a note pad off the desk and handed it to Jen.
A knock on the door.
