It was close enough so someone could get to it from the city centre in a matter of minutes, but not so close as to be part of the hustle of the central business district. It made Jim wish he’d though of something like it, he might not be where he was now if he had a place to hide out when the shit hit the fan. It location made Jim wonder why Rob came all the out to the Reach to talk to him.
The main room was barely furnished, a cheap couch was the only concession to comfort, there were no other chairs, no tables, no pictures. The curtains were drawn in the main room and Jim pushed one of aside to see the view, he coudn’t see out though. the window was covered in an opaque film.
Rob saw him looking and said “The film blocks most radio waves and all peeping eyes. I’m going to grab a shower, there a little bit of food in the cupboard, help yourself.” 
That’s when Jim realised what else was missing, there was no fridge, just a space where one would be. There was no kettle or toaster either, this was no a place to stop and have a meal.
He opened the cupboards and found a few bottles of water and some dry snacks, he helped himself and sat down in the couch. At least there was a good sized TV, so he flicked it on.
The news was set at the default feed and he could tile after tile showing his face, it looked like every channel was cover his story right now. That’s exactly what he’d planned. He clicked on the Channel 7 news feed.
“… and that’s the latest we have on the breaking update about executive turned fugitive Jim Dalton. After five days in hiding, Dalton resurfaces with a shocking new video accusing Nova Corp and Nova Corp CEO Shane Eldridge of manufacturing the incident at the exclusive Level 8 restaurant Apex. Eldridge has not responded to requests for comment directly, but a Nova spokesperson has said that the action of Rob Hughes actions were not planned or authorised by Nova and they are investigating the incident themselves and that Hughes has been terminated effective immediately. We’ll bring you more on this breaking story as it comes to hand…”
“And that’s how they fuck you completely” Rob said. 
Jim jumped, hadn’t heard Rob come into the room. He’d changed into a new set of clothes, what looked like a brand new set, they still had the creases from being folded in thier packaging on them. His eyes were still half closed due to swelling but he could look Jim in the eyes.
“Not fun is it what someone fucks you over” spat out Jim, he didn’t have much sympathy for Rob.
He wasn’t happy at all being stuck in this empty apartment with Rob, but Jim was learning that pragmatism and patience was sometimes the better policy.
Rob was rummaging through the kitchen for some food, finding a bag of trail mix, he turned around and leaned against the kitchen bench and looked at Jim.
I took a second to for Jim to realise Rob was waiting for him to speak, he didn’t need an invitation, the questions had been in his mind from the day the first video hit the net and the list had grown since then.
In reality, there was only one question he needed an answer to.
 “Why? Why did you set me up? You fucking ruined my life you arsehole” Jim said with an anger that surprised him.
Rob seemed to know the question was coming “Jim it really wasn’t about you, it’s not even about me, actually it’s not about any one person at all, there’s a much bigger game at play…”
Jim interrupted “I don’t give a shit about your big picture, I’m a wanted fugitive, I’ve lost my wife, my house, everything I worked for over the last twenty years, seems pretty fucking personal to me.”
Rob let Jim have his say, when Jim was done he put his hands up and said “Jim, I get it, I really do, especially right now, but you’ll never understand why, until you know the bigger picture”
“Go on then, explain it to me like I’m a five year old” Jim said cynically.
Rob turned around and splashed some cold water on his face, he opened a drawer under the counter. It was full of medical supplies and he picked out some painkillers, gulped a mouthful of water and swallowed three on them in one hit.
“Ok Jim, and this is important, what I‘m going to tell you will get both you and killed if you tell the wrong people, be very careful who you give this information to.” Rob said very deliberately.
“Sure thing” said Jim and shrugged his shoulders, the threat of getting killed was wearing thin now. Did he believe Rob, sure, did it matter to Jim right now, no.
Rob saw the shrug, Jim could see him calculate his response for a split second, like he’d decided to go on regardless, he continued.
“Jim, you’re just a pawn in a bigger game played by Nova Corp and it’s rivals. They use people like you to push and prod at each other, always trying to gain an advantage, to gain power over each other” Rob explained.
Jim sat in stony silence, the story Rob was telling him sound true, but it didn’t explain to why he was chosen. He asked “Ok, so big business runs the place, not something I didn’t already know, you still haven’t told me why you picked me to screw over.”
Rob’s face wore that calculating look again for a split second, it occurred to Jim that if not for the injury and fatigue Rob was carrying, he might never had caught those glimpses of the mask slipping.
“Up until a few hours ago, I ran the Special Projects Division at Nova, Jim you personally were one of those, project 29 to be exact.” said Rob.
“What do you mean I was a project?” asked Jim genuinely confused now.
Rob leaned forward and said “Jim, Nova Corp mines every test, every citizen does, ever. From primary school, to university, to corporate psychometric tests, nominally it’s to find the best and brightest to come and work for them. 
“The truth is we are looking for people we can mould to our will, to find hooks we can manipulate to get people to do what we want. You were flagged as someone who had very high resilience, off the charts almost, and strong will.”
“Combine that with the street smarts of growing up in as a Level 5 in the Outer, and Nova has been watching you since you were ten years old.”
Jim just didn’t have the bandwidth to be shocked or angry anymore, he’d just been told that his whole life now was one long manipulation, how much of it was real at all?
Jim was suddenly very tired “So what was the point of destroying my life?” he asked.
“I don’t know you’d have to ask Shane. Only he knows what he wanted. If I’d have to guess, you were used to create a news story to distract us from something else he was doing. What that might be, I don’t know.” Rob shrugged his shoulders.
“So I was just an expendable prop to be discarded?” Jim asked incredulous at the cold impersonality of it all.
“You’ve go to understand these people Jim, these CEO’s, they play a game we have no idea about. They don’t just control the system, they are outside the system… we are like ants to them.” said Rob, his swollen eyes now were starting to turn all sorts of colours.
“I’ve got to got down for a few hours, I can see of think anymore”
Jim could tell Rob was holding something back, dealing out just enough information to keep him hooked, but he wasn’t going to get more out of him tonight.
“I’ll take the bed you take the couch and we’ll work out what to do in the morning.” said Rob.
Jim, was still processing everything he’d heard, he knew need rest, perhaps a few hours would help him decide how to react and what to do. He just nodded and Rob went into the bed room and lay on the bed, there were no sheets, just a mattress, but that didn’t matter to Rob at all.
Within seconds Rob was asleep leaving Jim to sit in the quiet and think, not long after though he succumbed to the need to rest too.

#
Jim sleep awkwardly on the couch, his mind was subconsciously churning over what Rob had told him the day before. His dreams were disruptive and full of doom, not that he could remember them now that he was awake. He’s woken up early, or so he thought, the blind and the film on the windows blocking any light from outside. When he flicked on the TV, muting the volume so and not to disturb Rob, who needed the sleep more than Jim did, he was surprised to see it was a little after lunch. He didn’t feel like he’d slept so long, his body now cold and stiff, the throbbing pain in his ribs came flowing back now the he was awake and sitting up. He’d be surprised if John hadn’t broken a rib or two.
The news feed was more of the same, still the breathless analysis of his video across multiple channels, but nothing new. The heat was already dying down though, as local accidents, government scandals and celebrity gossip pushed the new cycle inexorably forward.
Jim heard Rob stirring in the other room and waited in silence for him to surface. when his did Jim could see that whilst his eyes and nose were a lot less swollen, half of his face was now turning a deep purple.
Without a weary glance and nod at Jim, Rob raided the drugs drawer and gulped down more painkillers. He opened a few of the wall cabinets and grabbed two Nova Corp branded mugs, they were only ones in the cupboard. From another cabinet he grabbed two sachets of instant coffee, ripped them open and poured them into the mugs. Without a kettle, Rob just simply ran the hot water and filled the cups to the brim.
He handed on of the cups to Jim along with the box of painkillers and sat down on the floor opposite Jim, he back leaning against the wall. Jim took both gladly.
Rob spoke first “How are you holding up this morning?” he asked.
“Battered and bruised, but I’ll survive” answered Jim.
There are a silence as the they sipped the coffee, it was terrible, but it was warm and the caffeine was real.
Jim broke the silence “So what are our options here?”
Rob thought for a second “The way I see it we have three options, one, we just get out of the city, head east or west, it doesn’t matter. The cities out there are not quite as connected as here in the Capital. The CEB and Nova will follow us eventually, but we’ve got a good chance of hiding out for a while.”
Jim shook his head “I’m not leaving my city for some toxic shit hole out, you can barely call them cities anyway, what are the other options?”
Rob shrugged “We can probably safely hide out in here for a few more days until I can set up something more permanent. Nova will have a team scouring everything I’ve done for the last ten years, they find this place eventually.”
Jim shook his head again “That doesn’t seem like much of a plan, wait for either the CEB or Nova to finds us, not doing that.” 
Jim paused, he looked directly at Rob and said “I know you didn’t risk your life to come and rescue me from the bunker when you could have just come straight here and left me to John. You’ve got a plan, you’ve always had one, stop buttering me up and just tell me.”
Rob chuckled and looked Jim up and down as if appraising him all over again “You’re getting better at this, there’s that resilience and intelligence shining through. Sure I’ve got a plan, you don’t get to where I got without running contingencies on contingencies. Right now my plan is really simple; the enemy of my enemy is my friend.”
Jim just stared at him, what did he actually man? Then it dawned on him “Are you fucking crazy? The Greys? I didn’t exactly leave them on the best terms, if they find me they’ll likely kill me, and they’ll certainly kill you. You don’t know Vex, she’s not one to fuck around when you cross her.
Rob leaned forward “Jim you still don’t really understand the game, it’s not about loyalty, it’s about leverage. You and I represent the single biggest defections that the Greys could ever dream of. Trust me on this, if they are as smart as I think they are, they’ll know it too.”
Jim didn’t think fro a second that he personally was some of sort of golden egg that Vex would want back, but he could absolutely believe Rob was. 
“Ok, say we do this, what’s to stop you selling me down the river to save yourself?” Jim asked.
“Nothing, you shouldn’t trust me, you shouldn’t trust anyone. What I will say is that video you released took the heat off them and dumped Nova in the shit big time, I suggest you leverage that.” Rob said.
Jim contemplated all his options, he hated to admit it, but Rob’s idea was the only where they didn’t get killed by Nova eventually. The Greys may still kill them, but it wasn’t a certainty. He knew Rob had his own agenda, and he was going to do his best to uncover it, but Jim wasn’t going to sit around in the apartment any longer.
“You need to get a car, not a taxi this time, an untraceable private car that won’t stand out, no matter where we go, can you do that?” Jim asked.
Rob smiled a wry smile “There’s one downstairs, ready to go”
Of course there was thought Jim.
“Then lets get the hell out of here” said Jim.
























CHAPTER 21

ENEMY OF MY ENEMY (JIM, & ROB)

Jim was impressed at the lengths Rob had gone to planning his secret bolt hole. The apartment, while not a place one could live in, it was fully stocked with all the things you could want if you were planning to escape and go off grid. They prepared themselves as much as they could for what they knew would be a dangerous game they were entering. 
Jim didn’t know how long it would take to find the Greys, but if his hunch was correct they would be out looking for him, along with Nova Corp agents and the CEB. It was a matter of being found by the least worst group first, the consequences of getting it wrong were deadly.
Both of them pocketed a slab of painkillers each, and after eating an unsatisfying meal of dry snacks, mostly stale VatSoy crisps, chased down by more of the awful frozen coffee they made the move.
Jim hesitated at the door of the apartment.
“What about the cameras in the building, are you bringing that camera blinder?” He asked.
“No, the blinder is single use, it send out enough UV waves to fry itself eventually. Don’t worry about the cameras in here, I took care of them ages ago, they don’t record and they aren’t connected to the net. We’re good until we get outside, then its only a matter of time before we’re picked-up.” Rob said as they walked out into the corridor, he carefully wiped down the handle and door to erase and trace of them on the outside of the apartment. 
One way or the other they were getting found thought Jim, he felt a clock start ticking somewhere in the back if his mind. 
“We’ve got to get found by the Greys quickly, it would help if you told me where were going?” said Rob.
“I don’t know the exact address, but I’ll know where it is when we get close. You drive and I’ll direct you.” answered Jim, leverage he thought.
Half way down the corridor to the lift, Rob took the cloth he wiped the door with and the door key and dumped them both down the garbage chute.
“We won’t be back” he said matter of factly.
A growing sense of unease was now settling on Jim, it felt like they were jumping off a ship in the middle of the ocean. The next few hours would determined if they drowned or not.
#
The car Rob had stashed in the basement carpark turned out to be an old diesel van. It looked like it had been some sort of delivery van in its previous life, but that was clearly many years ago. 
When Jim saw it he scoffed. “That old thing, it looks like it’ll break down after a block”
“Jim you’ve got to start thinking more clearly if you want to survive.” Rob said as they got in. 
“This old diesel is basically untraceable, if they do find use they can’t shut it down either, see, no console. Just a good old analogue machine, it took me ages to find one like this that still worked.”
“Well as long as it gets us out to the Scar territory” said Jim.
“The Scar Territory, I should have guessed” Rob nodded and they drove out of the building.
It was roughly a forty-five minute drive to get out to the Scar Territory, but they were at thier most vulnerable leaving the city centre. There were so many more cameras and people in the densely packed and bustling city centre. Jim was sure he felt the eyes of every other driver, passenger and pedestrian staring right at him. Any moment now someone would sound the alarm that a fugitive was driving in a battered old  through the city. A month ago he wouldn’t have hesitated, being the first to notify of a fugitive on the loose would have earn’t him a bucket load of points for being a civic minded citizen.
They drove in silence for the next fifteen or so minutes, Rob full of concentration manually driving the van through the bust streets, he wasn’t following any map route, just moving in the right direction.
Jim breathed a sigh of relief as the city faded away into the leafy affluent suburbs of the Green that surrounded the city like a well manicured moat. 
Rob spoke up “Are we going north or south of the bridge.”
“Go north of it, do a few loops around the outer before we get there, let’s see if we can get the Greys interest.” said Jim.
“Do you have a place in mind after that?” Rob asked.
“Were heading to a safe house a only few blocks from the Scar well into the abandoned zone”
Rob nodded in acknowledgement.
About twenty minutes later a nervous silence had settled inside the Van, both men caught in thier own thoughts. Rob had just driven a meandering circuity around the Outer, driving past as many shops and markets as he could find.
He broke the quiet of the van “We’ve picked up a friend I think, take a look.”
Jim got out of his and moved into back of the van to look through one of the openings in the paint covering them.
“Green sedan?” He called up to Rob as he saw the old green sedan about six cars back follow them around a right hand turn.
“I think so” Rob said as he executed series of random turns, 
“Yep still there” called Jim from the back as the green car stayed behind them, but didn’t get any closer.
Jim was about to go back to his seat when he saw another car that seemed to be shadowing the green sedan. This was car however, was a high end white SUV that was definitively not the Greys.
He jumped back into his seat and told Rob “I think we’ve picked up a second tail.”
Rob looked back through the side mirror and saw the white SUV. “Shit, that’s definitely Nova, I know those cars”
“What do we do?” Rob was getting anxious.
“We get the hell out of here” Rob floored the accelerator and the old van lurched forward, for the next few minutes Rob headed in straight line directly to the Scar territory. Jim held on as he sped though intersections and stop signs and weaved in an out of traffic, the old van felt like it was going to tip over any moment now.
The green was white cars followed suit, any pretence of hanging back was discarded.
“We’re never going to lose them in this old thing” Jim said, his eyes glued to the mirrors.
“No we won’t, and this won’t last much longer” He could see the engine temp gauge was stuck firmly in the red.
“There!” Jim yelled as pointed to a ramshackle street market a few blocks ahead of them.
“Got it, be ready to jump and sprint, I’m going to be in your hip pocket, you need to lead the way here, this is your realm.” Rob barked back, sweat now dripping down his face form man handling the van.
The market came up quickly, Rob slowed down with the traffic and then violently ripped the van down an alley just next to the mains street of markets. They both jumped out and spirited, Rob hadn’t turned the van off or put the brakes on. It stopped only when it veered off track and slid screeching its metal side down the walls of he alley, the bricks finally dragging it to a stop. The engine gave up and overheated, releasing a torrent of hot steam creating an instant dirty vapour cloud that obscured Rob and Jim from the entrance they just drove in.
Thankfully this alley connected to others and lead them deep into the market, Jim and Rob kept running, not looking back.

#
Jim slowed, his ribs were aching and he was out of breath badly, he just couldn’t keep running. Rob was right behind him, breathing heavily too, his nose starting to ooze blood, and his eyes were black and purple. It was a far cry from the arrogant and immaculately dressed Level 9 executive Jim had met at the Apex restaurant what seemed like a lifetime ago.
Jim had to stop and get his bearings, he wasn’t exactly sure where they were. This market, like many others that had organically grown up on the outskirts of the Outer, was a place for black market suppliers who would take anything, but credits, in payment. Nothing was ever reported out here and the Government never bothered coming out to clean it up. 
He dragged Rob up a small set of stairs to the first level gallery that gave them a view of the people below. Jim could see this was a big market, it would have started out at as an ordinary shopping strip, but over time it had completely taken over and blocked the street. At some point some a government official had the grand idea to build an awning that spanned across the street creating a covered market five city blocks long.
Jim knew where he was now, he’d never been to this market when he grew up in the Outer, but he knew all about it. This place had been notorious well before Jim was born, but more importantly he knew where it was in relation to their destination.
“What’s plan Jim? we can’t stay here” said Rob.
“We’ll go up and out, where about thirty minutes from the safe house on foot” he said pointing to more stairs that led up to the second gallery level.
They were just coming our of the stairwell, when Rob grabbed Jim and pushed him back into the shadows.
“Don’t move, they won’t see use here.” He pointed to three men pushing thier way through the crowd down on the main street. Unlike the rest of the public, these men were clearly not from the Outer. Not only was thier clothing out of place, but so were thier haircuts, thier square jaws and even the way they walked. People we starting to look at them.
“Those three are Nova Corp agents, the clean-up team, I know them well. The shit has really hit the fan if they are getting called out” Rob said as they peeked out of the shadows. “Get us out of here quickly”
We need to find a store up here that has a back door, most of them will be blocked but one of them up here will still be open. Let’s go and keep as close to the wall as possible.” Jim said.
They moved slowly creeping along the wall, peering into the shops as they made their way down the gallery. Rob kept an eye on the street below, the three agents were drawing more attention, clearly the crowd didn’t like outsiders.
Jim found a shop that had been abandoned and went inside to check for a rear door, as he expected it had been blocked off, but only with cheap boards that looked like they’d been there for years. He pushed at them and made a fist sized hole easily. This was thier way out.
Jim went to tell Rob he’d found a way out, Rob was standing still, looking down at the main street, Jim’s eyes followed. 
A confrontation was brewing down there, the Nova Agents in the middle of it. A group of local men had stopped them and were starting to ask questions, but neither Jim or Rob could hear what was being said from up on the second gallery level.
I was starting to get heated, the shouting and hadn gesture was escalating, snippets of words 
“They don’t take well to authority our here, locals probably thing they are CEB, which is bad luck for them” said Jim.
One of the agents who had been trying to calm the situation looked up at the same moment both Jim and Rob where looking down at him.
He immediately pointed to them, the words “There, that’s them!” made thier way clearly up to Jim and Rob.
The agents started to push through the crowd, who all of a sudden pushed back, a punch was thrown, then another, in a flash to mod turned violent raining down blows on the three men.
Jim knew where this was headed, he didn’t need to see any more, he grabbed Rob and they bolted through the shop to the back door, without stopping Jim used thier body weight to crash through the panels. They burst through into a back staircase which they flew down, the bottom of the staircase led to a back door which was not locked and the two of them ran out onto the street behind the markets. 
It was mostly empty of people and cars, anyone outside had gravitated towards the commotion inside. Jim slowed them to a brisk walk to not draw attention just in case, they crossed over the street and turned down the first corner they came too. Jim wanted tp put distance between them and the market quickly.

#
After a few blocks and random turn to see if they’d been followed out, Jim slowed the pace down. He actually couldn’t go any faster, he was pretty sure his ribs were broken, each deep breath was accompanied by sharp pain. He looked over to Rob who was faring slightly better. His nose had stopped bleeding, but the eyes we still half closed and looking decidedly worse than a few hours ago. 
The market was an informal signpost that defined the edge of the living city, as they walked further east towards to toxic quagmire of the miles wide Scar, more and more of the city was abandoned. The buildings were boarded up and left years ago, no one wanted to risk getting poisoned by the toxic fogs that were rumoured to blow in from the Scar. Jim had heard those stories all his life, but having been out there only a few days ago, he wasn’t so convinced those stories were completely true.
Ten minutes later, the streets and building were completely abandoned, apart from the sound of thier own footsteps, it was silent. Jim could relax slightly, he would be able to hear a car from blocks away out here and he didn’t think the three Nova agents would get out of the market any time soon. Now he just needed to find a familiar landmark that would lead him to the Grey safe house.
Rob spoke “You do know where you’re going don’t you?
“I do, just be patient, we’re getting really close.”
A foul smell wafted over them, it was a terrible concoction of rotten eggs and diesel fuel, both Jim and Rob grimaced and coughed at the smell. It passed withing a few seconds, but reminded them both they were a long way from the the city.
They rounded a corner and Jim saw it, the shop he hidden out in when he first escaped from the safe house. He walked over to it, the frantic escape of only a few days ago fresh in his mind, he pictured his path that, now the he was standing here, he could see it easily.
“We’re a block away, get ready, I have no idea how this is going to go” Jim Said
Rob nodded, he stood up straighter and shook his arms and stretch his neck to release some tension. He looked at Jim, the follower now gone, the corporate shark in his place like he’d just slipped on a familiar mack. 
He said with clear voice “I’m ready, remember leverage is the game here, only give them what you need too.”
With that, Jim lead them down the alleyway opened out onto the street where the safe house was. 
Except is wasnt, where the safe house used to be was a smouldering ruin, the central townhouse had been burnt completely to the ground. In fact the none of the townhouses in the row had been spared, they were all in various states of destruction, those closest to the safe house had suffered the same fate, the house a few doors down were still standing but there was nothing inside, most of the roofs had collapsed leaving smoking piles inside precariously leaning brick walls. Only the alleyways on each end of the row had stopped the flames.
Jim surveyed the scene with shock, he yelled to no one in particular “Fuck!”
“What’s going on? Was that where we were supposed to go?” he asked.
Jim didn’t answer straight away, he need to get his anger and disappointment in check.
“Jim, what the fuck is going on?” Rob pressed.
“Fuck it, this was where we were going, it’s a Grey safe house that I was in just a few days ago.” Did they get caught wondered Jim.
Rob shook his head as he looked at the scene of devastation “Jim, just when I think you’re getting the game, you disappoint me.”
Jim wasn’t really listening, he’d walked over the the ruin and was staring into the smoking ashed hoping to come up with some inspiration.
Rob followed him and continued “Didn’t you run away from here? It’s obvious they burnt the place in case you told someone about it, Jim you did this.”
Jim knew it was true.
“So, go any other ideas, it’s getting dark and I’d rather get out of stink if you don;t mind.” said Rob.
From behind them a townhouse door across the street opened silently, Brask and three others stepped out, each of the with gun drawn and pointed at Jim and Rob. With a practiced stealth they quickly closed the distance and snuck up behind the two men. 
Brask silently and gently placed the barrel of his pistol against Jim’s temple.
“Did you miss up Jim? Like what we’ve done to the place?” he said quietly.
Jim tried to turn around, but his arms were grabbed and forced behind his back, the pressure forcing him to his knees. He could see Rob too had a gun at his head and was on his knees held by two more men.
Jim went to speak, but all got was a smack across the face with the Gun Brask was holding “Now’s not the time for talking Jim, now’s the time for reckoning.” Brask was clearly enjoying this.
“Who’s you friend Jim?” Brask looked at Rob and his eyes widened in recognition.
“Rob Hughes, formerly of Nova Corp, currently MIA… well, well Jim haven’t you brought us a nice present. Mind you it’s not going to get you off the hook.”
A nondescript white van raced around the corner and screed to a halt next to them. the side door slammed open.
“Now if I had my way you’d have a bullet in the back of your head, but Vex wants to speak to you before I do that, so lets go one a little trip shall we?” said Brask as he nodded to the men inside the van who jumped out and put hoods over Jim and Rob’s heads. The were both bundled inside the van, the side door slammed shut and the van raced off. 
The street returned to is quiet decrepitude as the sound of the van faded.

CHAPTER 22

DATACENTRE (NEIL 8)

Neil didn’t have to get up as early today, he should have been thankful for the extra thirty minutes, except it just meant he waited in his apartment a bit longer after waking up in the early hours of the morning.
He’d been at the Nova Crop data centre for two days now, pretending to be a systems installer. At first Neil doubted his ability to play the part, but quickly found his previous experience in building was the prefect cover. He could read a plan instantly and know what to do straight away, he knew the words to say and had been on more that enough sites to blend right in. He was even considered somewhat of an expert by those technicians working with him.
The data centre was located just on the outskirts of the city centre, maybe twenty or so blocks from the shining mirrored glass edifice that was the Nova Corp office tower. It wasn’t a new building, in fact it was a plain looking five story office building tucked away in an area of the city was used mostly for secondary offices and storage by the many of large companies in the city. I wasn’t a busy area of the city, apart from the sporadic flow of delivery vehicles, the only people that worked there were staff who weren’t deemed important enough to be working in the fancy headquarters in the city. 
Nova has tried as hard as possible to make it looking like it was just a simple cosmetic remodelling taking place, instead of the complete gutting and rebuilding the interior of the build into a stated of the art data centre. 
Neil was sitting at the small table in his small lounger room, the TV playing but on mute for now. There were two hot coffees on the table in front of him. He checks the clock on the wall, right on time there was a light knock on the door.
Neil didn’t get up, the person on the other side had the key, the knock was just a courtesy. 
Knox opened the door quietly and walked, he saw the coffee’s and smiled that genuine smile that had tricked Neil more than a few times. “Glad to see you’re up and ready for the day” Knos said brightly.
“If you mean ready for another day where I don’t know what I’m looking for, then yes sure” replied Neil.
Knox sat and chuckled “I know, right now we just need you to get to know as much of the layout as possible, you need to find us the dark spots where we can get access into the system for a couple of minutes. 
“There’s plenty of places that aren’t fully commissioned yet and I can delay quite a lot of it actually, but I don’t know if I need to hide a person or a memory stick, some details will be good.” Neil said
Knox smiled and said “You’re just going to have to be patient, we’ve got some assets coming in right now that we’re hoping will be able to shed more light on what we need to get. Tomorrow, or the next day, we’ll have something to give you. More importantly, when do you think they’ll bring the servers online properly?”
“I’d say two or three days, the whole project is pretty close to completion. Speaking to a couple of the techs, they are running up parts of it now for testing, they tell me they start going live pretty soon.” Neil sipped his coffee trying to remember if there was anything else he could tell Knox that would shed light on the facility. There wasn’t be treading a fine line between outright asking questions and what seemed to be idle chit chat amongst the workers. 
Knox nodded as as he spoke he got up to leave “We need to nail down a timeline on that, it’s critical we get the job done as soon as they plug into the network and it’s fully online and can see what we’re doing. Keep probing.”
Knox was at the door and paused “Oh and I’ve arranged for a nice little surprise for you after work, make sure you come straight back here.”
“What surprise” Neil asked, Knox didn’t answer , instead quietly closed the door behind him.
Neil briefly wondered what it was, he figured it was probably some new tech device for him to work with, but with the Greys you never know what shitstorm they were going to throw at you. Neil was thinking about that when his alarm went off and he realised he’d have to hustle to catch his transport and the thought of the surprise was put aside so he could focus on the job at hand.
#
Neil’s day was busy, he had to pretend to be working when all he was really trying to do was peek into rooms, listen in to other people conversations and try to coax as much information out of this colleagues as he could. He’d learnt from many years practice that carrying a plan, or a tool, that most people would assume he was working on something and generally ignore him to concentrate on thier own work.
There was no supervisor breathing down his neck, like he had back at Stratum Projects, in fact Neil doubted any of the people knew he was a Level 3 ex-convict.
Walking the tightrope between spy and worked was tiring and Neil slumped into the seat of the AutoCab when his day ended. Just before he fell asleep he remembered Knox’s cryptic mention of a surprise, he couldn’t put a finger on why, but he wasn’t excited about it.
The trip had ended and the AutoCab chimed an alarm to wake him up, Neil was disorientated for a moment until he saw the drab exterior of the half-house entrance outside the AutoCab door. With a sigh he got our and walked into the lobby.
Grant wasn’t in his office, in fact since his last discussion with Grant Neil wasn;t sure he’d seen him at work. Had the Greys done something to him, or had he decided that keeping out if it meant staying away completely. Either way worked for Neil he though as pressed the button for the lift.
Normally the hall way on his floor was quiet, but this evening he could hear what sound like a child laughing. He approached his door and realised the laughter was coming from his apartment. Could it be? thought Neil.
 He didn’t hesitate and almost burst through the door.
Inside on the floor in front of the worn out two seater was Clara watching a tablet and talking and laughing in that way manner that most six year old do. Jen was sitting on the couch smiling at her.
She looked up to see what the noise, as soon as she saw Neil she jumped up and ran at him. The resulting hug almost knocked the wind out of Neil and he had to brace against the wall to prevent them from both falling over. Jen was holding on tight, both were crying. 
It took a minute or so before Neil looked up from the embrace, the feeling of his wife transporting him to a time and place he thought he’d lost forever. Clara was  watching Neil with some suspicion, to see his daughter look at him like that, it tore at his heart. 
“What are you doing here?” Neil finally asked, wipe tears from Jen’s eyes.
“It’s a bit of a story, but I don’t think we have much time.” 
Jen gestured to Clara “Come over here honey, remember Neil, he’s our very good friend, say hello.”
Clara walked over with a bit more confidence than she had in thier last meeting, she clearly remembered Neil form thier last reunion at the doctors office.
Neil knelt down “Hello Clara, it’s nice to see you again”
Instead of hiding behind Jen, Clara said, with the bluntness of youth and the manner of a teenager “Are you my Dad?”
The tears started again and Neil said “Yes”
Clara impulsively hugged Neil, sobbing at her simple and matter of fact recognition, he lifted her up in his arms, she didn’t resist. Jen hugged them both.
This was what Neil have dreamt of for all those years in Jail and those weeks and months exiled out here as a virtual slave, a pawn in some ones else sick game. The desire to wreak havoc on the system that could do this burned hotter than ever.
Their moment of peace and togetherness was broken as Rook and Knox came into the apartment without a knock or warning.
“What are you doing here?” he asked pointedly not wanted the moment with Jen and Clara to be broken, but it was too late
Knox always one to be calm answered “Neil, relax we took a huge risk setting this up, it was no mean feat to convince Jen who we were and to get her to come with us. Honestly, I think even Vex might have taken backwards step when Jen winds up”
Rook and Knox shared a chuckle at that.
Jen looked a bit embarrassed “I might have said some things” she said.
Rook chimed in “Yes and I’m still no convinced you won’t follow through”.
The tension in the room eased and Neil put Clara down.
“Ok, well this was a surprise is wasn’t expected, thank you, really.” Neil said in earnest.
“Neil, you’re a part of the team and we help each other out, no questions asked.”
Jen had kept hold of Neil, she wasn;t ready to let go yet, but she said “We’ve got to go Neil, it’s getting late, but Knox here says we’ll be back together full time very soon.”
“It’s only a few minutes, casn’t you stay?” Neil pleaded.
Knox interjected “It’s too risky, anyone could discover us all here, don;t worry, Jen’s right we re working on a more permanent solution.”
Neils’ head dropped, he hatred that he knew they were right. 
Jen picked up Clara and walked the short distance to the door “Say good bye to Daddy now honey” 
They all hugged, Clara in Jens arms, Neil enveloping the both of them.
Jen whispered into his ear. “Do what you need to do to take the bastards down”
“I will”he answered full of conviction that he would do just that.
Jen and Clara left with Rook, Knox was sitting a the table watching the farewell.
The door closed behind them and the apartment felt empty, washed out, devoid of colour now that they have left. Neil didn’t know what to next.
Knox spoke up snapping Neil back to the real world “Neil come and sit down, we’ve got to talk.”

#
Neil sat at the table with Knox, what next? he thought.
Knox started “I’m glad you liked the surprise, we though it would really help you to focus on what matters. We do this for our families, so not one needs to go through what we’ve all experience out here.”
Neil simply hadn’t pictured Knox, Vex or any of the Grey with family up until Knox mentioned it just them. He guessed that all must have family of one sort or another,though it was hard to picture with Vex. They probably all have the same kinds of stories he had.
“Are you taking them back home now?” He asked.
Knox paused for a split second, just enough for Neil to notice “No, actually we think they’ll be better off with us for now, we’ll take them to a safe place so they are not in danger.” 
“Danger from who? Neil said, the feeling of dread descended again, it was now a well worn feeling for Neil.
“Neil, what we’re doing is going to get some very dangerous people, extremely angry. these people with kill Jen and Clara, and the rest of us, without a second thought. If they go back home they are vulnerable, with us they are off grid and we can protect them.” said Knox with reasonableness that made Neil pause.
“Ok, sure, when can I see them again? You said a permanent solution?” He asked, but Neil had a sinking feeling he knew what the answer was going to be.
“At this point we don’t think it’s safe to get them back here until after the job is done, as for a permanent solution, nothing sold yet, but I’ll let you know as soon as we have one. Right now lets concentrate on the work at hand.” 
Knox smiled, but Neil was not buying it. He understood all to well what Knox meant, Jen and Clara were being held by the Greys to make sure Neil did the job properly, to keep him focussed.
“Ok, sounds good to me, do you have an update? Neil asked.
Again, the slight pause, Neil felt like he was able to start reading Knox’s tells “I do, we’re going to go ahead in 48 hours, unfortunately we just don’t have anyone available to help you, there are all tied up on another thing.” he said.
“What you mean, no one else?” 
“We’ve got another situation that has come up that is more… pressing, so you’re going to have to do the job by yourself.” said Knox.
Jim was stunned, he thought he was just scouting for a team of professionals, or at least someone like Rook or Brask to do the actual job.
Knox saw the hesitation and ploughed forward “Neil you’re the best person to do it, think about it. You know the place better than any of us, the other workers know, you to you, I know you can do it”
“Come on Knox, I’m no pro, I can’t do this on my own.” protested Neil.
“We don’t have a choice, think of your family” said Knox, the genial smile had gone missing, the point was being made directly.
They it was, the sting in the tail the Greys always had waiting for you. Did they ever have anyone else for this? or was it Neil the whole time he thought. Perhaps Jen didn’t just mean take down Nova or the Government, but Vex, Knox and the Greys too. He was inclined to agree with her.
“Ok, lets do it” he said instead.
Knox leaned back and nodded “Good Neil, I’m glad to hear it. You need to take tomorrow to tighten up your plan, make it fool proof, you only have one shot. Now it’s time for me to go. I won’t see you again until after the job is done.”
Neil didn’ t say good bye, he didn’t get up, he just watched Knox walk out. 
The only though going through his head was; Fuck you.







CHAPTER 23

INTEROGATION (JIM, ROB & VEX)

Jim had no idea where they were he was, the hood had remained firmly over his head from the time he and Rob had been bundled in to the van out by the burnt out shell of the Grey safe house. No one has spoken to him since then, not during the rough and bumpy van ride, which Jim thought might have taken an hour or so. No one has spoken to him when was dragged out of the van and tied to the chair he was sitting in now. He was sure exactly how long he’s been in the chair, he’d drifted off a few times, the darkness and cloying lack of oxygen under the hood making it had to stay awake.
A door to his rear screeched open and a pair of foot steps came in. His hood was yanked off his head, the sudden brightness of the room made his clamp his eyes shut and the room spun as he took a few deep breaths. The people who came into the room didn’t speak. 
Jim dared to open his eyes, just a crack, he saw Vex and Brask standing in front him. They waited until he’d opened his eyes fully and could see Brask holding a pistol loosely in his hands. 
Vex spoke “Jim, it looks like you been in the wars huh? Not so easy out there on the streets without any friends is it?”
Jim’s voice was hoarse from lack of water, and he croaked out “Better than staying with you.”
Vex looked shrewdly at Jim “So Jim if it was better than being safe with us, tell me why you’d come back?”
Jim was still blinking but he make out the room better now, it was a square box with raw concrete walls, floor and ceiling, it was lit by a single light fixture in the ceiling that probably wasn’t that bright, but to Jim was shining like a sun.
“I found out some things out there that I think you’d want to know and might help both us, plus I brought you a present as an apology.” Jim said, his voice coming back slightly.
“You mean Rob Hughes? The infamous Nova executive that they’ve totally burnt and are sending kill squads after? Fucking hell Jim, do we even know that whole story insn’t bullshit, that he’s isn’t just a plant to find us? Did you ever consider that?” Vex was getting agitated, Jim could see Brask grip the pistol a little tighter.
Actually the thought hadn’t occurred to him, thought Jim. Shit, what if it was all one elaborate game to get Jim to take him to the Greys? 
No, not with what they’d been through in that bunker with John, if they hadn’t fought back, Jim was sure they’d both be dead. That was real.
“No, it’s not a game, it’s as real as it gets, Rob saved me and I saved him.” said Jim with a firm conviction.
“You’ve cost us a lot Jim, time, people, assets that we’ve spent years building up, these things can’t just be replaced, you’d better have some seriously fucking good intel, or I’ll let Brask do what he really wants to do.” said Vex.
“Yeah sorry about that, I hope Corra is ok, but did you really need to burn the place down?” Jim asked.
“Yes we did Jim, if you’ve have been caught by the CEB you’d have sung like a bird, so yes it had to go…“ Vex paused her voice dropped “…as for Corra, she didn’t make it” she locked directly onto Jim’s eyes, almost daring him to say something else. Jim thought better of it.
Vex continued the stare well after Jim had looked away, then abruptly she turned to Brask and said “Untie him and get him something to drink.”
To Jim she simply said “To be continued, you’re not even close to being off the hook, you’re lucky I’ve got other fish to fry today.” and she promptly walk out of the room.
Brask flicked open a knife in the other hand, the pistol still drawn and menacing. He roughly cut the ties on Jim’s wrists and legs, deliberately nicking Jim each time with the knife. 
Jim protested “Hey, watch up, you’ve cut me.”
“It doesn’t matter, I’ll convince Vex to kill you soon enough” said Brask as he to left the room and slammed shut the steel door.
Jim was alone now, in another jail cell again, he had to get away from all these people. He checked his wrists to see how deep Brask had cut, but they were only superficial scratched, just enough to sting.
With no other choice, Jim lay down on the bare concrete floor and waited for others to decide his fate, he just hoped Rob had enough to give Vex that would save them both.

#
Rob was sitting in what felt like a school cafeteria, there were rows of long tables and chairs, one end of the room was a row of empty but still what seemed to be still serviceable Bain Maries, sadly empty though. Rob looked beyond them into the kitchen behind, again it was empty, but not abandoned, sure this wasn;t a safe house, it felt more like a temporary stop.
He didn’t know what happened to Jim, they’d separated the two of them as soon as they got out of the van. Honestly it didn’t matter to him, he’d already got everything he needed from Jim.
They had taken Rob directly here to this empty cafeteria, sat him down, untied his wrists and slipped the hood off, and then them left alone without a word.
Obviously they did think we was going to run as the room remained empty for what seem like a few hours. Rob had the chance to get up and walk around, he peeked through the doors, no one was standing outside guarding, the corridors outside were empty and quiet. He scouted some bottle water and helped himself. 
After a while he just sat down and waited, Rob knew the tactic very well, place the other person on your time schedule to show who controlled things. He’d used it countless times before; be as late as you could be to important meetings that other side couldn’t afford to walk away from. He was actually a quite amused to be on the other side for once.
Eventually, two people entered the room, a lean wiry women, dressed in dark fatigues, walking like she was the leader and a older man in his late forties or fifties Rob judged. Not the leader, maybe an advisor, not important, but not to be dismissed either.
The woman approached Rob and sat down at the table opposite him, the man held back a few seats.
She spoke “Hi Rob, how are you holding up? You look like you’ve been in the wars.”
“Despite how I look, I’m doing ok, thanks.” replied Rob reflecting the friendly tone.
“Good, we’ll get someone to check you over after this” she gestured to the man who nodded as if adding to a task list.
“Thanks, I’d appreciate it seems you know me, what do I call you?” Rob asked.
“You’re hard not to know Rob Hughes of Nova Corp, you’ve been splashed across the news for the last twenty-four hours. You’ve got yourself in to some serious trouble haven’t you?” she said.
“I guess I have been, but I’m just a casualty in a much bigger game. Oh and you didn’t give me your names, what can I call you?” pressed Rob, he knew they wanted to keep him at a disadvantage and he wasn’t letting them have it. He waited for her to answer the question.
“Of course, you can call me Vex” she answered.
“And your friend there?” Rob asked
The many chimed in “You can call me Knox” he said
Rob saw a flash of annoyance on Vex’s face that Knox had answered, she did well to mask it, but to Rob it was clear as day. Interesting thought Rob, Knox was perhaps more important than he’d initially thought.. He waited again.
After a short moment Vex spoke “So Rob do you mind telling me why you’re here?”
“Well you kidnapped me a gunpoint, so you’re why I’m here, it wasn’t my choice.”
There was that frustration again, good.
“No Rob, and I don’t think you’re in a position to start being smart. Why were you standing outside our safe house with Jim fucking Dalton, the man the whole city now knows you set-up.” Vex said.
Rob wanted her angry, and dug the needle in just a bit more. “You mean former safe house?” 
Before she could respond he leaned in and continued, his face carefully projecting a look of earnestness “Look, I‘ll save us both some time here dancing around what we need to talk about.”
Vex simply said “Go on.”
“I’m burnt at Nova, they won’t stop looking for me until I’m dead. They can’t have someone like me out in the wild with all the information I’ve a got about what they actually do and how they operate. I’ll be blunt, you need what I know otherwise you’ll forever be a small time operation…” he saw Vex bristle at that, but not Knox, he noted it and continued “…and quite frankly I need some friends who can keep me safe right about now. I’m proposing a swap, my safety for information that can bring down, not just Nova, but maybe the whole system.” Rob leaned back, giving here space to think about what he just said, he could she she was hooked.”
Vex looked Rob, and then back at Know who raised an eyebrow.
“Do we have a deal?” pressed Rob.
“If, as you say, you can help us bring down the system, them we’ll keep you safe. Let me make it very clear, if I think at any point you’re lying to me, I’ll give you back to Nova in a heartbeat.” She stood and left the cafeteria, Knox following behind her.
Rob put his hand behind his neck satisfied, the deal was done, but he needed to solidify it, and perhaps get Knox by himself to see what he could leverage there. 
He was safe for now at least and that was all he need today, there was time for the rest later on.
#
Rob was laying down on one of the long benches along side the cafeteria tables, he needed to rest his aching eyes, and he wanted to grab as much rest as he could to stay as sharp as possible.
He was going over the conversation with Vex in his mind, looking for hints of her motivation and angles he could push. The biggest reaction he got was when he called the Greys small time, so he knew it to be true. Vex had betrayed herself and her true feelings more than once in thier first meeting, and Rob was confident he could manage her. 
Still, she was dangerous and he had no doubt she’d meant the threat to kill him, he needed to stay ahead of that anger, and if needed focus it somewhere else.
Knox was a different story, a puzzle that Rob needed to work out, there was something between them Knox and Vex that was unspoken, and Knox was altogether too smooth for a group like the Greys.
He heard voices and footsteps coming towards the Cafeteria and sat up, as best he could he straightened himself up and put on his face, as he used to call it back at Nova, when he had to negotiate hard. This time he wasn’t going in blind, he knew the key players.
Vex pushed open the cafeteria doors and walked in with Knox and another man. Rob was curious now, the mood had shifted, this was not going to be an interrogation.
“This is Neil, he’s going to do a very important job for us tomorrow, and we’re going to need that information you promised now.” said Vex.
“Hi Neil, I’m Rob… newly minted friend of the Greys, what do you need to know” he asked.
Vex interrupted before Neil could answer “If you don’t mind, I’m asking the questions, right now this is a one way street.” 
Rob nodded in agreement “Sure thing, you’re the Boss.” 
The slight squint of the eyes told rob all he needed to know, she did not feel completely secure as the leader here. 
“Ok, here’s how it’s going to go, I’m going to ask the questions and then we’re going to tuck you away in a safe place whilst the job is done. If what you said is true and the job comes off, we’ll talk again. If not, well you can figure that out on your own. Understood?” She asked.
“I’m an open book Vex, let’s start I don’t want to hold Neil up.”
Neil didn’t react, he was waiting for Vex to continue. So Neil was just a soldier then, but he was valuable enough to be listening in, Rob filed that away for future reference.
Vex paused, as if she was standing on the edge of a pool deciding whether to jump in or not. Rob always watching for the slightest details saw the little nod to herself, more of a flick of the eyes than an actually nod, she’d decided to jump in.
“Were going to hack your new data centre tomorrow, what do you know about it?” she asked.
“Ok, straight to the point I see, let me think… it’s massively important to Nova actually, they’re going to run everything they do through it, but I don’t know much about the build out itself, it’s not my department.” said Rob.
“You need to give us more than that” said Vex.
Rob made a show of thinking for a few seconds “Ok, let’s see, I’m pulling from some old bits and pieces I’ve heard around the corridors if you will… what I understood at the time was that Nova intends to run it’s part of the Citizenship points calculation through it. The whole project was very hush hush, you know the information was compartmentalised, as few people as possible knew the real details. Just like you run things here, I mean I know there’s got to be more than four or five of you.” Rob said, smiling, testing.
No one took the bait this time.
“What do you mean it’s part?” Asked Knox interjecting.
“The government doesn’t run the points system themselves, they outsource it all to the private sector. Nova owns the final tabulation process, it brings in the data from all the various associated systems and registries and it creates your final score. It’s the end point, the Citizenship app is Nova’s.”
Neil whistled under his breath as both Vex and Knox took in the new information stony faced.
“Didn’t you know this? What were you looking to get from hacking the data center if you don’t know the first thing about the system? Fucking amateurs.” Rob was pushing them deliberately, he wanted to see who cracked.
Vex went still and spoke quietly “Rob, I’d shut the fuck up if I were you.” her eyes blazing with anger, Neil looked worried that he’d get caught up in her fury, but Knox  looked at Rob without reaction, did he already know this?
Vex got herself under control “Ok, that’s interesting, but not sure how it helps us? What we need is to find what they know about us and grab as much comms as we can whilst we’re in there, if we can find compromised executives we can work them to our advantage.”
Rob sat back and clicked his tongue, looking at them all with a sense of disgust, unfortunately these people really were amateurs Rob thought.
“You’re missing the whole point, the game is much bigger that just a few executives who play outside the rules, they are a dime a dozen and to companies like Nova totally expendable. Look at me, I was at the top, I had a seat at the table with the CEO, and they dropped me in an instant.” Rob said his agitation showing.
“Suppose you tell us what this game is then” said Vex sarcastically.
“Ok, I’ll spell out to you as simply as I can.” Rob paused, they were all waiting eagerly, this is where he wanted to end up.
“Corporations like Nova own the points system. Hell, not just the points system, they own the whole fucking system, it’s sure as hell not the Bi-Continental Government of the Americas, that just a veneer to keep everyone thinking they have a say.
“They call themselves Foundation Systems Companies, out of the ashes of old corporate America they survived and they built the whole system during the Reclamation. Each of them carving out thier own niches, the game is them fighting for power over each other.” Rob paused, he needed to land this story home when they were still hooked.
“Jim, Neil and countless other are just pawns their game, they don’t give a shit about any of you. I know because that was what I did, Jim was my project, he was always going to get burnt, none of them ever survive.”
“If you’re serious and you can hack the server before it’s fully locked down, you have access to everything, and I mean everything, you can change the world.” Rob was done, he could see he’d sold it.
The three of them, Vex, Knox and Neil, sat in stunned silence for almost a minute, until Neil spoke up “Ok, you tell me what I need to find in there to take these bastards down.
Rob smiled “I’ll be glad to.”

#
The meeting went on for another hour or so, the skylights dimmed as the sun went down outside and once brightly lit cafeteria darkened, shadows forming in all the corners, they weren’t turning on the lights Rob noted.
Rob had quickly become a vital element of the Greys plans to hack the data centre. He gave advice to Vex, Knox and Neil about what kinds of files to look for in the system, he gave advice on technical details of the Nova system, he even gave advice on how to use what they’d find if they could pull it off.
All the time he was drip feeding them information, he was skirting the fine line between looking like he was just a peripheral player with second hand knowledge and the fact that he knew every part of the system, what was in it and how to access every part of it.
Rob knew his value, and his life, depended on not only his information being correct, but also that they thought he had more to give. He was careful only to give them glimpses of what he knew, whilst dropping hints as to how much more he had.
The three of them had left Rob alone again when the meeting had drawn to it’s end. This time they didn’t leave Rob waiting, no sooner ha they left when a man, who Rob recognised as leading his kidnap off the streets came in.
“Follow me, I’ve come to put you on ice until the job is done.” the man said, it wasn’t a request.
Rob stood and followed him “I’m Rob, what do they call you?” he asked in a genial tone.
“You can call me shut the fuck up” snapped the man.
“Hey friend, I’m on your side, I remember you from the van that gave us a ride here” said rob, trying again to engage.
“I’m not your friend you corporate piece of shit, and if I had my way both of you’d still be face down in the dirt out there in the Scar” he answered.
Rob decided that silence was probably the best option from that point.
He was led though the dark and empty college or school, Rob couldn’t decide which it was, either way it looked to have been empty for a while. They descend down into a basement level where the lights were turned on at least, and the man stopped at nondescript door, on of many down on this level and opened it up without a word.
Rob understood what it was, this was his jail cell for the next few days, he went inside willingly confident he’d down enough to be leaving at alive later on.
He scanned the room, it must have been some sort of storage room, steel shelves lined the walls, they mostly empty now though. There was a small cot set up int he middle of the room, a few blankets neatly folded on one end. There was what looked liked a janitors sink and cleaning station against the back wall. Rob could handle this for a few day he thought and he turned to thank man only to have the door slammed shut in his face.
Rob had been dozing on the bed when there was a quiet knock on the door. He sat up immediately worried, this was far too quick for someone to be coming back. He though quickly, this couldn’t be a food delivery, as along with the blankets, they left a good amount and variety of food on the shelving, which meant he’d have to fend for himself for the next day or two. No, this was something else, it was a coin toss if it was good or bad.
The door unlocked and was pushed open, Rob was totally surprised to see Neil duck into the room and close the door behind him quietly. Before Rob said anything Neil held a single finger to his lips. 
“We’ve got to be really quiet, Brask is roaming the place.” said Neil.
“Brask, the guy who brough me down here?” asked Rob.
“Yep, that’s the one, he can be a nasty prick when he wants to, and he’s no fan of yours that for sure” Neil said as he sat at one end of the cot.
“I got that loud and clear” Rob dragged a blanket on to the floor and sat on it opposite Neil leaning his back up against the shelving.
“So, what’s going on Neil?” Rob asked genuinely curious.
“It’s just that this afternoon… you said some things… and I wanted to ask some questions. I mean I’m putting myself in danger tomorrow and I’m not going out there on just Vex’s say so if I can help it” Neil said.
Rob was impressed and had to immediately reassess Neil, it took some guts to sneak in here and fact check Vex in her own house. He waited for Neil, who eagerly filled the silence.
“You said these guys are amateurs, what did you mean?” Neil asked.
Interesting that Neil didn’t consider himself one of them, he could use that thought Rob.
“Neil, it’s simple; Vex is thinking far too small. Unless you all start seeing the bigger picture, you’re going to stay small time and end up risking your lives for absolutely nothing.” said Rob.
“Explain it to me so I can see the bigger picture, I’m not risking my life and my families live to achieve nothing.” Neil said.
“We’ll for a start when you hack the data centre tomorrow, you’ll have access to everything, but just for a brief moment.” Rob set the bait again with Neil, the bait Vex just didn’t, or couldn’t, see.
“You said the before, but tell me exactly what you mean.”
“When I said nova controls the points, that exactly it. When you’re in thier system you change chance the points status of anyone, to anything, and they won’t know you’ve done it.” he said, his hands emphasising the point. Come on Neil, follow me there, thought Rob.
“So what, I can make myself a Level 7 again? and my Wife too?” Neil asked grasping the gravity of what Rob was saying.
“Think bigger Neil.” He was enjoying this, he was about to split the Greys in two.
“Ok, Level 8 then for all of us?” 
“No, it doesn’t work like that, you’ll only have time to change one person, especially if you’re also going to grab the data Vex wants at the same time.” 
Rob stood up, the blanket wasn’t a good cushion on the cold concrete. He sat next to Neil on the cot and leaned in conspiratorially.
“When I say think bigger, I mean you can make yourself a God if you want to tomorrow, and no one will be able to stop you. There’s a Level above the system that no one, apart from maybe twenty or so people, knows about.” He paused, this was the most important piece of information that Rob had, he was taking a calculated risk that Neil was going to keep it to himself.
He continued “When the Citizenship system was set up by the corporations after the Reclamation, they created an invisible Level 10 that only the heads of those companies could hold. Neil, Level 10’s have access to everything, nothing is secret to them, they are above the system, hell they don’t even have points. They can basically do whatever they want, and I mean anything. They can’t be demoted out of Level 10, it’s with them for life, no one can take it away, not even other Level 10’s. 
Neil sat in silence, the news seemed almost to be too much to absorb. Rob let him sit an digest it before going in for the kill.
He put his hand on Neil’s shoulder and looked him directly in the eyes. “Neil, tomorrow, you can make yourself a Level 10 and no one, including Vex, can stop you. Think of what you can do, if you have access to everything. Think of how you can royally fuck this system from the inside, and I can show you how.”
Neil sat on the end of the cot silently for some time before he said.
“Show me how” said Neil with a conviction that caught Rob off guard, for a moment Rob worried that he’d just handed ultimate power to the wrong person. No he didn’t think so. 
Rob smiled, this was turning out to be a very productive day.










CHAPTER 24

NOVA HACK (NEIL 9)

Neil has a restless night in the old school, as much as his apartment in the half-way house was small and worn out, it was infinitely better than sleeping on a cot in an old office with Brask, and others, patrolling all night. He wasn’t sure if they were keeping him from running, or keeping him safe. 
He’d risked a lot by sneaking down to speak to Rob in the basement, but what Neil had learned had rocked his world. Everything he knew about the Citizenship system had been turned upside down in the last few hours. 
He had no doubt Rob was telling the truth, when he looked back now on all that had happened to him, he could never connect the dots, it never made sense why he was targeted. It was something had had kept him up at nights for more than five years.
Now that Rob had opened his eyes, he looked back and could see the invisible hands pushing and prodding at his life, like he and his family were some sort of disposable play things. 
Neil felt a sense of relief that the puzzle had been solved are all these years, but that didn’t cool the anger deep within him against those that caused his pain, in fact that anger flared brighter now that he had a real target for the first time.
Neil was sitting silent thing about what he had to do today when Brask, Vex and Knox came in.
“Are you ready?” asked Vex without even a hello.
Neil stood and said “Yep, nothing more I can do here, lets get on with it.”
Vex nodded to Brask who stepped forward and handed Neil a small tablet, it was no bigger than his outstretched hand, this was no commercial unit though. It looked to have been handmade, bits of black tape secured the screen to it’s backing and what looked like a storage unit was glued onto the back a wires from it punching into the back plate of the tablet. Brask handed over a set of short cables, each one with a different connection on one end.
“This is what you’re going to use to access the system, but remember it’s got to be plugged directly into one of the server controller units for it to work, the tablet will tell you if you’re plugged in to the right thing in about five seconds. If it says no, find a new server to plug into.” Vex said, looking to Neil to see if he’d understood.
Neil nodded and Vex continued “You don’t need to do anything once it gives you the ok, the screen will give you a timer on it’s job; it’s got to find, access and download the data we’re looking for. We think it will take five minutes or so, but it could be longer, we just don’t know.” Vex shrugged as if that was not a big deal.
Neil though it was a very big deal indeed, he was planning for a few minutes, not five or ten, or who knows how long.
Vex added “Neil, it’s got to stay connected once its starts, this is one time thing, if it gets stopped for any reason we don’t think it will be able to get back into the system.” 
She was done, she nodded to Knox, who stepped forward to Neil as she left the room.
“Neil, I know you can do this, you got the last job done, this is just the same, we all need this” He smiled and gave Neil a paternal pat on his shoulder as he followed Vex out.
Brask gestured for Neil to follow him and led him through the abandoned school and out a back door to the street where an autocab was waiting, it’s doors open ready to take him to work.
Just as Neil moved to get in, Brash stopped him putting a hand forcefully flat against his chest. “Don’t fuck this up Neil, I’ll be waiting with Jen and Clara until you get back” said Brask, a smirk on his face.
Neil said nothing and gave no outward reaction to Brask’s words. Once the doors closed and the autocab pulled away, the carefully constructed mask he’d worn faded away and the weight of the situation took hold. He’d do the job for them, and keep Jen and Clara safe, but he’d make damn sure he did something for himself too.

#
The autocab’s console showed fifteen minutes to the data centre, the abandoned school was lot closer to the data centre than his apartment in the Outer. He sat enveloped in the silence of the passenger compartment trying to keep his nerves tamped down as much as possible.
Far to quickly he’d arrived, the door slid open inviting Neil to leave, he took a moment and sucked in a deep breath. This was it, the time to actually take control he thought.
Neil stepped out onto the mostly empty street and made his way to the unremarkable building that housed the state of the art data centre, as he had done for the last three days and went inside.
The small foyer, like the outside, gave no indication what was going on inside and Neil flashed his ID badge at the guard who barely registered it before buzzing the heavy security door into the building proper. 
Neil, not wanting to change his routine when to the small staff break room and made himself a coffee, not just because that’s what he’d done every day so far, but also because it gave him a little more time to think.
He’d picked up his work allocations and put them onto a clipboard, today he was, as usual, allocated to trouble shooting faulty connections and cables across the building. He knew enough about reading an electrical plan that he could plausibly work out where to look and it didn’t take much to work a testing unit. It was the perfect cover as it allowed him scope to roam the building, ostensibly chasing down faults by in reality scouting for weak spots, almost by accident he’d even fixed a couple of faults.
He was sitting at one of the tables, the tablet under his jacket poking it’s sharp edges in his skin. A group of technicians walked in with exactly the same idea as Neil; start their shift with a coffee. They saw Neil sitting by himself and came over to join him.
“Morning Gents” said Neil sociably.
“Morning Neil” said the first man to sit down, the ID badge pinned to his chest reminded Neil this was Dominic, an installation tech.
In short order the other two men, Ronan and Travis, by thier badges, sat down.
“What have they got you doing today? ” asked Dominic.
“More of the same, tracing faults all over the place.” answered Neil.
“You’d think they take more care when running the cables would you, but no we all got to waste time trying to work out why one end doesn’t talk to the other” said Ronan, they all grumbled at that.
“Hey, any of you guys in the main server room today, I’ve got bunch of faults to trace in there? asked Neil.
“No we’re mostly done in there, we’ll be down in the terminal roos today running those up.” answered Travis who was the leader of the little group.
“Oh ok” said Neil.
“Don’t look so disappointed, did you want us to help you… like stand at the other end of the room and yell back if you’ve got a green light or not? Sorry not today Neil, you’ll have to rack up the step all by yourself. We’re only days away from going live.” said Travis.
“Not even if I give you a 5 credits per green light?” joked Neil.
The whole group laughed and shook thier heads “Not even for 50 credits would I trace lines my friend.” said Dominic.
Travis had finished his coffee and got up from the table.
 “Come on boys, let get to it” he turned back to Neil as they were all walking away. “Neil, just be careful up there, we’ve got about thirty percent of those units fully online, if you trace a fault to one of the come down and grab me.”
“Got it Travis.” said Neil.
He too had decided it was time to get to work, as he walked out of the staff room he breathed a sign of relief, not having that crew in the main server room meant his chances of pulling this off just got a lot better.

#
Neil didn’t want to wait around, so he headed straight for the server room on the second floor of the building. He had to swipe his ID card three times to get there, once in the lift just to give access to the second floor, and then two doors needed to be unlocked to allow him in the server hall. He hadn’t noticed before, but those door locked shut after him, if he alerted the system, he’d be locked inside.
The server hall was almost the full width and breadth of the building, whatever was there before had been completely stripped, including any walls or partitions, it was just one large room with only the support columns to hold up the building above breaking up the space. 
 The concrete ceiling,the bottom of the floor above, which was painted matt black and criss-crossed with air-conditioning ducting and water cooling pipes, was at least ten feet above the raised floor. 
The floor that Neil was standing on was raised about two feet off the concrete floor below and ran all the power and data cables into the server underfoot.
Neil took a seemingly causal stroll around the space, his clipboard in hand as if he was re-orienting himself to start his work. There were ten server rows in the hall, each row was a series half a dozen clusters of five of the six foot tall units joined together to share power and cooling services. At the end of each cluster there was a gap that allowed access to the rows in front and behind. Neil looked down through the gaps as he walked as he walked up and down each row.
The room was empty for now, so Neil stopped the middle of a row and pulled out the tablet. It was now or never.
He flipped the latches on the back of one of server units and opened the mesh door. The unit was humming with power, all the lights were blinking red, yellow and green across all the racks in the cabinet, Neil had no idea if this was one of the live units or not. 
He looked for the controller unit amongst the rows of drive units found an empty network port, his hands shaking and a sheen of sweat materialising on his brow he plugged the tablet in. He waited, a small script ran on the tablet screen for a few second then disappeared. The screen went blank for a moment, then a red frame around a word flashed.
[No access detected]
“Shit” muttered Neil.
He moved the cable to another port on the controlled unit, and plugged in it, same result.
[No access detected]
Ok, calm down, this cabinet is not the one, no problem thought Neil.
He unplugged the tablet and opened the back of next cabinet over.
With a bravado his didn’t feel he went straight for the network port on this new controller and plugged it in. He held his breath.
[No access detected]
“No!” Neil spat out to no one.
Ok think rationally, this unit is connected to the one next door, it just means they are the same status in each cluster, that will save time, he though.
The solution was go to a completely different row.
Neil crossed over two rows and went across a couple of clusters and repeated the process.
[No access detected]
Neil was starting to panic, this was going all wrong,
He ran to another cluster in a different row, and plugged in the tablet.
[No access detected]
Fuck, what was he doing wrong? The panic started to get hold of him, he could picture Brask sitting with Jen and Clara. 
Just above the hum of the servers, he heard the unmistakeably click of the security lock opening, someone was coming in.
Neil suddenly realised that he’d left the back doors of all the servers he’d tried open, he could see two of them from where he was standing. 
Fuck, how stupid could he be?
He was blown.

#
Neil quickly shut the door in front of him, and sprinted low and quiet to the end of the row to she who came in.
It was Travis, the installation team’s leader, this was bad; he was a senior manager and wouldn’t hesitate to blow the whistle on him.
He had to think fast, Travis would see the open doors and wonder what was going on any moment now. He looked around there was no where to hide, that wasn;t true there was one option and he hauled on the the floor panels ups to reveal the two foot deep crawl space underneath.
Neil dropped down into the space amongst the stacked runs of power and data cables, he flicked the tablet out of sight. Quickly he lay down on his back and pushed himself under the tiles so only his feet were showing, then he pulled out the small torch he carried with him and flicked it on.
Sure enough, less than a minute later Travis called out his name.
“Neil, are you in here?” Yelled Travis.
Neil yelled back “I’m down here.” the floor muffled most of the sound.
“Hello Neil, was that you?” Travis called out, his voice getting closer.
“I said, I’m down here” Neil yelled as loud as he could.
Travis turned the corner and saw the floor tile lifted up and Neil’s feet poking out from under. 
He bent down and called and spoke into the space “Neil, did you open all those cabinets?”
Neil made a show of sliding out from under the floor space and stood up in the open with a groan. “Sorry Travis, I couldn’t hear you under there.”
“I said did you open all those cabinets” said Travis.
Neil, wiping the very real sweat from his face and smearing some dust on his cheek at the same time “Yep, guilty, there was a small short somewhere in the and I though it might be the connection into one of the cabinets… but I found it under here, it was a screw just pinching the wire.”
Travis looked at Neil for a second considering the story and shook his head “Damn that fault has got you sweating, even five hundred credits isn’t going to get me crawling under these floors”.
Neil forced a laugh “I’ve give you all of them if could avoid going under there again, but I’ve still got to go in an patch it up now.”
“Ok, don’t let me stop you, I just came in to activate the whole first row here, so make sure you don’t touch them, they’re now online.”
“Got it, I should be done here soon anyway” said Neil as nonchalantly as he could.
Travis started to walk away and over his should he sais “Oh, and don’t forget to close up and latch all those doors thanks”
“Will do.”
Neil watched travis disappear around to the front of the first row, he couldn’t see what he was doing. Neil stood in the opening in the floor, the fear of being caught him to the spot, he was hoping with all his might Travis wasn’t going to come back.
