Whatever Travis needed to do only took a few minutes, without another word he walked out of the server hall. It was only when Neil heard the soft click of the door locking behind his did Neil relax. He slumped down onto the cold concrete floor and put his head in his hands as the adrenalin washed out of his system leaving him light headed and dizzy.
That was far too close, he can’t keep making mistakes like that.

#
He wasn’t sure how long he sat there on the hard concrete floor, hidden from view slumped down under the raised flooring in the server hall. long enough that the stars flashing in his eyes had subsided and his hands had stopped shaking. Did the device Vex handed to him even work? Right now Neil was doubting every part of the plan, he’d be lucky just to get out of this safely with nothing.
He was just wallowing in his defeat when an idea flashed like a lighting bolt into his head. He sat up straight.
He had been trying to avoid the live server units in case they set off alarms that they were being hacked, maybe it was only the live ones that would work? 
Travis had just told him which units were live now, Neil had no choice but to risk it.
Neil grabbed the tablet that he’d hastily slid out of sight, got up slowly and deliberately from the floor and climbed out of the opening. He carefully replaced the floor tile and walked to the first open server cabinet door. He closed it and latched it shut with what seemed to be an exaggerate care, then he walked the aisles of servers and closed and latched every other door he’d left open. 
He did want to be reminded of the stupid mistake he’d made, plus he needed a few more minutes to gather up the courage to plug the tablet into a live server.
He came to the final open door, the first one he’d opened, it’s dark grey mesh and black frame silently daring him to access the server inside.
He took a deep breath and plugged the tablet in and waited.
A green fame flashed with the words
[Access detected]
Then a few moment later a timer appeared
[Script started, Runtime 00:07:35]
Neil stood completely still, waiting for alarms to sound or security to come rushing into the room. Nothing, apart from the low hum of the servers and cooling fans, he checked the timer, he had seven minutes.
The plan was back on track, was he going to risk taking something for himself now? without really think his body decide for him as he ducked around to the front of the rack and pulled open the controller’s fold-out screen and keyboard, the kind he’d seen the techs use to access the system directly.
Rob had sketched out the backdoor script on a slip of paper the night before in his holding cell. It was a backdoor into the system that Rob had been working on it since he started at Nova Corp years before. 
Rob never had the right circumstance to test it himself, but told Neil if it worked, it was possibly the most valuable set of letters and number in existence. In theory it would grant anyone with the code access deep into the Citizenship Points system, all that down to the original program from the time when the system was set-up. 
However it could only be done directly from inside the system, and Rob never had access to the primary servers.
Neil stood in front of the console and couldn’t help thinking that he was a guinea pig here testing Rob’s theory, he wasn’t pass up the chance.
He typed the first line on the note
[#!/bin/bash]
He literally had not idea what that meant, but he pressed on and typed in the next lines.
[HIDDEN_SERVER_ENDPOINT=”http://legacy.grid.hidden:8080/setup_access]
[HIDDEN_USER=”legacy_admin’]
[HIDDEN_KEY=”L3G4CY_1901_150YR”]
He re-read what he’d typed in a checked it against the notes, it seemed correct so he pressed Enter.
Nothing happened, the cursor simply went to the next line and blinked on and off waiting for a new instruction.
[dialog --title “Setup Override Access” \ --msgbox “Enter Citizen ID for Level 10 elevation. This is permanent and untraceable.” \ 8 60]
Here heard a beep from around the back of the server, had something gone wrong with the tablet?
Neil sprinted around the back and checked it, the box around the text was still green.
[Script started, Runtime 00:04:23]
The beep sounded again, but it was coming from a small box mounted high up in the cabinet, there was a yellow light flashing.
Ok, just normal server things guessed Neil, he didn;t have time to think about it and rushed back to the console.
The was a small grey dialogue box that simply asked for the a citizenship number.
Rob typed in his thirteen digit citizenship number, every citizen had thier own number etched into thier memory almost from birth.
Another beep from the server.
The dialogue box then asked
[Confirm permanent upgrade Y/N}
Neil’s finger hovered, if this worked he’d be untouchable, he pressed the Y key.
[Upgrade confirmed] displayed in the box and the the box disappeared.
A script ran.
[echo “Upgrade complete. Disconnecting...” exit 0]
The screen went bank as if nothing had happened.
The beep came again and this time was starting to repeat more quickly, did he trigger that?
Neil closed folded down the screen and pushed the whole unit back into it’s slot in the rack. He rushed around to the back and checked on the Tablet.
[Script started, Runtime 00:01:21]
The light on the box was blinking even more, Neil stepped up on one of the storage racks in the bottom of the cabinet to look closer, there was a little blue lit screen at the top which read “Transmission detected, scanning, locating 73% complete”
Transmission detected, what does that mean, then he noticed the yellow warning light on the device was flashing in time with the the one one the tablet.’
Oddly the tablet still showed the same time on it.
[Script started, Runtime 00:01:21]
Had it stalled? He turned it over to make sure cable was still connected and the back cover came off in his hands. The heat from inside the cabinet had loosened the glue on the tape. The tablet opened up like a clam shell and what Neil saw in side chilled him to his bones. 
There were two long black antennas running up the each side of the handmade back cover,they were hard wired into the back of the tablet. There was not storage unit in sight, this was a transmitter.
The beeping stopped and a red light replaced the yellow on the device. A second later alarms came to life in the room, Neil’s ears hurt at the sudden shrieking sound.
They’d screwed him.

#
Without any hesitation Neil bolted for the door as the light in the server room turned red as if to back up the shrieking alarm. Instinctively Neil knew he had minutes or even second before the building went into a full lock-down.
He slammed into the first door using it as a brake, he didn’t have time to slow himself down. He frantically swiped his ID card, the lock clicked open. Neil, slowed down by knocking the wind out of himself on the first door, he hit the second door with slightly less force. Still the impact rattled the heavy door and took the wind of out him for a second time. He swiped the ID car, the light went green and the lock opened, he burst through it in the lift lobby. 
Wheezing and trying to catch his breath he saw the lift was already on its way up to his floor, he had seconds. 
He darted for the fire stairs he didn’t want the fire door to slam shut and echo around the looby, so he the remains of tablet he was still holding between the door and the frame to keep it open, just a crack. 
The doors to the lift opened, four armed security guards, weapons drawn rushed out and headed to the server room.
“Go, go, go” yelled the leader as they opened the first door and disappeared inside.
Once Neil had heard the door click locked behind them, he pulled the tablet away and allowed the fire door to close.
He jumped down the stairs his breath coming back to him, inside a minute he’d gone down the two floors to the ground level and stood at the exit to outside. This door shouldn’t have been locked down under fire regulations, but Nova didn’t seem to care about rules. 
Neil stood for a moment to try and place where there door would exit, by his reckoning it would exit on the side of the building, he should have a straight shot to the street out side.
He pushed open the locking bar on the door, it opened smoothly and Neil causally walked out, he knew not to run that would draw attention. No alarms on the street, but he didn’t know if he’d just set off alarms inside, he didn’t fancy and encounter with Nova security. 
Neil walked across to the other side of the street and as briskly as he could got to the next intersection and turn into and way from view from the building.
We he needed now was a faster way to get our of here.
“What I need is a fucking cab right about now” Neil said to the near empty street in frustration.
Moments late an autocab pulled up beside him and stopped, it’s doors opening as if inviting him in. Neil felt very conspicuous standing out on the footpath looking into the empty cab, so he got in.
The door smoothly slid shut and the console displayed a message.
“Welcome Prime Dalton. What is your destination?”
He’d expected the cab to recognise his face, he was only going to take it few blocks before he ditched it.
What was this Prime? Normally the console would say citizen Dalton and show his citizenship number to confirm who to take the credits from.
The autocab’s interfaces were quite dumb, but could anser basic questions, so Neil asked “What is a Prime?”
“You are, Prime Dalton, what is your destination? it answered back in it’s neutral mechanical voice. Neil had a theory.
“Do I have enough credits to get to the Outer?” he asked.
“You will not be charged any credits for this trip Prime Dalton, all public services are credit free a Prime Citizen” the autocab answered back as if it was the dumbest question it had ever heard.
Neil smiled, it had worked, he now had the power of the whole system in his hands.
He was going to pay some people a visit and it wasn;t going to be comfortable for them, but first he was going to make a couple of stops.














CHAPTER 25

ALONE (JIM 11)

Jim thought he’d been in the cell for two nights now, not that he was exactly locked in. Brask or Knox has come delivered food and water, they’d set-up a small cot and even let him out every few hours to use the bathroom down at the end of the corridor. It was hard to tell what time is was, his only reference was how much light was coming down the stairwell into the basement each time he passed it.
Neither of them had said much to Jim in thier visits, they both looked pre-occupied the whole time, like Jim was just an afterthought, or a even an annoyance. It felt like he wasn’t any use to them anymore. That actually worried Jim a hell of a lot as these were not the kind of people who just let you go.
Jim assumed it was morning on the third day when Vex came into the room. He was still saying in his cot snoozing, there wasn’t a lot of motivation to get up when you had no idea if it was morning or not, or even a reason to get up.
Vex flicked the overhead light on, Jim was for once pleased it was provided only a weak illumination and he wasn’t blinded by it.
Vex threw a new set of clothes onto the cot.
“Go have a shower Jim, you stink.” 
Jim remember he had actually been in the same clothes he had on when he left Rob’s apartment hide-out, he surreptitiously sniffed at his armpit, Vex was right. he stunk.
“Meet us upstairs in the cafeteria, we need to have a serious discussion.” said Vex as she walked out leaving the door open.
Jim got up from the cot cautiously expecting Brask, or some other Grey muscle, to walk in and escort him around the old school. Nobody did, and there was nobody outside in the corridor either. It’s like they didn’t care what he did anymore.
Not one to look a bit of freedom in the mouth, Jim sauntered down the corridor to the bathrooms. They were actually the old staff change rooms, with dozens of lockers and half a dozen shower booths with benches inside. They even left half full soap dispensers on the walls.
Jim picked a shower booth and laid out his new set of clothes, he chuckled when he saw it was an old staff uniform. 
He was going to take a long hot shower, if they didn’t care enough to guard him, he was going to take his damn time.
#
Jim has luxuriated in the long hot shower, he be sure but he though he’d been in there fro at least half an hour. No one came in to look for him, or at least not that he noticed, and the hot water never ran out. He left the shower feeling like a new man, except for the purple and yellow bruises on is side where John had punched him repeatedly.
He dressed and set about finding the cafeteria, luckily the old school had plenty of signs pointing the way, the gym, the library and most importantly the cafeteria. The school was silent a still dark with shafts of early morning light peaking through the shuttered windows and from under locked doors. It was a quiet and calmness that made Jim slow down even more to soak in.
Soon enough he saw the entrance to the school cafeteria, as he approached he heard voices. With no other sounds, not event the low drone of air conditioning, the hard concrete walls of the old school carried the voices further they they would normally have. 
Jim stopped just short of the doors to try and hear what was being said.
“… so we got everything we needed from the Datacentre?” Vex was talking.
“Yes, we got what we wanted and much more. Neil kept the unit sending data back to us for almost nine minutes before it was disconnected. I didn’t think it would take so long for the transmission to be detected.” Brask answered in his usual rough tone.
“Did we ever find out what happened to Neil? asked Vex.
Brask scoffed “Do we care?”
Someone else interjected “We care because he can still cause up damage with what he knows.” The voice was older and softer, Jim didn’t recognise it.
“We tracked a security alert at the data centre, it looked like a big spike in activity, but nothing seems to have leaked outside, ninety-nine percent chance they caught him.” said Brask.
“Ok, do we still have an asset in thier security?” Vex asked.
The older voice answered “Yes, he’ll do what needs to be done.”
“What about the wife and kid? ” asked Brask.
“Cut them loose, we don’t need them anymore” answered Vex
Jim had heard more than enough from these people. They were no rebels, they were most like the old mobster, just in it for themselves and fuck the rest of us, he was disgusted with them. 
He want to risk being caught listening in, he quietly back tracked down the hallway. He stopped, took a deep breath and as loud as he could he stomped towards the cafe. 
It did the trick, they were expecting him when he pushed through the doors of the cafeteria. Vex and an older man were sitting at one of the table, Brask was already standing and walked towards Jim, he pushed past and out of the room without a word.
“Jim, come sit” Vex ordered point to the bench opposite here and the man.
Jim did as directed, they might be preoccupied but that didn’t mean he things couldn’t end up badly here.
Without introducing the man sitting next her, Vex pushed a tablet over in front of him. “Watch” she instructed.
The tablet was playing a clip from the news, another nameless anchor standing in front of a video wall plastered with Jim’s picture. He could he see his ID picture, screenshots of his at the Apex restaurant, even a video loop of him pushing over the cameraman at his house.
The anchor spoke “Now for breaking news of former Level 8 executive Jim Dalton, who’s spectacular fall from grace has captivated the city for weeks now.”
Reading from the statement that flashed onto the screen, the anchor continued.
“The Citizenship Enforcement Bureau has announced that in a closed court session yesterday, they moved forward with Jim Dalton’s trial on the charges of seditious libel and seditious conspiracy. The Court tried Dalton in Absentia and found him guilty of those charges.”
The anchor walked over to a tall desk on the set where three other journalists where sitting “So is that the conclusion of this story, Jules?” She asked.
“I don’t think so, legally they are yet to sentence Dalton, don’t forget he’s also still a wanted fugitive.” answered Jules.
“What do you thing the sentence will be?
“Well it’s kind of an unprecedented conviction, sedition of any type is one of the most serious crime on our books. It wouldn’t surprise me one bit if the recieve a full life sentence.” said Jules.
Jim absorbed that fact, now there were no soft landing options any more, no convincing people of his innocence, just a jail cell for the rest of his life.
The video kept playing
“Very serious indeed, lets move over to you Amos what are your thoughts?” She asked one of the other panellists.
“Boy oh boy, Dalton is in a world of hurt when they catch up with him that’s for sure… but Wow! What a ride he’s had, racking up multiple theft and assault charges, on top of the sedition charges. Then the cherry on top, his videos accusing the CEO of one of the biggest and most successful companies in the whole of the Bi-Con Americas of some sort of wild conspiracy to take Dalton down. I’m note sure we’ll ever see a ride or die like this again, am I for one and all for it!” said Amos, clearly the shock jock on the panel.
“Ronan, what are your thoughts about the protest outside the Nova Corp tower since Dalton’s last video? acceptable or not?” She asked the final panellist.
“Unacceptable, these were baseless accusations against on of our most important business leader, the Police and the CEB need to shut those protests down now.” said Ronan, clearly the foil to Amos on the panel.
“Indeed, well like it or not, Dalton is the hot topic right now as people choose thier sides….
Jim pushed the tablet away, he couldn’t stomach any more of the slick TV voices discussing him like he was some sort of soap opera character.
“So I’m fucked, but I knew that already.” Siad Jim caustically.
“Jim yes, you are, and quite frankly, you’re to hot for us to handle. With the conviction, every member of the Police and the CEB, and quite frankly most of the population will be on the look out for you, and w’ere not going to risk ourselves hiding you.” said Vex 
Are they really cutting me loose? thought Jim.
“So, what now?” asked Jim.
“This is where we part ways Jim, but know this if you get caught out there, we’ll be keeping a close eye on your parents. One sniff of you talking and, well I’ll let Brask loose.” Threatened Vex.
Jim waved her off, he’d had just about enough threats from these people. He was past feeling afraid of them now, they could threaten him all they wanted, he just didn’t care. He wasn’t exactly numb to it, but his anger and fear had transformed into something else, he wasn’t sure what it was yet.
Vex got up and walked out of the room, no good byes or good luck, just complete cold rejection.
Jim put his head in his hands and closed his eyes, he’d manged to lose every single ally he had.
#
Jim looked up a few moments later startled to see the man still sitting there quietly looking at him. 
He smiled, it was a genuine warm smile, he waiting for Jim’s shock to subside and spoke “Hi Jim, we haven’t met, but I though that before we all head our own separate ways, I wanted to have a quick chat with you.” He said congenially.
More threats? thought Jim.
“Vex made it pretty clear I need to keep quiet, but there was not need to get my family involved.” Spat out Jim.
The man shook his head and said “Jim you misunderstand me, I’m not here to make treats, Vex is more than capable of that. I’m here because I think I have a opportunity for you.”
“For starters, I don’t talk to people who name’s I don’t know, and why the fuck would I care what you people want anymore?” Jim made a point not hide his disgust.
“You can call me Knox, I’m not here to tell you to do anything, I think we both know where you stand. I’m just here to show you a possibility, you can take it or leave it” Knox said.
He really had no plan, so Jim thought he might as well listen.
“Ok, dazzle me with your plan then” he said sarcastically.
Knox pulled out a phone and placed it on the table in between them.
“This is totally anonymous phone, we’ve cracked it’s software and installed phantom routine app that means it’s virtually impossible to trace, even to us.” said Knox
“Ok, a phone, thanks”
“The phone is not the proposal. It what you can do with it. Jim, I don’t think you realise what’s happening out there. Your videos have stirred something within the people of the city, a deep sense of unhappiness at the system.” Knox said earnestly.
“Hey you won’t see me disagreeing, the system is rigged, but what can I do about it?” asked Jim.
Knox looked up behind Jim and waved someone over, Jim turned, it was Rob.
Rob nodded to Jim and sat next to Knox, it wasn’t lost on Jim that he and Knox were sitting together with Jim alone on the other side.
“I was just explaining to Jim what’s going on out there.” Knox said to Rob.
“Jim, your videos, and now your conviction have got people openly talking about the system and how unfair it is.” Rob said as he took over the pitch from Knox.
“Get to the point, I don’t want to be here any longer than I need to” said Jim.
“Jim, you know how I keep saying this isn’t about you, that much bigger than you? Rob asked.
“You, Nova, this lot here” he waved at Knox “have made it clear I’m pretty much insignificant” said Jim.
“Well I was wrong, right now you and you alone are very important. You’re the name that has got everyone asking questions. Your last video outing me and and Nova broke open the floodgates of dissent. There’s protests breaking out in the Grey right now, and the rust belt is rumbling.” Rob said.
“Right now they are unorganised and don’t have leader, the Greys can’t get in front of it, they’ll be blown and they’ve got other fish to fry right now. Jim, if you go out there and keep dropping videos, give these protesters a purpose, a leader, then you might just kick of something no one can stop. That’s how you get back at the bastards.” 
Jim sat back and blew out a big breath, as he tried to get his head around what Rob had just said. The more he thought about it the more it made sense. He knew how much damage his last video did to those faceless corporations and the self appointed gods who ran them. Rob sitting across the table, with two black eyes, was testament to that fact.
Jim didn’t feel like giving up the fight today.
“So, let me get this straight, instead of disappearing into the shadows, you want me to be even more public?” Jim asked not quite believing it that was the deal.
“Yes Jim, only you can do it, the people seem to think you’re one of them now, they are desperate for a direction, for leadership, your leadership” said Rob.
“What about you?” Jim asked.
“I’m going to stay with these guys for a bit, we’ve got some big things to work on together.” Rob answered.
Jim picked up the phone was turning it around in his hands contemplating the choice; go down in blaze of glory sewing as much chaos as he could, or slink away into the shadows like coward.
Jim was no coward.
Just as he was going to give them his answer Brask burst into the cafeteria at a full run. 
“We’ve got to go now” he said to Knox and Rob, ignoring Jim altogether.
“What’s going on?” said Knox, his nice guy demeanour almost instantly disappearing.
“There’s been complications with the data centre job, looks like Neil got away, but he managed to take sort sort of backdoor access with him. We don’t know what, but the tablets has been used to trace our location and has pinged everyone of our ID’s. We’ve got to get out here and go off grid right now!” Brask was almost yelling the last sentence, his panic showing.
Knox and Rob got up a ran out of the Cafeteria behind Brask as the all exiting without a backwards glance at Jim.
For the second time today he’d been left alone, but this time he knew they weren’t coming back, he was truly on his own. He stood, still holding the phone Knox had given him, he looked around the empty room.
He made his decision, he put the phone in his pocket and said to no one “Fuck them all.”

#
Jim got away from the school and had just ducked down when he looked back to the school as a dozen or so black clad agents stormed into it. They didn’t look like Police or even the CEB, he assumed they were some sort of private mercenary group, well funded and armed to the teeth. He didn’t think there were going in to take prisoners.
Thankfully the old staff uniform they’d given him had included a hoodie, Jim walked away briskly with the hood pulled down low over his face, it would stop most people recognising him.
He needed to get out of the city, so he jumped on the first bus, pretending to swipe on with his phone. He’d done it hundreds of times before when he was growing up in the Outer, the other passengers didn’t even look up.
Sitting on the bus he thought deeply about what Rob had said, he was going to sew as much chaos as he could, but he might as well do it in a way that was as hard to stop as possible, and that mean playing the part of rebel leader, he play that role until they stopped him.
For the next few hours Jim jumped bus after bus, each trip taking him further away from the city centre, but never taking the straight line, always careful to double back and check the no one was tracking him.
He got to his destination, one of the major bus hubs in the Outer. It was the most dangerous part of his journey, the hub was brightly lit, bustling full of people coming and going from various parts of the city, and worst of full of cameras.
He kept his head down and jumped one of the buses that took him over the miles long causeway bridge that spanned the Scar and connected the city to the factories of the Rust Belt.
Jim had been out the once or twice in his life, but he knew enough about the are to know, virtually not one lived out there and the Police and the CEB rarely, if ever, crossed the Scar bridge. It was his only option of getting out of the city, but staying in civilisation.
It was dark when the bus dropped him at the terminal in the Rust Belt, only a few night shift workers were milling around waiting for thier feeder busses to thier factories. Jim decided he just walk and stop when he found somewhere to hide.
It didn’t take long, whilst a lot of the Rust Belt factories were brand new shiny corporate behemoths pumping out all the product the city needed, there were plenty of buildings that had been abandoned for one reason of the other.
Jim found a smaller factory with a small attached office building that looked like it hadn’t been used for many years. The once manicured grass an the entrance had turned into foot tall weeds, where the company name used to be, was simply a less faded patch of the wall.
Jim ducked through a hole in the fence and walked up the front door, it was chained shut. Jim stumbled around to the side of the building into dark night time shadows and found a side door that was starting to rot. With a few kicks directed at the weak spot, he manager to open a hole that he could crawl through.
The warehouse inside was pitch dark and mercifully completely empty, Jim knew he wasn’t far from the offices and managed to fell his was to them. Once inside he went up to the second floor and chose and office at random to make his bed for the night. The floor would do for now, he’d scout for supplies tomorrow when the sun would stream in and up the whole place.
Before he allowed himself the luxury of sleep, he had some things to say. He pulled out the phone Knox had given and turned it on, luckily they’d set it up just for his needs, as if they knew he’d take the bait. 
He flicked open the streaming app and turned the camera on himself, the small phone light illuminating him against a dark background. 
He pressed the live button and started speaking.
“I’m Jim Dalton and I’ve got a story for you about corporate greed and corruption that you won’t want to miss…”











CHAPTER 26

SHANE AND THE TABLE (SHANE 2)

Citizenship Act: revised 2045 (Classified – no access below Level 10)
Section 4, Subsection 3 – Citizenship Status
11.1 Citizenship Status Level 10 – No points accrued.
11.2 Such status will be appointed to the heads of each of the five (5) Foundational systems Corporations (FSC) as outlined in the Citizenship Act 2035 – Appendix 23.
11.3 The Heads of the FSC’s have at at thier direction the ability to appoint, and remove, any other person to Level 10.
11.4 The Heads of the FSC’s Level 10 status can not be revoked, except by an extraordinary meeting of the FSC Board.
11.5 Such status will allow full and unrestricted access to all data and status systems controlled by their FSC.

Shane arrived deliberately late to the meeting, he wanted the others to wait for him, after all they’d called the meeting, not him. It didn’t happen often that all of the heads of the Foundational Systems Companies, as they liked to call themselves, were all together in one place for an official meeting. Of course any one of them could technically call a special meeting such as this, they simply didn’t need to, besides they all universally despised each other and were normally content on plotting each others downfall from the safety of thier own corporate empires.
The venue was the Club House, a sort of neutral ground that had been built by the six founding corporations during the reclamation when they met almost daily to set up the Citizenship system. Now it was only occasionally used by one or other of the company heads and almost never at the same time, as technically there were only six members.
That didn’t stop the Club from being immaculately maintained to the highest possible standards at all times. Staff and workers were selected only from the six foundational companies and even then were thoroughly vetted fro loyalty and discretion. It was often a path to promotions and elite status back in the city to be seconded to the club for a six month posting.
There had been quite debate over what architectural style to build the Club, two of them had wanted a call back to styles from old America, the white neoclassical style of the old White House or the old Capitol building, both of which had been wiped out in the Last War. 
Shane for his part wanted to dispense with the failures of history and had won the argument to build an ultra modern building, made from new glass and steel made out in the, at the time, newly recommissioned factories of the Rust Belt.
The drive was as pleasant as ever, the Club was about an hour north from Shane’s mansion in the hill, which already at the northern tip of the city. Shane’s car drove itself effortlessly, weaving it’s way deeper into the forest, where no one ever went, purposely, and surprisingly smoothly for such a big heavy car.
Shane sat in the car alone, well accustomed to the opulence of the interior he did even notice it. He was silently fuming at having been summoned by the so-called Board of Primes, as they liked to call themselves. Even the thought of the title Prime, disgusted Shane what arrogance this small group of people had, they better off all gone with Shane left to run the place.
He knew why he’d been called in; Jim Dalton, he chuckled at the thought that he‘d lost control of Dalton. No, whilst Jim had wrong footed Shane time and again, eventually he’d turned into exactly what Shane wanted, a lighting rod to stir dissent and weaken the system. 
A weak system that Shane could exploit and ultimately take over.
Shane’s car finally slid into the grounds of the Club, the sleek low single level building, its grey steel frames and reflective glass walls, framed by exotic vertical wooden battens, looked like it was floating over the manicured grounds.
The car effortlessly rolled into the underground garage and parked in Shane’s spot. He opened the door and got out, no staff were here today to attend to Shane, they’d all vacated from the site completely. He could see four other similar cars parked neatly in thier designated spots, they all varied slightly in colour and style, except for the last one. It’s gaudy bright yellow almost seem designed to annoy Shane.
Lucien De Montfort, head of Pinnacle Analytics, and Nova’s chief rival in the data space, insisted on driving his assortment of vintage petrol cars around the northern Hills. He did it “in the interests of keeping these magnificent engineering pieces of art alive”. Today the bright yellow Ferrari made Shane dislike him even more.
Shane allowed the thought to dissipate and cleared his head of any annoyance or anger. He was about to enter a room full or apex predators, any weakness in emotion or mood would found and exploited, not that Shane went in unarmed, he knew he was equally as dangerous.
Shane straightened his immaculate dark grey suit and walked upstairs to the Club ready for battle.
#
The main room of the Club was exactly as one might expect of an and exclusive venue for the ultra rich and powerful. The tall sweep of the flat ceiling was held up by the exposes structural grey steel beams. The spaces in between clad with huge panels of exotic woods from the rainforests of the southern continent. On one of the room the twenty foot tall glass wall gave unappareled view across the valley and into the deep forest, the smart glass able to block the view in an instant.
Opulent, irreplaceable artwork graced the walls, side table held equally expensive vases and sculptures, even the couches dotted around the room were upholstered in the finest fabrics.
Shane strode purposely into the room and saw all the other Primes casually chatting with each other around he vintage side board that had been pile with foods of all kinds.
They turned as one when he entered.
Lucien took it upon himself to welcome Shane on behalf of the others.
“Shane, my friend, so good of you to grace up with your presence, I hope the trip out here wasn’t too onerous” he said effusively.
Shane a smile planted firmly on his face took Lucien’s outstretched hand and shook it with all the warmth he could muster “Lucien! It’s been far too long since we lunched together, so good to see you. I saw you dragged another one of your old relics up here” Shane said still smiling.
Lucien feigned hurt “Mon ami, you hurt my feelings, my cars are as irreplaceable as the artwork that hangs on these walls, and much more fun.” 
The affected old school Québécois accent and smattering of French just annoyed Shane even more, he knew they both grew up in this city, although Shane didn’t have the fancy family name nor had he inherited his position.
The others in the group wandered over to shake his hand and greet him with hugs or handshakes, god he hated these people.
Sofia, the head of Ruiz AgriSystems the largest supplier food in the Bi-Con, spoke up with her soft, almost musical, Argentinian lift that had lured many a competitor to thier demise.
“Everyone, please lets get started now that were are all here” she gestured towards the sleek dark marble hexagonal table that sat under a matching cut-out high in the ceiling. 
When they were all seated Sofia spoke again “As the appointed Chair of this group, I’ve called this meeting to address concerning reports coming out of the Capital of growing civil unrest. Would the either of the gentleman who run the city care to expand?” she looked at Shane and Lucien.
Shane took a breath and started to speak, he had neat little story worked out for this meeting, but Lucien started a second before him.
“I agree Sofia, it is rather concerning, we’re tracking quite a spike in unsociable behaviour, the resulting points fines don’t seem to be having the same effect as we’d normally expect.” Lucien said informatively to the group.
Shane sat, it was a simple trick by Lucien to speak first and lay down a his narrative, Shane couldn’t let the group know he was annoyed or frustrated, let him have his say, Shane could counter it.
Lucien continued “ça me fait mal, I don’t like seeing our city so, I’d certainly like to know why there has been such a spike?” he looked at Shane who simply returned his gaze as nonplussed as he could.
There he goes with that french conceit again, as if everyone in the room didn’t speak at least five or more languages each, thought Shane. He sat silently as if in deep contemplation, making sure he was giving due deference to the question they had summoned him here to ask.
When he spoke it was with authority “I can understand those of you in your cities away from the Capital might get the wrong idea from reports, but as the man on the ground controlling things here” He paused for effect, he didn’t need to look at Lucien to know that barb had struck home, he continued “I have the city completely in control, nothing needs to concern any one here.”
A different voice spoke up, Kael Voss the head of Horizon Ventures who’s vast empire spanned old Brazil plundering he rainforest for mineral resources, asked simply in his clipped and very formal english heavily accented by his native Portuguese tongue “If the Capital is under control, as you claim, then why would we have such a spike in anti-social activity?”He asked.
Lucien followed up quickly with his own question, as if he’d been prepared for Kael’s question “Perhaps if you be so kind, my dear friend Shane to enlighten us all on all the special projects that you’ve been running around the Capital, one can’t help but think they have some bearing on the current situation?”
Lucien was playing a big card now, he was telling Shane he thought he knew exactly what he was doing, but he’d anticipated in on the drive over.
“Lucien, you’re getting a bit excited with rumours and innuendos my old friend, we all run projects from time to time to root out dissent. That’s all that is going on, we’ve set-up a series of events simply to draw out those more prone anti social behaviour to flag and track them better. This will all calm down as soon as we end the projects.” Shane smiled at the group expansively.
“What about project 29” Lucien asked innocently.
Shit, how does he know Dalton’s project number? Thought Shane, immediately realising he had a major leak. It must be Rob Hughes, damn it, he was going to have to clean that up quickly.
“Oh you mean Dalton, that was just a simple project to uncover a small, but I do admit annoying, group of want-to-be rebels. Not to worry, it’s all bee cleaned up already.” Shane said dismissing the matter as something trivial.
Sofia had been sitting back and observing the interaction between Shane and Lucien, spoke up. “Shane, Lucien whilst it’s amusing for us all the what your schoolboy jabs. we do really need to deal with the matter.” Looking at Shane she asked “What do you intend to do to resolve it?”
Kael interjected “ I would hope you punish all those caught up in rebellious activity harshly.”
Shane smiled “No Kael, the opposite in fact, of course the handful of hard core cases we’ll send over to you die in the mines, but I’m proposing to give a general amnesty for minor offences. We will build more loyalty to the system if the people think it has some measure of compassion.” he said reasonably.
Shane knew full well the genie was out of the bottle, the civil unrest would grow and he’d be happy to inflame it, but he had some moves he needed to make before he could light the fire for real.
“We’ll, I can speak for us all Shane that we expect you and Nova to get this under control and quickly. Otherwise the Board may have to review the allocation of your contracts to the system.” She smiled.
There it was, the sledgehammer wrapped in the smooth song like voice, Shane knew what she meant and it was not an idle threat. Even the sniff of losing contracts to run the system to the others in this room would trigger a coup inside Nova and Shane would be cast aside. There was no peaceful retirement options for Primes.
Shane smiled sweetly to Sofia and nodded to the rest of the board to acknowledge the reprimand with due deference. 
Underneath his outward air of calm and professionalism, he was seething, none of these so-called Primes had any right to be running the system. He got up from the table and as he said his goodbyes one thought was solidifying in his mind.
You’ll never have the chance to take me down.
You people have no idea what’s coming.



THE END!!!!!
